If `config.LoadLayered`/`Check` become generic over the root type later, the feeder can switch; please then also generate a feeder section in docs/CONFIG.md. Nothing is blocked.

## WP-W-web -> internal/server (WP-H): routes and DTOs the network UI calls
The web UI (`web/static/js/net/endpoints.js` lists every path with its reply shape; `web/dev/mock-network.mjs` is a working reference implementation with fixture data) expects these routes. Every field is optional on the UI side (missing = dash), but names should match:
- **Public (no session):** `GET /api/public/summary` -> `{name, stationCounts:{total,online,public}, aircraft, mlatAircraft, messagesPerSec, mlat:{p50M,p95M,p99M,fixesPerMin}|null, updated}`; `GET /api/public/stations` -> `{stations:[{name? (only when showName), online, pin:{lat,lon,precision}|null (stored public point only), stats?:{msgRate,uptimePct,aircraftDay,positions,mlatContrib} (only when publicStats)}]}` - opt-in entries only, no `sid` of hidden stations.
- **Feed Check (public, IP match):** `GET /api/me/check` -> `{links:[{proto:"avf"|"beast"|"mlatc", idTail ("â€¦" + last 6 of UUID/feeder ID), uuidMissing, uuidMismatch, since (ms), msgRate, posRate, kbps, rttMs, clockClass (wire name), mlatSynced, sid?, stationName?, claimed, yours (claimed by the viewer), claimable, admission}]}`. Must answer 200 with an empty list for public visitors without a matching link (not 401).
- **Owner:** `GET /api/me/stations` -> `{stations:[OwnerStation]}` with OwnerStation `{sid,name,kind,admission,online,lastSeen,clockClass,lat,lon,alt,locStatus,locSource,sigmaM,pin,publicStats,showName,publicPoint:{lat,lon,precision}|null,stats:{msgRate,posRate,aircraft,uptimePct,aircraftDay,onlyMe24h},antennas?}`; `GET /api/me/stations/{sid}` -> OwnerStation + `connection{proto,version,since,rttP50Ms,rttP95Ms,kbps,fpsByDF:{df:rate},lateFrames,spoolBytes,hostOffsetMs,timeline:[{from,to,online}],links:[{proto,since,ip,msgRate}]}`, `timing{clockClass,anchor:{state,reason},sigmaThetaNs,driftPpm,betaShortNs,residualBiasNs,outlierPct,events:[{t,reason,detail}],peers:[{tag,band:"near"|"mid"|"far",syncRate,rmsNs,ppm}]}`, `mlat{contribPerHour,usedPct,excludedPct,residualBiasNs,outlierPct}`, `location{status,autoUsed,effective{lat,lon,alt,source,sigmaM},declared{lat,lon,alt,altRef}|null,gnss|null,estimates:[{id,method:"survey"|"locateme"|"gnss",t,lat,lon,alt,sigmaM,ellipse:{a,b,angle}}]}`; `PUT .../privacy {pin,publicStats,showName}`, `PUT .../name {name}`, `PUT .../location {lat,lon,alt,altRef}`, `POST .../location/confirm {estimate}`, `POST .../unclaim`; `POST /api/me/claim {code}|{uuid}` -> `{sid,name}` (429 + Retry-After); `GET /api/me/export` (zip); `DELETE /api/me/account`.
- **Admin:** `GET /api/admin/stations?q=&admission=&kind=&online=&limit=` -> `{stations:[OwnerStation + owner (user name), feederId, msgRate], total}` (exact lat/lon here feeds the map's Admin view; audit as you see fit); `POST /api/admin/stations/{sid}/admission {admission}`; `PUT /api/admin/stations/{sid} {name}`; `POST /api/admin/stations/{sid}/reveal` -> `{lat,lon,alt,source,sigmaM,ip}` (audited `station.reveal`); `DELETE /api/admin/stations/{sid}`; `POST /api/admin/feeders/{feederId}/revoke`; `GET|POST /api/admin/tokens` (`POST {label,maxUses,days}` -> token incl. secret `token`, shown once); `DELETE /api/admin/tokens/{id}`; `GET /api/admin/audit?limit=&before=` -> `{entries:[{t,actor,action,target,detail}]}`; `POST /api/admin/retention`.
- **station= parameter:** the UI appends `station=<sid>` to every station-scoped legacy `/api/*` GET (stats, antenna, rf, timing, guess, aircraft, reports, weather, trails, events, aircraft) and to the SSE URL when an owner/admin has picked a station (never on /api/public|me|admin|auth, /api/session|login|logout|config|status). Please resolve it with the owner/admin check and serve that station's namespace (`ant='s<idx>'`); per-viewer geometry (`distKm`, `bearing`, `elevation`) relative to that station.
- **Public view:** the UI only requests `/api/session`, `/api/status`, `/api/aircraft[/{icao}]`, `/api/events`, `/api/trails`, `/api/public/*`, `/api/me/check`, `/api/auth/setup*`, `/api/auth/register`, `/api/login`, route/photo/trace/prediction lookups (`net/model.js` `PUBLIC_ALLOWED`). Please make sure `/api/status` for public viewers carries no `station`, `feeds`, `located`, `ntp` details and `counts.maxRangeKm`, and the SSE `status` event is projected the same way. `web/dev/privacy-crawl.mjs <base-url>` checks these payloads.

## WP-HL-headless -> cmd/airview (WP-E): no Raspberry Pi heap target on servers
`cmd/airview/memlimit.go` sets a 384 MiB soft Go heap limit on every linux/arm and linux/arm64 build unless `GOMEMLIMIT` is set. An arm64 VPS (Ampere, Graviton) running an aggregator with hundreds of feeders would then garbage-collect constantly. Please skip that default in aggregator mode (or when the cgroup memory limit / physical RAM is above ~2 GiB), and consider deriving a target from the cgroup limit (`/sys/fs/cgroup/memory.max`, ~85 %). Until then `deploy/server/airview.service` and the `Dockerfile` set `GOMEMLIMIT=off`; remove that once the code does the right thing.

## WP-HL-headless -> cmd/airview (WP-E) + internal/store, internal/registry: `airview db snapshot` for online backups
`deploy/server/backup.sh --online` needs consistent copies of `airview.db` and `stations.db` while the service runs. Today it requires the `sqlite3` CLI (`.backup`). Please add `airview db snapshot -data <dir> -o <outdir>`: for every SQLite database of the data directory write `<outdir>/<name>.db` with `VACUUM INTO` (modernc.org/sqlite supports it; WAL lets it run beside the live server), exit 0 on success, non-zero on any failure, `-h` exits 0. backup.sh already probes `airview db snapshot -h` and uses it when present (contract tested in deploy/server/scripts_test.go with a fake binary); it runs as the `airview` user with `AIRVIEW_CONFIG`/`AIRVIEW_DATA` set.

## WP-HL-headless -> internal/server (WP-H): cheap unauthenticated liveness endpoint
The installer, upgrade/rollback check, restore check and the Docker HEALTHCHECK (`deploy/server/healthcheck`) probe `GET /api/status`, which builds the full status DTO. For monitoring at scale please add `GET /healthz` (or `/api/health`): no session, no scope, no rate-limit charge, 200 `{"ok":true,"version":...}` when the engine and store are up, 503 while starting/stopping. When it exists, tell WP-HL (or change the default path in deploy/server/healthcheck/main.go and deploy/server/lib.sh `wait_healthy`, which currently append `api/status`).

## WP-HL-headless -> repository root (integrator): Docker build context ignore file
Please add `/.dockerignore` (or `/Dockerfile.dockerignore`) with at least: `dist/`, `data/`, `**/data/*.db*`, `*.exe`, `airview`, `airview.exe`, `.git/`, `docs/`. The Dockerfile only COPYs `go.mod go.sum cmd internal web deploy/server/healthcheck`, so nothing private reaches an image layer, but without an ignore file `docker build .` still sends the whole tree (including `dist\data`, the user's live databases) to the Docker daemon as build context.

## WP-HL-headless -> deploy/feeder/pi (WP-FB): binary names in airview-feeder-pi.tar.gz
`deploy/build.ps1` now builds `airview-feeder` (linux arm64/armv7/amd64, windows amd64/arm64) and packs `airview-feeder-pi.tar.gz` as `airview-feeder-pi/{airview-feeder-linux-arm64, airview-feeder-linux-armv7, airview-feeder-linux-amd64}` (0755, root) plus every file of `deploy/feeder/pi/` except `*.go` and `testdata/` (`*.sh` 0755, others 0644; text files converted to LF). The installer there should pick the binary by `uname -m` with exactly these names. Windows: `airview-feeder-windows-{amd64,arm64}.exe` (console subsystem) in the output directory; the Inno Setup / portable ZIP packaging of `deploy/feeder/windows/` is not part of build.ps1 yet. Tell WP-HL if you need different names or a different subsystem (`-H windowsgui`).

## WP-HL-headless -> docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, README.md (integrator): mention the server kit
New: `deploy/server/` (Linux server install kit: install/upgrade/uninstall/backup/restore, hardened unit, logrotate, firewall guide, Caddy/nginx examples, avf-cert-sync.sh; guide in deploy/server/README.md), `Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.example.yml`, and `deploy/build.ps1 -OutDir/-Version/-SkipFeeder` with the artifacts listed in its header (server bundles `airview-server-linux-{amd64,arm64}.tar.gz`, `airview-server-install.sh`, feeder binaries, `airview-feeder-pi.tar.gz`, `SHA256SUMS`). Please link them from the project docs.

## WP-D-store -> internal/engine, cmd/airview (WP-E): open stations.db statistics, per-station analyzers, maintenance
Contract: `internal/store/WIRING.md`. In aggregator mode:
- `stationsDB, _ := store.OpenStations(dataDir, store.Options{Logger: log})` (same file as the registry; close it on shutdown after the lanes' final flush). Pass it as `tracker.LaneOptions.Store`.
- `LaneOptions.OnFlightStation`: convert `[]tracker.FlightStation` to `[]store.FlightStation` (field-for-field, snippet in WIRING.md) and `airviewDB.UpsertFlightStation(rows)`.
- Per-station analytics: `analytics.New(stationsDB, stationCfg, analytics.WithAntPrefix(store.StationAntPrefix(idx)), analytics.WithNetwork(airviewDB, sid))`, where `stationCfg()` returns the config with `Receiver` set to the station's effective location (and its antennas if known). Keep them in the Â§6.5 LRU (32, retired after 30 min offline). The host station may keep the standalone analyzer on airview.db.
- Run `airviewDB.Maintain(ctx, pol)` and `stationsDB.Maintain(ctx, pol)` hourly (plus the existing `airviewDB.Prune(ctx, cfg.Storage)`), `pol` from config (see the integrator request below; until then `store.DefaultTierPolicy()` with `MinuteDays = aggregator.retention.minuteDays`, `HourDays = 30*hourMonths`).
- Station deletion: after `registry.DeleteStation` completes (or from the registry deletion job via a callback), call `airviewDB.DeleteStationData(ctx, store.StationKeys{Prefix: store.StationAntPrefix(idx), SID: sid})` â€” flight_station and mlat_minute live in airview.db, which the registry job does not touch. Calling it on stationsDB too is harmless.
- `registry.Options.ExportExtra`: adapter in WIRING.md (`ExportStationData` on both stores per owned station).

## WP-D-store -> internal/registry (WP-R): statistics retention overlap
`registry.DefaultRetentionRules` deletes `stats_hour`, `range_hour`, `coverage`, `signal_hist`, `elev_hist`, `stats_minute`, `station_*_minute`, `latency_minute`, `receiver_status` by age. `store.Maintain` (stations.db) now applies the same tiers but safely: stats_minute only after its hours are rolled up, stats_hour only after its days are rolled up into the new `stats_day` (kept forever), and per-day histograms are merged into month buckets before deletion. Please (a) drop these store-owned tables from `DefaultRetentionRules` once the engine runs `stationsDB.Maintain` (or make the rules opt-in), so a registry pass never deletes hour rows that were not yet rolled into `stats_day`; and (b) note that the new tables `stats_day` (ant), `station_minute`/`station_clock_minute` (sid), `latency_minute` (feed) are already covered by your generic deletion. Also, `heat` in stations.db is keyed `(ant, cell)` (WITHOUT ROWID): your batched deletion by `ant` is a key-range delete there.

## WP-D-store -> internal/tracker (WP-T): no duplicate channel rows for single-channel stations
`lane.chanStats(ch)` creates `s<idx>/<ch>` statistics for every channel >= 0, so a single-channel station (channel 0) writes every minute, hour, coverage, signal, elevation and heat row twice (`s<idx>` and `s<idx>/0`, identical content). Measured with simnet this doubles stations.db growth for such feeders. Please write channel rows only for stations that have reported at least two channels (e.g. create channel slots lazily but emit them only when `len(used channels) >= 2`, or have the resolver / RECEIVER record declare the channel count). Analytics reads `s<idx>` for the station and `s<idx>/<ch>` only when a channel is selected.

## WP-D-store -> internal/model + internal/config (integrator): file-configurable storage tiers (Â§20.2/Â§20.3)
Optional `aggregator.storage` block mapped onto `store.TierPolicy` (also usable for airview.db): `minuteDays` (30, 1..3650; default from `retention.minuteDays`), `hourDays` (395, 7..3650; default `30*retention.hourMonths`), `dayDays` (0 = forever), `histDailyDays` (35, 1..3650), `histDays` (395, 0 = forever), `heatIdleDays` (395, 0 = forever), `freePct` (10, 1..90), `walCheckpointMb` (64, 4..4096), `optimizeHours` (24, 1..720), plus `queueRows` (2000000, 10000..50000000) for `store.Options.MaxQueueRows` of stations.db. Until it exists the defaults of `store.DefaultTierPolicy()` apply.

## WP-D-store -> internal/rfstat (owner of rfstat): station key for rfstat tables
DESIGN Â§6.6 lists `rfstat_*` among the stations.db tables keyed by ant, but `rfstat_hour` is keyed by `t` only, so it cannot hold more than one station. If per-station RF statistics are wanted in aggregator mode, add a version-2 migration with an `ant` column (key `(t, ant)`) and register it with `store.RegisterMigrationFor(store.KindMain|store.KindStations, "rfstat", 2, ...)`; until then rfstat stays in airview.db (host station only).

## WP-H-server -> internal/server/auth_routes.go (WP-A): declare the route scope of the account routes
Every route now declares `Access` (internal/server/access.go; lint `TestRouteAccessLint`). The account routes check their session themselves; please add `Access: AccessSession,` to each entry of `authRoutes`. Until then `routeAccess` recognises them by `Group == grpAccounts` (the only group allowed to rely on that fallback).

## WP-H-server -> integrator (docs/API.md): regenerate the API reference
New routes (routes_net.go: `/healthz`, `/api/public/*`, `/data/*.json`, `/api/me/*`, `/api/admin/*`, `/api/uplinks`) and the `station` parameter of `GET /api/aircraft` / `GET /api/events` are listed in the generated table of `internal/server/WIRING.md`; `TestDocsCoverage` accepts them from there until docs/API.md is regenerated (`$env:AIRVIEW_GEN_API_DOC="1"; go test ./internal/server -run TestGenerateAPIDoc`). After regenerating, nothing else is needed.

## WP-H-server -> internal/ingest/avfsrv (WP-I), internal/ingest/mlatc (WP-B3), cmd/airview (WP-E): live links for Feed Check and claims
Implement `server.LinkSource` (`LiveLinks() []server.LiveLink`: proto "avf"/"mlatc", station index, UUID, feeder ID, remote IP (memory only), connected since, msg/s, pos/s, kB/s, RTT, clock class name, MLAT synced (*bool), software, pending) and have cmd/airview add them to `server.Network.Links` next to `server.BeastLinks(...)`. Without them, Feed Check (`/api/me/check`), compat claims from the feeder's address and the admin link view see only Beast links.

## WP-H-server -> internal/engine, cmd/airview (WP-E): tracker network mode and per-station analytics for station pages
- The owner/admin projections (`mine`, `stations`, geometry of `?station=`) need `tr.SetNetwork(reg.Resolver(), lanes)`; without it owners get the merged picture with every receiver field stripped (safe, but no "heard by me").
- The UI sends `station=<sid>` to the legacy single-station routes (stats, antenna, rf, timing, coverage, ...). Those stay admin-only in aggregator mode (their data is the host station's) until per-station analytics exist: when `Engine.Stations()` can build an `analytics.Analyzer` with `WithAntPrefix("s<idx>")` (and the station's own position), tell WP-H and the server will open these routes to owners with `Station: StationQuery` and route them to that station's components.

## WP-H-server -> internal/mlat (WP-M*, phase 2/3): data for the owner Timing / MLAT sections and the public MLAT quality
`/api/me/stations/{sid}` returns `timing: null`, `mlat: null`; `/api/public/summary` returns `mlat: null`. Expose (read-only, lock-free snapshot) per station: clock class, anchor state + reason, Ïƒ_Î¸, drift ppm, Î²_short, residual bias, outlier %, clock events, sync peers (`reg.PeerTag(viewerSID, peerSID, now)`, distance band, sync rate, rms ns, relative ppm); MLAT contributions/h, used/excluded %; network P50/P95/P99 and fixes/min, plus 1Â° cells with >= 5 contributing stations for the public quality map. WP-H will map them into publicdto / owner DTOs.

## WP-H-server -> internal/tracker (WP-T): public network coverage union (DESIGN Â§8.2)
The public "network coverage union" (cells below 3000 ft AGL suppressed where fewer than 3 stations contribute) needs a per-cell station count across lanes. Please provide e.g. `lanes.CoverageUnion(minStations int, lowAltFt int) []CoverageCell{LatIdx, LonIdx int; MaxAltFt; Stations int}` (no station identities); the server will publish it at `/api/public/coverage` from publicdto types.

## WP-H-server -> web/static (WP-W): shapes as implemented
All routes of "WP-W-web -> internal/server" exist with the names in `web/static/js/net/endpoints.js`, with these differences: `/api/me/check` links also carry `registered`, `claimMethod` ("code" | "uuid" | "admin") and `pending`; claimable is a bool. OwnerStation also has `kind`, `rxNo`, `uuid`, `feederId`, `located`, `anchor`, `created`; `stats` is `{msgRate, posRate, aircraft, positions, uptimePct?, aircraftDay?, mlatContrib?}` (uptimePct / aircraftDay / onlyMe24h are not computed yet). Station detail sections: `connection{proto, since, kbps, rttMs, lateFrames, links[{proto, since, ip, msgRate, ...}]}` (no p50/p95 RTT, fps by DF, spool or host offset yet), `location{status, effective, declared, gnss, estimated, estimates[{id, method, t, lat, lon, alt, sigmaM, ...}]}` (no ellipse yet), `timing: null`, `mlat: null`. Admin list `{stations, total, counts}` (audited `station.list`); `PUT /api/admin/stations/{sid}` takes `{name, trust (absolute), owner (id or user name)}`; token create takes `{label, maxUses, days}` and answers the token fields plus `token` (secret); audit entries carry `target` (SID); retention answers `{ran, ...}`. `/api/public/stations` entries: `{sid? (only with a public pin), name?, online, pin|null, stats?}`. Public/owner `/api/status` and the SSE `status` event are `publicdto.Status` (no station, feeds, NTP, maxRangeKm). `GET /api/config` below admin: `{version, mode, ui, prediction, aggregator{name, publicMap, publicStats, tarJson}}`.

## WP-H-server -> deploy/server (WP-HL): the health endpoint exists
`GET /healthz`: no session, no scope, never rate-limited or cached, no database query. `200 {"ok":true,"status":"ok"|"degraded","mode","version","uptimeS","checks":{"engine":"ok","registry":"ok","admin"?:"setup pending"},"adminReady"?:bool}` while the engine runs (`degraded` = an aggregator service such as the registry is missing); `503 {"ok":false,"status":"unavailable",...}` while the engine is stopped or restarting. `HEAD` works too. Please switch `deploy/server/healthcheck/main.go` and `lib.sh wait_healthy` from `api/status` to `healthz`.

## WP-H-server -> WP-PI-installer / WP-UP (deploy/feeder/pi, internal/selfupdate): /feeder/* routes not implemented yet
`GET /feeder/install.sh`, `/feeder/manifest.json(.sig)` and `/feeder/dl/<target>` need the AVF certificate pin, the release signing key and the release files from the engine / selfupdate package, none of which the server can reach today. When an engine accessor exists (e.g. `Engine.FeederRelease() (install []byte, manifest, sig []byte, open func(target string) (io.ReadSeekCloser, error), err)` plus `Engine.AVFPin()`), tell WP-H; the routes (AccessPublic, not rate-limited, `Cache-Control: no-cache`, preset lines validated) are then a small addition to routes_net.go.

## WP-W-web -> internal/server (WP-H): follow-up after reading the WP-H route table
The web UI now uses the routes and DTOs of `internal/server/routes_net.go` as they are (`/api/public/stats`, `/api/public/stations` `entries`, Feed Check `claim{}` + `warnings`, `POST /api/me/stations/{sid}`, `/stats`, `/connections`, `POST /api/admin/stations/{sid}`, tokens `{note,maxUses,ttlHours}` -> `{secret,token}`, audit `sid`); `web/static/js/net/endpoints.js` normalises them, so the shapes in the entry above are superseded. Remaining requests, all optional for the UI (it shows a dash or a note until they exist):
1. `/api/events`, `/api/aircraft`, `/api/aircraft/{icao}`, `/api/trails`: accept `?station=<sid>` (StationQuery) for owners/admins and add the geometry (`distKm`, `bearing`, `elevation`) of that station, like `/api/me/aircraft`. The UI already sends the parameter when a station is selected; today it is ignored.
2. `/api/public/stats`: an optional `mlat: {p50M, p95M, p99M, fixesPerMin}` (hold-out accuracy, DESIGN Â§8.2 "empirical MLAT quality") - the Network page shows it when present.
3. `/api/me/stations/{sid}`: optional `timing` (`clockClass, anchor{state,reason}, sigmaThetaNs, driftPpm, betaShortNs, residualBiasNs, outlierPct, events[{t,reason,detail}], peers[{tag,band:"near"|"mid"|"far",syncRate,rmsNs,ppm}]`) and `mlat` (`contribPerHour, usedPct, excludedPct, residualBiasNs, outlierPct`) blocks once the MLAT engine exists (DESIGN Â§11 Timing / MLAT sections).
4. DESIGN Â§10.1 "Use this location": a `POST /api/me/stations/{sid}/location/confirm {"t":<estimate t>,"method":"survey"}` that confirms a survey estimate (status `confirmed`). Until then the UI's "Use this location" declares the estimate's position (`declared`, altRef `hae`).
5. `GET /api/admin/stations`: the owner's user name next to `ownerId` (the UI shows "account #id"), and optionally the exact `effective` position (audited) for the map's Admin view - without it the Admin view draws only the admin's own stations.
- WP-E-engine (update to the avfsrv request above): the engine now starts `avfsrv` itself (internal/engine/avflisten.go) with `TLSFromConfig`, `LoadOrCreateServerID`, gate, clone guard, watermarks, `OnReceiver` (location -> registry) and `SetPolicy` on config changes; cmd/airview adds an AVF `LiveLinks` adapter over `Server.Sessions()`. Still wanted from WP-I: nothing blocking; `OnGNSS` / `OnMinuteStats` / `OnInputStatus` / `OnOnline` consumers are open (owners of the survey, stats merge and admin pages).

## WP-I-ingest -> internal/engine, cmd/airview (WP-E): wire the station-aware ingest and the AVF listener
Contract: `internal/ingest/WIRING.md` and `internal/ingest/avfsrv/WIRING.md`. In aggregator mode only:
- `ing, _ := ingest.New(ingest.Options{Handler: tr, Resolver: reg.Resolver(), Limits: cfg.Aggregator.Limits, Now: ntp.Now, Logger: ...})` after `tr.SetNetwork(reg.Resolver(), lanes)`; `go ing.Run(ctx)`; `ing.SetLimits(next.Aggregator.Limits)` on ApplyConfig.
- The host station's feeds go to `ing` (it is a `model.FrameSink`; Station 0 = `model.HostStation`) instead of `tr.HandleFrame` (never call `HandleFrame` in aggregator mode). SBS-in bridge and own MLAT results: `ing.HandleUpdate` (passed through).
- beastin: `beastin.New(opt, reg.Beast(), ing)` with `opt.Budget = ing.Budget()` (replaces `beastin.FrameSinkAdapter`).
- AVF when `aggregator.avfPort > 0`: `tlsCfg, pin, _ := avfsrv.TLSFromConfig(dataDir, cfg.Aggregator)`, `sid, _ := avfsrv.LoadOrCreateServerID(dataDir)`, `o := avfsrv.OptionsFromConfig(cfg.Aggregator)`, set `TLSConfig, ServerID, Resolver: reg.Resolver(), Gate: accounts AdminReady, Now: ntp.Now, Logger, Hooks`; `srv, _ := avfsrv.New(o, reg, ing)`; `go srv.ListenAndServe(ctx)`; `srv.SetPolicy(avfsrv.PolicyFromConfig(next.Aggregator))` on ApplyConfig; `srv.Close()` on shutdown before `ing` stops. Show `pin` and `sid` on the admin page / install one-liner.
- `Hooks.OnReceiver(st, avf.Receiver)`: apply the RECEIVER's declared location (`reg.SetDeclared` when the station has none or the owner has not overridden it), name and privacy per the engine's policy (DESIGN Â§3, Â§10.1); `Hooks.OnGNSS` -> `reg.SetGNSS`; `Hooks.OnMinuteStats` -> selective-mode statistics (phase 4 WP-I2 merge); `Hooks.OnInputStatus` -> station status page.

## WP-I-ingest -> internal/avf (WP-P): server-side flate writer memory
The live 1,000-feeder run (`avfsrv-load`) shows ~0.7 MB per AVF session in `compress/flate.NewWriter` / `(*fastGen).reset` for the serverâ†’client stream (PING/ACK/INTEREST/results only, a few hundred bytes per second): ~700 MB of a 1.0-1.2 GB heap at 1,000 feeders (DESIGN Â§20.2 targets 1,000+). Please make the writer cheaper for the server direction, e.g. `flate.NewWriter(w, flate.HuffmanOnly)` (still a valid raw DEFLATE stream for any reader; no LZ77 window/hash tables) or a lazily created writer, selected through `ConnOptions` (default unchanged for clients). The ingest needs no API change.

## WP-I-ingest -> internal/registry (WP-R): persistent AVF serverId in stations.db
AVF.md Â§3.2 says the serverId is "generated randomly once and kept in stations.db". Until the registry provides it, `avfsrv.LoadOrCreateServerID(dataDir)` keeps it in `<data>/avf-tls/server-id` (0600). Please add `reg.ServerID() string` (created once in `registry_meta`, migrating an existing `avf-tls/server-id` file value if present so feeders do not see a changed network identity); WP-E then passes it as `avfsrv.Options.ServerID`.

## WP-I-ingest -> internal/server (WP-H): admin ingest pages
Admin scope only: `ing.Stats()` (ingest counters + budget), `ing.Sessions()` (links: queue fill, bytes/s, FLOW, per-station mode/fps), `ing.Timing(idx)` (time correction per station), `srv.Stats()` (AVF counters, refusal reasons), `srv.Sessions()` (contains the raw remote IP: never owner/public), `POST .../avf/{feederId}/disconnect` -> `srv.Disconnect(feederID, "denied", reason, 600)`. Owners may see their own stations' `Timing` and mode without addresses.

## WP-I-ingest -> internal/mlat (WP-M, WP-K, WP-GR; phase 2): grouper and clock interfaces
Implement `ingest.MLATSink` (`Observe(*ingest.Group)`; optionally `ingest.LateObserver`), `ingest.ClockView` (`NetTime`, `BaselineNs`, `SyncCandidate`) and `ingest.ClockObserver` (CLOCK_EVENT/PAIR/STATUS) and pass them as `ingest.Options.MLAT/Clock/ClockObserver`. `*Group` is valid only during the call (sweeper goroutine); `Copy.Timed` already excludes Beast 30004, `timestamps:false` and second-channel copies. The planner sends `ing.SetInterest(st, ingest.InterestCmd{...})`; results and feedback go out with `avfsrv.Server.SendResults` / `SendFeedback`. Identical replies 0.74 ms apart (interrogation pairs) can share one group (~0.3-0.5 % of groups in simnet): the Â§9.3 clustering must split by network time.

## WP-I-ingest -> internal/model + internal/config (integrator): file-configurable ingest tunables (Â§20.3)
Optional block `aggregator.ingest` mapped onto `ingest.Options` / `avfsrv.Options`: `windowMs` (1000, 200..5000), `flushAfterMs` (400, 100..window), `retainMs` (1000, 0..10000), `maxMatchMs` (300, 20..700), `minMatchMs` (10, 1..maxMatch), `uniqueSkewMs` (2500, 0..10000), `lateAfterMs` (window+retain+500), `shards` (64, 1..4096), `reduceMs` (500, 100..5000), `flowHoldSec` (5), `flowGraceSec` (10), `avfQueueRecords` (64, 4..4096), `avfResumeGraceSec` (30, 0..300), `avfMaxSessions` (10000). Until it exists the defaults apply (the limits already come from `aggregator.limits`).

## WP-I-ingest -> docs/aggregator (WP-DOC / integrator): DESIGN Â§6.2 same-station threshold
`internal/ingest` uses 32 Âµs of the station's own ticks (not 1 ms) to decide that a second copy from a station already in a group is the same transmission (other channel). Identical Mode S replies 0.74 ms apart occur (ACAS / DF4-20 interrogation pairs; reproduced by simnet), and a reply lasts at least 64 Âµs. Untimed copies: a copy on the same channel is always a new transmission, one on another channel within 1 ms of host time is the same. Please amend Â§6.2 accordingly.
Addendum (WP-D-store, same request): also `flightStationDays` (90, 0 = keep with the flights, 1..3650) for `TierPolicy.FlightStationDays` â€” measured with simnet, flight_station grows by ~115 bytes per (flight, station), i.e. up to ~1.6 MB per feeder per day in airview.db; with the flights retention (730 days) that would be ~1 GB per feeder.

## Integrator (after Wave 1B): status of every open request

Build/vet/test after the fixes: `go build ./...` and `go vet ./...` are clean for windows/amd64, linux/amd64, linux/arm64 and linux/arm (GOARM=7); `gofmt -l` is empty; `go test ./...` passes in every package. The wave left no build or test break; everything below is hook work.

- WP-HB-hub -> internal/engine (hub admin-only): **DONE** (by WP-E: `syncHub` passes `AdminOnly`, calls `SetAdminOnly` on reconfigure and warns when no password is set). `QuietRejections` in admin diagnostics: not done (optional).
- WP-HB-hub -> internal/config (`config check` rule): **DONE** (integrator; `semanticIssues` in internal/config/report.go warns on `hub.password`; test `TestCheckHubPasswordInAggregatorMode`).
- WP-DOC-docs -> internal/registry, internal/store (flight_station with a station): **DONE** (integrator). New `registry.Options.DeleteExtra(ctx, idx, sid)`. The deletion job calls it before the station's registry rows go; an error keeps the job queued (test `TestDeleteExtra`). The engine wires it to `airviewDB.DeleteStationData` (flight_station, mlat_minute), so RETENTION.md and PRIVACY-NOTICE.md are now true for flight_station.
- WP-DOC-docs -> internal/server (anonymous /api/config, /api/status): **DONE** (by WP-H). The integrator checked it live on port 18540 in aggregator mode with no session. `/api/config` returns only `{version, mode, ui, prediction, aggregator{name, publicMap, publicStats, tarJson}}`, with no receiver. `/api/status` reports `mode: aggregator`. `privacy_golden_test.go` covers both routes.
- WP-DOC-docs -> web/static, deploy/feeder/**, cmd/airview-feeder (consent texts): **PARTLY DONE** (integrator).
  - C1 is verbatim in `web/static/js/net/view.js` (quote style corrected), `deploy/feeder/pi/install.sh` and `cmd/airview-feeder/setup.go`. `TestConsentTextMatchesDesign` now also checks these three files.
  - The UI label of `cell01` is "Area (0.1Â°)", which matches C2.
  - `airview-feeder config set 'uplinks[0].timestamps=false'` is accepted (same path parser as `-set`).
  - **Open (WP-W2 / WP-FB / WP-WF):** C2-C8 word for word at the places CONSENT.md lists. The Windows installer privacy page does not exist yet.
- WP-DOC-docs -> deploy/**, cmd/airview (names the operator guide assumes): **PARTLY DONE**. These final names now exist and are listed in docs/ARCHITECTURE.md (command-line section): `airview serve`, `airview backup|restore`, `airview db snapshot`, `GET /healthz`, `deploy/server/install.sh` (one-line installer via `--base-url`) and the `Dockerfile`. **Open (WP-DOC):** change OPERATOR-GUIDE.md Â§0/Â§3/Â§13 from *(planned)* to these names; put the licence texts in the release bundles.
- WP-DOC-docs -> internal/refdata, internal/modes (clean-room R1/R2): **PARTLY DONE** (integrator). The R2 comments in internal/modes/infer.go now rest on Doc 9871, the book or physical reasoning; no pyModeS reference is left. This is logged in the CLEANROOM.md Â§7 "Review log". **Open:** a human reviewer has to sign off R2 (structural comparison) and R1 (registration rules). The integrator opened no GPL file.
- WP-PI-installer -> cmd/airview-feeder (CLI/env contract): **DONE** (integrator + WP-FB).

- WP-HL-headless -> docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, README.md: **DONE** (integrator). README has a new "Network aggregator and Linux servers" section and the build artifacts; ARCHITECTURE lists the deployment kits.

- WP-D-store -> internal/registry (retention overlap): **DONE** (integrator). New `registry.Options.StoreMaintained`. The engine sets it when the stations.db statistics are open, so `RunRetention` leaves the statistics tables to `store.Maintain` (test `TestStoreMaintainedSkipsStatsRetention`). Without it (cmd/airview admin, tests) the old rules apply.
- WP-D-store -> internal/tracker (duplicate channel rows): **DONE** (integrator; `lane.multiChannel`). Channel rows (`s<idx>/<ch>`) are written only once a station has reported two channels; a single channel's slot is drained without writing rows. Tests: `TestNetworkHandleAndLanes`, `TestLaneMinuteRows` (updated), `TestLaneChannelRows` (new).
- WP-D-store -> internal/model + internal/config (storage tiers): **DONE** (integrator). New block `aggregator.storage`, mapped by `store.TierPolicyFromConfig` (test `TestTierPolicyFromConfig`). docs/CONFIG.md and the example are regenerated. Fields and defaults:

## WP-X-scale -> internal/server/routes*.go (WP-H): viewport subscription route and parameters (SO-1)
Register `{Method: "POST", Path: "/api/events/view", Group: grpPublic, Access: AccessPublic, Raw: rawEventsView, NoAuto: true}` (handler in `internal/server/sse_view.go`; params `id` = stream token from the `stream` event, `bbox` = `south,west,north,east`, empty = whole world; 204, 404 unknown id, 400 bad box). Add to the Params of `GET /api/events` and `GET /api/public/events`: `bbox` ("`south,west,north,east` (west > east crosses the antimeridian): a viewport stream; first event `stream` `{id, bbox}`, then only the aircraft inside the box; delta streams send aircraft entering the view in full in `upd`, aircraft leaving it (outside the box grown by 10 %) in `del`; `counts` stay global; aircraft without a position are never sent"), `view` ("`1`: a viewport stream of the whole world, narrowed later with `POST /api/events/view`") and `replace` ("stream id of a previous stream of this client: it is closed first, so a reconnect with a new viewport is not refused by the one-public-stream-per-address limit"). Regenerate docs/API.md. Consider a per-IP rate limit on the POST route (map pans; the client debounces).

## WP-X-scale -> web/static/js/live.js (WP-W): use the viewport subscription on the map page (SO-1)
The server side is done and backwards compatible (no change needed to keep working). To save bandwidth: open the map's stream with `&bbox=` = the visible map bounds (`south,west,north,east`, normalised to -180..180, west > east across the antimeridian); listen to the `stream` event and keep `id`; on `moveend` (debounced ~500 ms) `POST /api/events/view?id=<id>&bbox=...` once WP-H has registered the route (until then, or on 404: reconnect with `&bbox=<new>&replace=<id>`). Frames are ordinary keyframes / deltas of a smaller picture: aircraft that leave the view arrive in `del`. Pages that need every aircraft (lists, tables, stats) must keep the unfiltered stream (no `bbox`); a viewport stream never carries aircraft without a position. Measured at 10k aircraft: zoom-8 view 54 kB/s raw / 10.5 kB/s gzip against 712 / 139 kB/s for the unfiltered delta stream.

## WP-X-scale -> internal/model + internal/config (integrator): trail tier settings (SO-6, Â§20.3)
Add `aggregator.storage.trailFullDays` (default 2, 0..3650; 0 = never thin) and `aggregator.storage.trailThinSec` (default 30, 0 or 2..3600; 0 = never thin) with field docs. Then in `store.TierPolicyFromConfig` (internal/store/tiered.go) replace the line `p.TrailFullDays, p.TrailThinSec = DefaultTrailFullDays, DefaultTrailThinSec` by the two config fields (WP-X-scale or the integrator may do that edit; `TestTierPolicyFromConfig` compares against `DefaultTierPolicy()`). Until then the defaults apply in aggregator mode (Maintain on airview.db, hourly, `internal/engine/network.go` already calls it). Standalone is unchanged (Prune only); a standalone could opt in by calling `st.ThinTrails(ctx, cutoff, 30)`.

## WP-X-scale -> internal/server/api_replay.go + routes_ext.go (WP-H): replay of thinned trails (SO-6)
Trails of flights that ended more than `TrailFullDays` (2) ago keep one point per 30 s (gaps up to 35 s). `stale = max(step*2, 3*trailIntervalSec, 30)` can blink an aircraft for a few seconds between two kept points: use at least `TrailThinSec + trailIntervalSec + 5` (40 s) for frames older than two days (`store.DefaultTrailThinSec`), and update the `/api/replay` note ("Stored trails have one point per `config.storage.trailIntervalSec` for 2 days, then one per 30 s").

## WP-X-scale -> docs/aggregator/OPERATOR-GUIDE.md Â§2.3 (WP-DOC): measured numbers after SO-1/SO-6
Disk: positions 0.61 GB per 1,000 aircraft and day (35 B/row at 5 s); steady state with 7 trail days 4.3 GB -> 1.7 GB per 1,000 aircraft (10,000 aircraft: 42.8 -> 16.9 GB) with the trail tiers (2 days full, then 30 s). Outbound: a viewport stream at 10k aircraft costs 13-54 kB/s raw (2.5-10.5 kB/s gzip) for zoom 9-8 maps, 185 kB/s (36 gzip) at zoom 7, against 712 kB/s (139 gzip) unfiltered.

## WP-X-scale -> internal/ingest, internal/engine/netingest.go (WP-I, WP-E): CreditBatch is optional (SO-5)
`tracker.Lanes.NewCreditBatch()` gives a per-worker buffer (`Credit`, `CreditChannel`, `Flush`; equivalent output proven by `TestCreditBatchEquivalent`, 0 allocs). Measured with 256 shards it is within about Â±8 % of `Lanes.Credit` (BenchmarkLaneCreditContention; with the old 16 shards it saved 10-25 %), so wiring it is **not** required now. If a profile on a many-core host shows `sync.(*Mutex).lockSlow` under `Lanes.Credit`, use one batch per reader goroutine, `Flush` at the end of every network read and before the goroutine blocks (at most a few ms of delay).

## WP-RF-ref -> internal/mlat (WP-M, engine root): feed and maintain the reference store
Create one `ref.Store` (`ref.New(opts, weather)`) per engine. For every transmission (once, not per copy) call `Store.Observe(&msg, txNs, txSigmaNs)` with the decoded message and its transmit instant in network time (best: `Store.TransmitInstant` over synced copies; fallback `Group.FirstNs` âˆ’ 1 ms with Ïƒ â‰ˆ 5 ms). Call `Store.Expire(now)` about every 10 s. Feed every hold-out MLAT fix (and any TDOA solution of an ADS-B message from â‰¥ 4 GPS anchors) back with `Store.ObserveFix(key, tau, posECEF, covECEF)`: it learns the T=0 position latency (simnet: P95 66 m â†’ 5 m). Contract and examples: `internal/mlat/ref/WIRING.md`.

## WP-RF-ref -> internal/mlat/clock (WP-K): use ref.State as the sync reference
`Store.At(key, tauNs, tauSigmaNs, &st)` + `st.Usable()` implement the Â§9.2.2 gates (NACp â‰¥ 8, NIC â‰¥ 7, position age â‰¤ 1 s, integrity flags). `st.Cov` (ECEF) is the full Î£_pos (smoother + NACp bias + latency + Ï„ uncertainty). The Â§9.2.4 blacklist should call `Store.Suspend(key, until)`; `Store.CheckHeard(key, tau, stationsECEF)` rejects references below a receiver's radio horizon.

## WP-RF-ref -> internal/mlat/solve (WP-SV): altitude row through the GNSSâˆ’baro field
Convert Mode S pressure altitudes with `Store.HAEFromBaro(key, lat, lon, baroFt, tNs)` (own GNSSâˆ’baro of ADS-B aircraft, else the field) or `Store.Field().BaroToHAE(...)`; the returned Ïƒ is the conversion 1Ïƒ (add quantisation and age Ã— vertical-rate inflation).

## WP-RF-ref -> internal/engine or internal/mlat (owner of the METAR adapter): ref.Weather
Implement `ref.Weather` (`SurfaceAt(lat, lon, tNs) (qnhHPa, tempC, ok)`) from the `internal/weather` METAR cache (nearest station â‰¤ ~150 km, cached) and pass it to `ref.New`. Without it the field fallback is the geoid only (Ïƒ 150 m instead of 60 m) until cells learn from TC 19 data.

## WP-RF-ref -> internal/model + internal/config (integrator): file-configurable reference-track tunables (Â§20.3)
Optional block `aggregator.mlat.ref` mapped onto `ref.Options` / `ref.FieldOptions` (zero = default): `window` 16, `qHorizontal` 4, `qVertical` 1, `minNacp` 8, `minNic` 7, `maxPosAgeS` 1, `latencySigmaS` 0.15, `gateChi2` 30, `gateFloorM` 25, `probationS` 30, `suspendS` 600, `trackTimeoutS` 120, `field.cellKm` 50, `field.bandFt` 2000, `field.driftMPerH` 20, `field.obsSigmaM` 12. A mapper `ref.OptionsFromConfig` can live in internal/mlat/ref once the model type exists.

## WP-GR -> internal/mlat (WP-M, engine root): wire the assembler and the planner
Construct `plan.New(...)` and `group.New(group.Options{Clock, Sites, Hints, Selector: planner, Sink})` as in `internal/mlat/group/WIRING.md` and `internal/mlat/plan/WIRING.md`. The engine's `ingest.MLATSink` calls `planner.ObserveGroup(g)` then `asm.Observe(g)`; `ObserveLate` goes to `asm.ObserveLate(g)`. Clusters with `Use >= group.UseSolveAlt` go to the solver, every cluster (incl. `UseTrack`, `Late`) to the tracker. Feed `planner.NoteADSB(key, now, good)` from `ref.Store` verdicts (NACp â‰¥ 8, NIC â‰¥ 7) and `planner.MarkSuspect` from ADS-B verification; give `HoldOut` from quality. `go planner.Run(ctx)`. Clock adapter: convert `clock.Snapshot.NetTime` seconds-since-origin into `group.Time` without losing sub-ns precision, return its ÏƒÂ² as clockVar and the station's learned jitterÂ² as jitterVar, and implement `group.Domains` (clock component) â€” without it stations of different components could be clustered together. `group.CrossCov` is optional.

## WP-GR -> internal/ingest (WP-I): adaptive flush per aircraft (DESIGN Â§9.3 "Flush")
The ingest flushes every group at the fixed `FlushAfter` (400 ms). DESIGN Â§9.3 wants the p99 delivery latency of the aircraft's usual hearers, clamped to 150-600 ms; `group.FlushEstimator` computes it (`Delay(key)`, 53 ns). Requested: (1) an optional `ingest.Options.FlushPolicy interface{ FlushAfter(key uint32) time.Duration }` consulted when a group is created (or at its first sweep), falling back to `FlushAfter`; (2) a per-copy delivery-latency report, either `Copy.RecvNs` (server receive time; latency = RecvNs âˆ’ CorrNs) or a callback `DeliveryObserver.ObserveDelay(st, latencyNs, nowNs)`. Until then the fixed 400 ms flush stays and late copies (â‰¤ 1 s window) are joined by `ObserveLate`.

## WP-GR -> internal/ingest/mlatc (WP-B3): planner hooks for mlat-client sessions
Implement `plan.SendingController` (`StartSending(st, keys)`, `StopSending(st, keys)` â†’ `start_sending` / `stop_sending` with ICAO hex lists; keys are tracker keys, bit 24 = non-ICAO) and pass it as `plan.MLATCOut{C: srv}` in the planner's `Out` (via `plan.Fanout` with `plan.IngestOut`). Feed `seen` messages into `planner.NoteHearer(key, st, now)` (and nothing for `lost`: hearers expire after `HeardTTL`).

## WP-GR -> internal/avf + docs/aggregator/AVF.md (WP-P / integrator): Mode A/C interest flag (phase 5)
INTEREST has no way to ask a selective feeder for Mode A/C frames. For DESIGN Â§9.9 add an optional `"modeAC": true|false` member to INTEREST (absent = unchanged; feeders that do not know it ignore it). The planner exposes `ModeACEligible(st)`; once the field exists, `plan.Delta` gets a `ModeAC *bool` and `IngestOut` forwards it.

## WP-GR -> internal/model + internal/config (integrator): file-configurable group/plan tunables (Â§20.3)
Optional blocks mapped onto `group.Config` and `plan.Config` (zero = default, both have `Normalize()`): `aggregator.mlat.group` {`nSigma` 4, `slackM` 1000, `hintSlackM` 300, `hintMaxSigmaM` 5000, `colocatedM` 1000, `minSites` 4, `minSitesAlt` 3, `maxParts` 4, `lateHoldMs` 2000, `lateSlots` 16384}; `aggregator.mlat.plan` {`syncSetMin` 15, `syncSetMax` 30, `mlatSetMax` 400, `stationFpsBudget` 400, `stationsPerAircraft` 12, `minStationsPerAircraft` 6, `targetSigmaM` 30, `heardTtlS` 60, `adsbGoodAgeS` 10, `minDwellS` 30, `maxChurn` 10, `replanEveryS` 5, `acquireHz` 4, `trackedHz` 2, `holdOutHz` 1, `suspectHz` 0.5, `globalSolveHz` 20000}. `aggregator.mlat.modeAC` (exists) should set `plan.Config.ModeAC.Enabled`.

## WP-SV-solve -> internal/mlat (WP-M, engine root): wire the solver
One `solve.NewSolver(solve.Options{Atmosphere: atm})` per solver worker (not concurrency-safe; `Solve` allocates nothing). Per cluster with `Use >= group.UseSolveAlt`: build `solve.Problem` from `clock.Snapshot.Convert` (TOA seconds relative to an epoch near the cluster; `Cov` = clock covariance nsÂ²Â·1e-18 + diag(jitterÂ²) via `solve.CovFromClock`), the altitude row via `solve.AltitudeFromHAE(refStore.HAEFromBaro(...), AltitudeInput{QuantFt, AgeS, VRateMps})`, the track prediction as `Prior`, the last fix as `Last`, and the corrected first host arrival (`HostFirstS`). Publish `f.MLATFix(icao, epochNs)` when `f.OK()`; hand `FlagAmbiguous` fixes with `f.Alt` and `FlagPriorFused` posteriors to the tracker only. Feed `f.Resid/WTest/Weight/MDB/Excluded` to quality (per-station Ïƒ learning, bias flags). Contract: `internal/mlat/solve/WIRING.md`.

## WP-SV-solve -> internal/model + internal/config (integrator): file-configurable solver tunables (Â§20.3)
Optional block `aggregator.mlat.solve` mapped onto `solve.Options` (zero = default, `Options.Defaults()` clamps): `maxIter` 10, `huberK` 1.345, `pfa` 1e-3, `maxSigmaHM` 2000, `altRejectSigma` 4, `priorSeedMaxSigmaM` 2000, `gridStepM` 5000, `gridHalfM` 400000, `maxSpeedMps` 308.7, `machFactor` 1.25, `hostTolS` 0.05, `horizonSlackM` 300, `minHaeM` -500, `maxHaeM` 25000, `maxRangeM` 800000, `relinM` 5000, `ransacMinStations` 6, `ransacMaxSubsets` 64, `alwaysAlternates` false. A mapper `solve.OptionsFromConfig` can live in internal/mlat/solve once the model type exists.

## WP-TR-track -> internal/mlat (WP-M, engine root): wire the tracker
Construct `track.NewSharded(track.Params{Atmosphere: atm}, 0)` (contract: `internal/mlat/track/WIRING.md`). Route every solver fix to `Fix` (ambiguous 3+alt fixes as `NHyp = 2` with `solve.Fix.Alt` as the second hypothesis), every assembled cluster (incl. 2-station `UseTrack` and late re-emissions) to `Cluster`, DF17 TC19 / BDS 5,0 / 6,0 velocities to `Velocity`, and call `Sweep(now, emit)` at 1 Hz; publish confirmed estimates via `est.MLATFix()` as `DecodedUpdate{MLAT:true, Src: mlat-av}`. **TOA Ïƒ split:** `TOA.Sigma` = learned per-transmission jitter, `TOA.SyncSigma` = the clock snapshot's marginal Ïƒ_Î¸ of the station (do not add Ïƒ_Î¸ into `Sigma`/`Cov`; the tracker models it as correlated). Attach the latest Mode S altitude (HAE via `ref.Store.HAEFromBaro`, Ïƒ inflated by age Ã— |v_z|) to clusters of frames without altitude. Use `Predict` as the solver prior / grouper hint. Call `Delete(icao)` when ADS-B positions take over.

## WP-TR-track -> internal/model + internal/config (integrator): file-configurable tracker tunables (Â§20.3)
Optional block `aggregator.mlat.track` mapped onto `track.Params` (zero = default, `Normalize` clamps): `qCv` 0.3, `qCt` 0.5, `qVert` 1, `qOmega` 3e-5, `omegaTauS` 20, `sojournCvS` 300, `sojournCtS` 20, `initPCt` 0.1, `initSigmaVelH` 150, `initSigmaVelU` 15, `initSigmaOmega` 0.03, `gateProb` 0.999, `iterations` 3, `confirmWindowS` 10, `confirmUpdates` 2, `coastS` 60, `maxSigmaHM` 5000, `maxSpeedMS` 360, `hypLR` 100, `maxHyp` 4, `respawnMisses` 3, `losMarginM` 3000, `maxMembers` 24, `maxExclude` 2, `maxLagS` 2.5, `excessRefreshM` 2000, `recentreM` 5000, `syncTauS` 15. The atmosphere comes from the existing refractivity settings / k_atm.

## WP-B3-mlatc -> internal/engine, cmd/airview (WP-E): start the mlat-client listener (31090)
In aggregator mode with `aggregator.mlatPort > 0`: `opt := mlatc.OptionsFromConfig(cfg.Aggregator)`, `opt.ServerKey = reg.IngestKey()`, `opt.Now = ntp.Now`, `opt.Gate = AdminReady`, `opt.Logger`; `srv, _ := mlatc.New(opt, regAdapter, mlatc.FromIngest(ing))`; `go srv.ListenAndServe(ctx)`; `srv.SetPolicy(mlatc.PolicyFromConfig(next.Aggregator))` on ApplyConfig; `srv.Close()` on shutdown (before the ingest stops). Hooks: `OnSeen` -> `planner.NoteHearer(key, st, now)` for each address (WP-GR request), `OnAttach`/`OnDetach` -> `reg.ConnOpen`/`ConnClose` and `reg.Seen`, `OnPosition` -> `reg.SetGNSS` (Radarcape GNSS, de-duplicated like AVF GNSS_POS). Feed Check / compat claims: a `server.LinkSource` adapter over `srv.Links()` (proto "mlatc", UUID, IP, since, `MsgRate`, `KBps`, clock class, software = `ClientVersion`, pending = `Admission == pending`). Contract and example: `internal/ingest/mlatc/WIRING.md`.

## WP-B3-mlatc -> internal/registry (WP-R): mlatc.Registry adapter
Like `reg.Beast()`: a `reg.MLATC()` implementing `mlatc.Registry`. `Attach`: `Admit{Kind: model.StationKindMLATC, UUID, Name: User, ClockCls, PrivacyOff}` (UUID-less: `IdentKey: reg.UnidentifiedKey(RemoteIP, port)` plus `"/<Slot>"` for `Slot > 0`); when `Identity.Located` and the station has no owner-declared position, `SetDeclared(idx, {Lat, Lon, Alt}, model.AltRefAmbiguous)` (DESIGN Â§5.2: the survey resolves MSL vs HAE). Return `Attachment{Station, FeedID, Admission, Reason, EpochBase, NoTimestamps}` (NoTimestamps when the owner withheld timing). `Detach` is called exactly once per admitted attach.

## WP-B3-mlatc -> internal/mlat (WP-M, engine root): results, planner output, client stats
- Results: for each published fix, for each contributing station that is an mlatc station, `srv.SendResult(st, mlatc.Result{MLATFix: fix, Callsign, Squawk, NStations})` (or `srv.SendResults(st, fixes)`, same signature as avfsrv). Never to non-contributors (DESIGN Â§8.4).
- Planner: `*mlatc.Server` implements `plan.SendingController` (compile-checked in `internal/ingest/mlatc/plan_test.go`): put `plan.MLATCOut{C: srv}` into the planner's `Out` fan-out next to `plan.IngestOut`. In the ingest's full mode the server already requests every seen aircraft; the planner's start/stop override that per aircraft.
- `mlatc.StatsSource` from the clock filter: per station `peer_count` (synced peers), `outlier_percent`, `bad_sync_timeout` (s). Without it no `stats` messages are sent.

## WP-B3-mlatc -> internal/model + internal/config (integrator): file-configurable mlat-client tunables (Â§20.3)
Optional block `aggregator.mlatc` mapped onto `mlatc.Options` / `Policy` (zero = default): `splitSync` false, `maxRequested` 2000, `maxSeen` 10000, `maxBadLines` 200, `idleTimeoutSec` 150, `heartbeatSec` 30, `statsSec` 60, `overLimitSeconds` 5, `coolDownSec` 300, `maxClients` 4096, `maxOutQueueKB` 256. `mlatc.OptionsFromConfig` will map them once the type exists (today it maps port, limits, handshake timeout, `aggregator.mlat.enabled/returnResults` and the aggregator name as MOTD).

## WP-B3-mlatc -> docs/aggregator/DESIGN.md (integrator / design owner): Â§5.2 findings
- Timestamp convention fixed (from rule 4 facts; to be re-checked with a capture): the client reports start-of-frame values (12 MHz: âˆ’768/âˆ’244 ticks; GPS: ns of UTC day âˆ’64/âˆ’120/âˆ’20.3 Âµs; SBS: +1 120/+1 920 ticks, widened). The server adds the offsets back, so 31090 ticks equal AVF/1 ticks of the same receiver (`internal/ingest/mlatc/doc.go`).
- `split_sync`: measured on simnet (10 stations, 60 s, through the real ingest) paired sync delivers only 86 % of multi-station sync transmissions complete to the MLAT grouper (the first half of a pair arrives up to 5 s late), split sync 99.98 %; Mode S frames 99.8 % either way. Recommendation: reply `split_sync:true` (option `mlatc.Options.SplitSync`) once a capture confirms the reference client's ssync behaviour; until then the DESIGN default (false) is kept.

## WP-B3-mlatc -> internal/ingest (WP-I): late sync halves from paired mlat-clients (optional)
With paired sync the first half of a pair reaches the server up to 5 s after reception; after the grouping window it is only credited (and after `LateAfter` routed as late), so it never joins its transmission's MLAT group. If split sync cannot be used, consider joining DF17 position copies (unique payloads) into a retained group and delivering them through `LateObserver` for up to ~5 s of corrected time.

## WP-B3-mlatc -> internal/sim/simnet (WP-S): reference-client timestamp convention in the emitter (optional)
`MLATClientEmitter` sends AirView-convention ticks (the server needs `Options.RawTicks` for it). An option to emit the reference client's convention (start-of-frame offsets, GPS nanoseconds of the day, widened SBS) and `ssync` would let other packages test against realistic streams; `internal/ingest/mlatc/simnet_test.go` (`realClient`) shows the conversion.

## WP-B3-mlatc -> docs/aggregator/README.md (WP-DOC): link MLATC.md
Please add `[MLATC.md](MLATC.md)` (AirView's description of the mlat-client protocol on 31090) to the developer table, and mention in FEEDER-GUIDE that `privacy:false` in mlat-client means consent to the region pin and name (DESIGN Â§19.2).

## WP-K-clock -> internal/mlat (WP-M, engine root): wire the clock filter
`internal/mlat/clock` is ready (contract in `internal/mlat/clock/WIRING.md`, maths in `doc.go`). Please: create one `clock.Filter` (`OriginUnixNs` = server start truncated to the second; `EpochS` from `aggregator.mlat.epochMs`; `MaxComponentStations` from `aggregator.mlat.maxComponentStations`; `Atmosphere` from N0/H/k_atm; `OnBlacklist: refStore.Suspend`) and `go f.Run(ctx, now, time.Second)`; keep stations current with `f.SetStation(idx, clock.StationInfo{Class, Pos (ECEF, HAE), Located, NoStatus (mlatc radarcape_gps), Flagged (admin), JitterNs (mlat/quality)})`; pass `clock.View{F: f, Ref: refStore}` as `ingest.Options.Clock` and `f` as `ingest.Options.ClockObserver`; call `f.ObserveGroup(g, refStore)` from `MLATSink.Observe` for every group. Solver and grouper read `f.Snapshot()` (`Convert`, `NetTimeUnix`, `CrossCov`, `Domain`) â€” one time domain per cluster; set `FixFlagRelativeTime` from `StationClock.RelativeTime`. `ingest.ClockView.NetTime` returns ns since `OriginUnixNs` (a float64 Unix-ns value would resolve only 256 ns).

## WP-K-clock -> internal/mlat/ref (WP-RF): RefSource
Please implement `clock.RefSource` on the reference store: `RefPosition(key uint32, df uint8, tUnixNs int64) (pos geo.Vec3, cov [6]float64 /*xx,xy,xz,yy,yz,zz mÂ²*/, ok bool)` with the Â§9.2.2 rules (NACp â‰¥ 8, NIC â‰¥ 7, valid CRC, age â‰¤ 1 s propagated, not suspended) and `Usable(key uint32) bool` (cheap, for the grouper's SyncCandidate). `Store.Suspend` already matches `clock.Config.OnBlacklist`.

## WP-K-clock -> internal/ingest/mlatc (WP-B3) / engine: StatsSource
`mlatc.ClientStats` from the snapshot: `PeerCount = sc.SyncedPeers()`, `OutlierPercent = sc.OutlierPct`, `BadSyncTimeout = 0` (the filter re-syncs a stepped clock within seconds instead of excluding it; while unsynced `sc.Synced` is false). A two-line adapter in the engine (`clock` does not import `mlatc`).

## WP-K-clock -> internal/server (WP-H): owner Timing section
Map `clock.StationClock` into `timing{}`: `clockClass` = `Class.String()` (effective; `Declared` is what the station claimed), `anchor{state: AnchorState, reason: AnchorReason}`, `sigmaThetaNs` = `SigmaThetaNs`, `driftPpm` = `Phi/1000`, `betaShortNs` = `Beta`, `outlierPct` = `OutlierPct`, `events` = `Events` (`T` is network seconds since the snapshot's `OriginUnixNs`), `peers` = `Peers` (`Count` per 30 s, `RMSNs`, `RelPPB`; the peer identity must go through `reg.PeerTag`).

## WP-M-engine -> internal/engine, cmd/airview (WP-E): construct and run the MLAT engine
Contract and example: `internal/mlat/WIRING.md`. In aggregator mode with `aggregator.mlat.enabled`: `eng, _ := mlat.New(mlat.Options{Config: cfg.Aggregator.MLAT, Now: ntp.Now, Logger, Updates: ing, Results: []mlat.ResultSender{avfSrv, mlatcSrv}, PlanOut: plan.Fanout{plan.IngestOut{Ingest: ing}, plan.MLATCOut{C: mlatcSrv}}, OnPosition: notifyOwner})` before `ingest.New`, then `io.MLAT = eng`, `io.Clock = eng.ClockView()`, `io.ClockObserver = eng`; `go eng.Run(ctx)`. Registry â†’ `eng.SetStation(idx, mlat.StationInfo{Class, Declared/GNSS/Confirmed (ECEF, HAE), Suspect, DiscrepancyM, NoStatus, Flagged})` on every change and `eng.RemoveStation`. mlat-client server: `mlatc.Options.Stats = eng`, `Hooks.OnSeen = eng.NoteSeen`. This closes the carried requests WP-RF/WP-GR/WP-SV/WP-TR/WP-K/WP-B3 -> internal/mlat (the engine does their wiring internally).

## WP-M-engine -> internal/server (WP-H): MLAT data for owner / admin / public views
Read-only, safe from any goroutine: `eng.Station(sid)` (owner own / admin: position source, clock report incl. peers by raw station index â€” map through `reg.PeerTag`, MLAT report: contributions/h, used/excluded/outlier %, residual bias ns, learned jitter, bias flag; timed frames/min and result eligibility), `eng.PublicQuality()` (public: hold-out P50/P95/P99 of the last hour once â‰¥ 20 samples, updates/min, 1Â° cells with â‰¥ 5 contributing stations, no identities; answers the WP-W "mlat: {p50M, p95M, p99M, fixesPerMin}" request), `eng.QualityReport(windowMin, cells)`, `eng.SyncMatrix()`, `eng.Stats()`, `eng.CRLBMap(quality.CRLBParams{})` (admin; CPU-heavy, cache it). JSON tags are on every DTO.

## WP-M-engine -> internal/ingest (WP-I): the ClockView gate biases the clock filter; frame kind; late sync copies
Measured with the real ingest on simnet (4 GPS + 20 SDR, 14 min): with the engine's clock as `ingest.ClockView` the synced-station gate (`clockAdmit`, |Î”t_net| â‰¤ d/c + 5Ïƒ against the group's first synced copy) removes exactly the copies of a clock that has started to drift, so the filter never sees the evidence: 1,237-1,941 false clock steps vs 105 without the gate (published P95 112 m vs 84 m). Two defects feed it: `ClockView.NetTime` has no frame kind (Radarcape GPS stamps short frames 56 Âµs earlier than long ones relative to the preamble; Î²_short applies to short frames only), and `clock.View.NetTime`'s Ïƒ excludes the receiver jitter (400 ns for an RTL-SDR vs ~100 ns clock Ïƒ). The engine's `ClockView()` therefore answers "not synced" to `NetTime` and false to `SyncCandidate` (the single-copy flush made the other stations' copies arrive as a late delivery with only the new copies, useless for sync). Requests: (1) add the frame kind to `NetTime` and use the Ïƒ including jitter, or drop the gate for groups that go to MLAT; (2) when a group flushed with one copy later gets copies from other stations, deliver it once more with every copy (or put the earlier copies into the late delivery); (3) note: corrected times of SDR feeders run â‰ˆ 47 ms late (sample blocks), measured as transmit-instant error P50 +47 ms; the reference store gets Ï„ = FirstNs âˆ’ 1 ms Â± 5 ms as specified, but that Ïƒ is optimistic for SDR-only networks; (4) DESIGN Â§9.6 excludes hold-out aircraft from latency calibration: the station latency learner uses every shared position squitter, hold-out ones included (`mlat.Engine.HoldOut().In(key, now)` can tell).

## WP-M-engine -> internal/mlat/clock (WP-K): robustness of SDR-only networks
Reproduction: `MLAT_SEEDS="13 17" go test ./internal/mlat -run SeedSweep -v` (20 RTL-SDRs / 150 km, hold-out 10 %, 16 min, real ingest and reference store). 6 of 8 seeds are clean (0 steps); on seed 17 from minute 2 and on seed 13 from minute 11, 40-250 false steps per minute, ~90 aircraft blacklisted, published P95 0.3-2.9 km. Observed state: a few SDR stations with Ï† 30-50 ppm off the truth (relative to the network common mode) and |Ïˆ| 70-150 ppb/s, stepped every ~2 s. Requests: (1) after N same-sign steps within a minute also reset Ï† (and Ïˆ) â€” the engine's watchdog experiment (RemoveStation + SetStation) did not recover the component, the fix belongs in the filter; (2) in an anchor-less component the common mode of Î²_short is unobservable: short-frame sync observations made the instability far more frequent (seeds 16, 19 too), so the engine feeds short frames only in anchored components (Î²_short then stays unestimated in SDR-only networks, Â±200 ns short/long error on simnet SDRs) â€” please add a gauge (Î£Î² = 0, as for Î¸/Ï†) so short frames can be used everywhere; (3) start-up: a filter started on the first few dozen unconfirmed references could lock SDR clocks into a wrong solution; the engine holds sync back for 60 s (`Options.SyncWarmup`); (4) `View.NetTime` Ïƒ without jitter and without frame kind (see the WP-I entry).

## WP-M-engine -> internal/mlat/track (WP-TR): wrong 3+alt branches confirmed from minimal fixes
On simnet (8 GPS / 150 km) Mode S-only aircraft 300 km outside the network, heard by 3 stations, were created from ambiguous DOF-0 fixes and confirmed on the near (wrong) branch with Ïƒ_h 20-60 m; later a 4-station cluster consistent with the true position was applied to the wrong track (an outlier member was excluded instead of gating the cluster), and the track stayed stationary at FL350. The engine now publishes a track only after it has accepted Î£ DOF â‰¥ 2 from unambiguous solver fixes, uses unverified tracks neither as prior nor as hint, and restarts a confirmed track that two redundant fixes contradict (Ï‡Â²â‚ƒ 1e-4 and > 1 km). Suggestions for the tracker: do not confirm from DOF-0 fixes alone while `Ambiguous`; prefer gating a cluster over excluding a member when the track has never had redundant updates; a speed < 30 m/s at > FL150 is implausible for a non-rotorcraft.

## WP-M-engine -> internal/sim/simnet (WP-S): AircraftAt index out of range
`(*Network).AircraftAt` panics with "index out of range" in `acTruth` (truth.go:78): `stateAt` interpolates from one snapshot while `best` is chosen separately, and `best.truth[st.truth]` uses the truth index of the other snapshot. Reproduced with the 4 GPS + 20 SDR harness (seed 12) around t = 16 s. The MLAT harness recovers from it; please index `best.truth` with `best.ac[best.idx[icao]].truth` only.

## WP-M-engine -> internal/model + internal/config (integrator): engine tunables (Â§20.3)
Optional block `aggregator.mlat.engine` mapped onto `mlat.Options` (zero = default): `workers` (GOMAXPROCS, â‰¤ 64), `queueLen` (4096), `publishIntervalMs` (500), `maxSolveSites` (16, 4..64), `contribWindowSec` (60), `minTimedPerMin` (10), `holdoutRotateMin` (10), `latencyFeedback` (false), `jitterFeedback` (false), `applyKAtm` (false), `verifyEverySec` (5, âˆ’1 = off), `syncWarmupSec` (60, âˆ’1 = none). Until then the defaults apply; `holdout`, `autoUseSurvey`, `autoUseMaxSigmaM`, `returnResults`, `epochMs`, `maxComponentStations`, `modeAc` and the refractivity settings are already read from `aggregator.mlat`.

## Integrator (after Wave 2B): status of every open request

Build/vet/test: `go build ./...` is clean for windows/amd64, linux/amd64, linux/arm64 and linux/arm (GOARM=7); `gofmt -l` is empty; `go vet ./...` is clean. Wave 2B left no build break. The only failure in the first `go test -count=1 ./...` was `TestNetworkBudgets` (internal/tracker, a wall-clock budget), which failed while the 3-minute MLAT suites ran in parallel. It passes alone and in the final run.

The MLAT engine now closes these carried requests, because it does their wiring internally (`internal/mlat/WIRING.md`): WP-RF-ref, WP-GR, WP-SV-solve, WP-TR-track, WP-K-clock and WP-B3-mlatc (results, planner output, StatsSource) -> internal/mlat, and WP-K-clock -> mlatc/engine (StatsSource): **DONE** (WP-M, plus the integrator wiring below). WP-H-server -> internal/mlat (timing / MLAT data) and WP-I-ingest -> internal/mlat: **DONE** (see below).

- WP-M-engine -> internal/engine, cmd/airview (construct and run the MLAT engine): **DONE** (integrator, `internal/engine/mlatwire.go`).
  - **Construction.** With `aggregator.mlat.enabled` (default on), `attachNet` calls `mlat.New` before `ingest.New` and sets `io.MLAT`, `io.Clock = eng.ClockView()` and `io.ClockObserver`. `eng.Run(nc.ctx)` runs on the network wait group.
  - **Outputs.** Updates go to the ingest (`HandleUpdate`). Results go to whichever AVF/1 or mlat-client listener is running: the adapters look the listener up on every call, so listener restarts are safe. Planner deltas go to `plan.IngestOut` and `plan.MLATCOut`. If the ingest core cannot be built, the engine is closed.
  - **Station sync.** The registry has no change notification, so a loop maps every approved station every 5 s with `mlatStationInfo`:
    - GNSS position;
    - owner-confirmed effective position;
    - declared position, converted MSLâ†’HAE through the geoid; an `ambiguous` mlat-client height gets Ïƒ = 30 m + |N|;
    - suspect flag plus the survey discrepancy.

    It calls `SetStation` only when a station changed and `RemoveStation` for stations that are gone. At 1,000 stations this is one pass over the in-memory cache.
  - **Restart.** A change of any `aggregator.mlat` setting sets `RestartRequired`, because the engine is built only once.
  - **Clock-class fix (found in the live smoke run).** Some AVF stations sent their first RECEIVER record before their first frame and kept clock class `none` in the registry, because nothing ever called `SetClockClass`. The engine therefore never synced them. `onAVFReceiver` now stores the class from later RECEIVER records.
  - **Not wired:**
    - `SetSurvey` (phase 3);
    - `EnableModeAC` (phase 5);
    - `Weather` (no METAR source);
    - `OnPosition` is only logged: notifying the owner needs per-owner notices (phase 3, with the survey).
  - Tests: `TestMLATStationInfo`; `TestAggregatorMLAT` (real engine; an mlat-client handshake creates a station that reaches the engine as `PosDeclared`; the sync adds and removes stations).
- WP-B3-mlatc -> internal/engine, cmd/airview (start the 31090 listener): **DONE** (`syncMLATC`).
  - It runs only while the MLAT engine and the ingest core run, and follows ApplyConfig like the other listeners.
  - Wiring: `Gate` = admin ready, `Stats` = engine, `Hooks.OnSeen = eng.NoteSeen`, `ServerKey = reg.IngestKey()`.
  - Visible in `NetworkStatus().MLATC`, `MLATCStats` and the `listener.mlatc` health check. There is also a new health line, `mlat`.
- WP-B3-mlatc -> internal/registry (mlatc.Registry adapter): **DONE** (`registry.MLATC()`, `internal/registry/mlatc.go`).
  - Identity: the UUID or `Identity.Key()`; the name comes from `user`. It also carries the clock class and `PrivacyOff`, and writes the connection log and Seen.
  - The handshake position is stored as declared with `altRef ambiguous`, but only when the station has no declared position yet.
  - Test: `TestMLATCAdapter`.
- WP-H-server -> avfsrv, mlatc, cmd/airview (LinkSource, mlatc part): **DONE**. cmd/airview adds `engineMLATCLinks` (proto `mlatc`, with `synced` from the engine's clock report) to the link sources for Feed Check and claims.
- WP-M-engine -> internal/server (MLAT data for owner / admin / public): **DONE** (integrator, `internal/server/api_mlat.go`).
  - **Public:** `GET /api/public/mlat` returns `publicdto.MLATCoverage`:
    - hold-out P50/P95/P99 and fixes per minute over the last hour, once there are at least 20 samples;
    - 1Â° cells with at least 20 samples from at least 5 stations;
    - no station identity or station count.

    The `mlat` member of `/api/public/summary` is filled from the same source.
  - **Owner:** `GET /api/me/stations/{sid}/mlat` returns the position source, clock report, MLAT report, timed frames per minute and result eligibility. Peers appear as `registry.PeerTag` weekly tags, never as SIDs.
  - **Admin:**
    - `GET /api/admin/mlat`: engine statistics;
    - `/api/admin/mlat/quality?window=&cells=1`;
    - `/api/admin/mlat/sync`: peers by SID;
    - `/api/admin/mlat/crlb`: cached for 10 minutes; `refresh=1` recomputes it;
    - `/api/admin/stations/{sid}/mlat`.
  - Without a running engine, every MLAT route answers `running:false` with status 200.
  - Tests: route-lint entries, the golden owner crawl, and `TestMLATRoutes` (fixture plus a real aggregator engine). docs/API.md and the server WIRING.md are regenerated.
  - Not done: the web UI pages for these routes (WP-W).
- WP-M-engine -> internal/ingest (gate, frame kind, late sync copies, SDR bias, hold-out):
  - **(4) Hold-out excluded from latency learning: DONE.**
    - New option `ingest.Options.LearnExclude(addr, nowNs)`. The sweeper skips unique-payload groups of excluded aircraft before they reach the station latency estimator.
    - The engine passes `eng.HoldOut().In`.
    - Test: `TestLearnExcludeHoldout`.
  - **(1) and (2): REJECTED for this wave.** (1) asks for the frame kind and the jitter in `ClockView.NetTime`, or for dropping the gate. (2) asks to re-deliver a single-copy flush with every copy.
    - Nothing is lost today: the engine's `ClockView` already answers "not synced" and `SyncCandidate = false`, so the gate is inactive.
