Addendum (integrator, found by the Wave 2B live smoke run):

- internal/tracker: **FIXED** (integrator). `HandleUpdate` offered decoded positions as *relative* fixes. An aircraft already heard through Mode S frames (`nConfirmed > 0`, which is every Mode S-only aircraft in a network) therefore rejected every MLAT result: the engine published about 3,000 fixes per minute and the map showed none. Decoded positions (SBS/JSON inputs, MLAT results) are now independent fixes, so two agreeing results start a track, like a global CPR decode. Regression test: `TestMLATStartsModeSOnlyTrack`, which fails without the fix.
- Open, for WP-M + WP-TR. With 12 simulated AVF feeders (mostly RTL-SDR) over 150 km:
  - the hold-out **fixes** had P95 188 m;
  - the hold-out **tracks** had P95 4.5 km, mostly along-track (cross-track P95 410 m).

  A few engine tracks run away from their aircraft. The Â§13.2 simnet gates do not show this. It needs a reproduction with `airview-netsim` (seed 7, 12 AVF + 3 Beast, 250 aircraft).
- Open, for WP-M + WP-R. With `admission: open`, 8 synthetic mlat-client stations from `mlatc-load` (random timing) were admitted at once. While they were connected, the published hold-out P95 rose from about 110 m to about 4.5 km. The cause is not separated from the previous item. Phase-4 trust/quarantine (DESIGN Â§9.6) should keep new stations out of the solver geometry until their residuals are clean.

## WP-E2-engine -> internal/mlat (WP-M, engine root): exact per-station fix and outlier totals
The engine now persists `mlat_minute` (internal/engine/mlatmetrics.go). Frames and groups per station are counted exactly by a tap in front of the engine; fixes and outliers per station can only be recovered from `quality.StationReport` (hourly ring of ContribPerHour and percentages), which gives a zero-mean random-walk error (derivation in the internal/engine package documentation, "MLAT metrics") and loses the outliers of minutes with excluded rows only. Please add `func (e *Engine) StationTotals(st model.StationIdx) (fixes, outliers uint64, ok bool)`: cumulative counts since the engine started of (a) accepted solver fixes in which the station had a used row and (b) its rows excluded by RAIM or with |w| > 4 (the same rule as `quality.Stations`). The engine picks it up by interface assertion (`mlatStationTotals`) with no further change.

## WP-E2-engine -> internal/server (WP-H): MLAT history beyond the RAM rings
`mlat_minute` (airview.db; per station sid and the network row sid ""), `station_clock_minute` (stations.db) and `mlat_quality_hour` (airview.db, hold-out error histograms per hour Ã— 1Â° cell Ã— altitude band) are now written every minute in aggregator mode and pruned by the tiered retention. Read with `store.MLATMinutes(ctx, sid, from, to)`, `StationClockMinutes`, `MLATQualityHours(ctx, from, to, cell)` (`eng.Store()`, `eng.StationsDB()`). The admin console and the owner Timing section could show days/weeks from these tables instead of the 24 h / 2 h RAM rings of `mlat_history.go`; `mlat_quality_hour` has the hold-out percentiles per cell for the error map history. Public scope: only the network row; `mlat_quality_hour` has no station identities but also no contributing-station count, so the Â§8.2 "â‰¥ 5 stations" cell rule needs the live `PublicQuality` cells as the filter.

## WP-W2-web -> internal/ingest/avfsrv (WP-P / WP-B2 owner) + cmd/airview (WP-E): MLAT results per station link
The owner Timing & MLAT section shows "results received" from `server.LiveLink.Results` (*uint64, new; nil = not reported, the UI then shows the eligibility only). Please (1) count MLAT fixes per connection in `avfsrv` (`SendResults` already adds to the global `st.results`; add a per-conn counter) and set `LiveLink.Results` in `avfsrv`'s `LiveLinks()`; (2) in `cmd/airview` `engineMLATCLinks`, copy `mlatc.LinkStats.Results` into `LiveLink.Results`. Test: `TestMLATConsoleRoutes` (fixture links with `Results`).

## WP-W2-web -> integrator: regenerate docs/API.md
New routes (listed in internal/server/WIRING.md until then): `GET /api/admin/mlat/history`, `GET /api/admin/mlat/stations`, `GET /api/admin/mlat/longterm`, `GET /api/me/stations/{sid}/mlat/longterm`; new notes on `/api/admin/mlat/sync`, `/api/admin/mlat/crlb` and `/api/me/stations/{sid}/mlat` (distance band, summary, history, event times in Unix ms). `$env:AIRVIEW_GEN_API_DOC="1"; go test ./internal/server -run TestGenerateAPIDoc`.

## WP-W2-web -> internal/mlat/clock (WP-K): `Stats.LastEpochNs` reads 0 on Windows
On the live netsim run (8 synced stations, 490 epochs) `/api/admin/mlat` reported `clock.LastEpochNs: 0` in every sample, so the console's "Clock epoch" tile and chart show 0.0 ms. Probably `time.Since` of a sub-resolution step or the field is overwritten by an empty epoch; please measure the full `Step` (or keep the maximum / EWMA over the last minute) so the Â§13.2 epoch budget is visible live.

## WP-W2-web -> internal/mlat (WP-M): per-station peers are one-directional
`ClockReport.Peers` lists only the peers a station's residuals were taken against. A station that mostly serves as the reference (e.g. the GPS station rx12 on netsim seed 7) reports no peers and `SyncedPeers: 0` while 7 stations sync against it. The server now mirrors `SyncMatrix()` edges for the owner view (`mergedPeers`, api_mlat.go); an engine-side symmetric peer list (and a `SyncedPeers` that counts both directions) would make `mlatc` "stats" (`ClientStats.PeerCount`) right too.

## WP-E2-engine -> internal/ingest/mlatc (WP-B3) + internal/mlat (WP-M): mlat-client stations degrade the hold-out and the tail
Measured with `internal/engine/simfeed` (reference-client timestamp convention, paired `sync`, `mlat` only for requested aircraft) through the real listeners (`TestMLATEndToEnd`, same simnet world, 7 RTL-SDR timed stations + 1 standalone GPS station, 2 min): with the 3 middle stations on 31090 instead of AVF/1 the map error vs truth was P50 97 m vs 100 m (no timestamp bias) but P95 3,160 m vs 881 m, clusters 36.7k vs 67.4k, accepted solves 4.3k vs 7.8k (`MLAT_E2E_SDR=1 [MLAT_E2E_MLATC_AS_AVF=1] go test ./internal/engine -run TestMLATEndToEnd -v`). Live (port 18621, `airview-netsim` seed 7, 12 AVF + 4 Beast + 8 mlat-clients, community device mix, 300 aircraft) the public hold-out P50 was 0.9-2.3 km; without the mlat-clients 61 m. Likely cause: DF17 positions of hold-out aircraft reach the server only as delayed even/odd `sync` pairs, so hold-out clusters lack the mlat-client copies or get them late. Requests: (1) confirm `split_sync` against a capture and default it on (simfeed would need `ssync`); (2) consider treating `sync` halves of hold-out aircraft as late copies (`ObserveLate`) up to ~5 s, as already suggested in WP-B3 -> internal/ingest.

## Integrator (after Wave 2C): status of every open request

Build/vet/test: `go build ./...` is clean for windows/amd64, linux/amd64, linux/arm64 and linux/arm (GOARM=7); `gofmt -l` is empty; `go vet ./...` is clean. Wave 2C left no build break. The only failure in the first `go test -count=1 ./...` was again `TestNetworkBudgets` (internal/tracker, a wall-clock budget) while the long MLAT suites ran in parallel; see PROGRESS.md for the final run. A stray `clock.test.exe` (a `go test -c` output of internal/mlat/clock) in the repository root was moved out to the integrator's scratch directory.

- WP-E2-engine -> internal/mlat (exact per-station fix and outlier totals): **DONE** (integrator).
  - `quality.Stations` keeps cumulative per-station counters next to its hourly ring: used rows (fixes the station contributed to) and outlier rows (RAIM-excluded or |w| > 4, the rule of the hourly report). They live in their own map that `Forget` does not prune, so they stay monotonic. `quality.Stations.Totals(st)`.
  - `mlat.Engine.StationTotals(st) (fixes, outliers uint64, ok bool)` forwards them. internal/engine picks it up through `mlatStationTotals` (now asserted at compile time, `var _ mlatStationTotals = (*mlat.Engine)(nil)`), so `mlat_minute` per-station fixes and outliers are exact; the hour-ring recovery remains only for sources without the method (unit-test fakes).
  - Hot path: one map lookup per residual, no allocation after a station's first fix (`TestHotPathAllocs` must stay at 0 allocs per group; see PROGRESS.md).
  - Test: `TestStationTotals` (quality; exact counts, monotonic across `Forget`).
- WP-E2-engine -> internal/server (MLAT history beyond the RAM rings): **DONE** by WP-W2 in the same wave: `/api/admin/mlat/longterm` and `/api/me/stations/{sid}/mlat/longterm` read `mlat_minute` and `mlat_quality_hour` (hourly points, up to 90 days; `api_mlat_longterm.go`). `station_clock_minute` is not read by a route yet: its per-station clock columns duplicate the ÏƒÎ¸ / drift / residual RMS that `mlat_minute` already serves to the owner long-term view; a dedicated clock-history chart can use it later.
- WP-W2-web -> internal/ingest/avfsrv + cmd/airview (MLAT results per station link): **DONE** (integrator).
  - avfsrv counts the fixes `SendResults` delivers per receiver of a session (`rxState.results`, under the connection lock; one short loop over the session's receivers per results batch) and reports them as `SessionStation.Results` (`json:"results"`).
  - cmd/airview: `engineAVFLinks` sets `LiveLink.Results` from `SessionStation.Results`; `engineMLATCLinks` copies `mlatc.LinkStats.Results`. The owner Timing & MLAT "results received" figure is therefore live for both protocols.
  - Test: the AVF session test (`internal/ingest/avfsrv`) checks the per-station count after `SendResults`.
- WP-W2-web -> integrator (regenerate docs/API.md): **DONE**. `AIRVIEW_GEN_API_DOC=1 go test ./internal/server -run TestGenerateAPIDoc`; the four new routes (`/api/admin/mlat/history`, `/stations`, `/longterm`, `/api/me/stations/{sid}/mlat/longterm`) are now in docs/API.md.
- WP-W2-web -> internal/mlat/clock (`Stats.LastEpochNs` reads 0 on Windows): **DONE** (integrator).
  - Cause: Go's monotonic clock on Windows ticks in steps of about 0.5 ms on this machine (measured: 99.95 % of 100,000 timings of a ~2 Âµs loop read exactly 0; the smallest non-zero reading was 414 Âµs). A clock epoch at 8 stations costs well under one tick, so most single readings are 0.
  - Fix: `clock.Stats.MeanEpochNs`, an EWMA over about 32 epochs of the per-`Step` wall time. The mean of the quantised readings is unbiased because the epoch start is not phase-locked to the tick. `LastEpochNs` is unchanged.
  - The server history (`epochMs`) and the admin console tile use `MeanEpochNs` (falling back to `LastEpochNs`); the tile's tooltip now says "mean wall time of recent epochs".
  - Test: `TestMeanEpochNs` (clock).
- WP-W2-web -> internal/mlat (per-station peers are one-directional): **DONE** (integrator, `internal/mlat/peers.go`).
  - The engine builds a symmetric peer index once per clock snapshot (O(stations Ã— peers), shared through an atomic pointer): each edge Aâ†’B is mirrored as Bâ†’A with the relative frequency negated; a pair seen in both directions keeps the direction with more co-observations (the same rule as the server's `mergedPeers`, which now merges an already-symmetric list and is kept as a harmless second pass).
  - `Station(st).Clock.Peers` / `SyncedPeers` and `ClientStats(st).PeerCount` (mlat-client "stats") use it, so a reference station now reports the stations syncing against it.
  - Test: `TestSymmetricPeers` (simnet, 3 GPS + 5 SDR stations, every sync-matrix edge present at both ends; `PeerCount` = `SyncedPeers` â‰¥ the one-directional count).
- WP-E2-engine -> internal/ingest/mlatc (WP-B3) + internal/mlat (WP-M) (mlat-client stations degrade the hold-out and the tail): **REJECTED for this wave**, carried to WP-B3 + WP-I + WP-M.
  - (1) Defaulting `split_sync` on needs confirmation against a real mlat-client capture, which this machine does not have, and simfeed cannot exercise it (no `ssync`). Operators can already turn it on (`aggregator.mlatc.splitSync`). Changing a wire-level default on an unconfirmed reading of the protocol is the wrong order.
  - (2) Treating `sync` halves of hold-out aircraft as late copies up to ~5 s changes the ingest â†” MLAT late-delivery contract and the grouper's memory per aircraft at 10k aircraft; it needs its own truth runs (the `MLAT_E2E_SDR=1 [MLAT_E2E_MLATC_AS_AVF=1]` comparison in `TestMLATEndToEnd` is the acceptance measure). It joins the already-carried WP-B3 -> internal/ingest "late sync halves" item.
  - The degradation is confined to stations on the mlat-client protocol; AVF/1 and Beast networks are unaffected.

Still open from earlier waves (unchanged, carried): WP-I + WP-K gate/`NetTime` items (1)/(2)/(4), WP-K Î²_short gauge and SDR-only robustness, WP-TR 3+alt confirmation, the run-away hold-out tracks (WP-M + WP-TR), trust/quarantine of new stations (phase 4), C2-C8 consent texts (WP-W2/WP-FB/WP-WF).

## WP-GATES -> internal/mlat/ref (WP-RF): GNSSâˆ’baro field fallback at flight levels without learned cells
Found by `TestMLATResults` (docs/aggregator/MLAT-RESULTS.md Â§1). Two Mode S-only aircraft at FL320/FL350 in Â§13.2 row 1 are published 364 m and 377 m too high for their whole track. `Field.Offset` returns `fallback` (geoid only, +44 m, Ïƒ 150 m), because no ADS-B aircraft in that 2000 ft band reported GNSS height and `ref.Weather` is nil, while the truth is GNSSâˆ’baro â‰ˆ âˆ’340 m. Request: when a cell is empty, derive the offset from learned cells of neighbouring altitude bands in the same (or adjacent) column. For example, fit the ISA temperature-deviation term to the learned cells, since GNSSâˆ’baro grows about linearly with pressure height for a given temperature error. Also wire a surface-weather source. Acceptance: row 1 vertical P95 < 50 m (now 75 m) and mix100 vertical P95 < 50 m (now 275 m) in `MLAT_RESULTS=row1,mix100`.

## WP-GATES -> internal/mlat/clock (WP-K): SDR-only robustness and clock Ïƒ calibration (measured)
`MLAT_RESULTS=row3`: 336 detected steps in 16 minutes, only 65.6 % of clock samples within 3Ïƒ, |relative error| P95 1.15 Âµs. Anchored mixes: 84-94 % within 3Ïƒ (expected 99.7 %); 300 stations: 83.8 %. `TestEpochCostGate` at 200 stations: 71.8 ms idle, 92.8 ms while other simulations loaded the machine (gate 80 ms).

## WP-GATES -> internal/mlat (WP-M) + internal/mlat/track (WP-TR): optimistic published Ïƒ, and a tail outside the hull
Published 1Ïƒ coverage: row 3 21 %, harsh20 45 %, mix300 55 % (target 60-75 %). Outside the network radius, published P99 is 0.4-2.2 km, and in harsh20 3.7 % of all published positions are more than 1 km off (0.28 % inside). Consider inflating Ïƒ from the learned residual scale and/or withholding grade C outside the receiver hull (GDOP gate).

## H-platform -> internal/mlat (WP-M, engine root): map the new `aggregator.mlat.clock` block onto clock.Config
`model.AggMLAT.Clock` (`model.AggMLATClock`, zero = default; checked by `Aggregator.Normalize`, documented in docs/CONFIG.md, env `AIRVIEW_AGGREGATOR_MLAT_CLOCK_*`) reaches `mlat.New` inside `Options.Config`, but `engine.go` builds `clock.Config` from `EpochMs` / `MaxComponentStations` only. Please copy the block into the `clock.Config` literal in `mlat.New` (every field has the same name and unit as in clock.Config, and clock's own `withDefaults` already treats 0 as "default"): `HostSigmaS`, `PPSCorrelatedNs` (negative = off, as in clock), `ResetThetaSigmaNs`, `MaxMembers`, `SyncRatePerStation`, `MaxObsAgeS`, `StationTimeoutS`, `CoObsWindowS`, `ProbationS`, `AnchorFreqMaxPPB`, `AnchorOffsetMaxNs`, `AnchorDriftMaxNsPerH`, `AnchorHostMaxS`, `GateProb`, `WTestMax`, `StepSigma`, `StepCount`, `StepRunCount`, `StepRunWindowS`, `BlacklistS`, `BlacklistMaxFrac`, `StationBadOutlier`, `SyncedMinObs`, `SyncedWindowS`, `Workers`, `NoJitterLearning`. A mapper `clock.ConfigFromModel(m model.AggMLATClock, base clock.Config) clock.Config` next to the other `*FromConfig` mappers would keep it testable (`TestConfigFromModel`: a non-zero value of every field arrives). Until then the block is accepted, checked and documented but has no effect (docs/CONFIG.md says "zero = default", so the defaults are what runs). Note for the clock owner: clock's `withDefaults` turns `StepRunCount <= 0` into 3, so the documented "StepRunCount < 0 = off" of `clock.Config` cannot be reached; the model block therefore offers only positive values.

## H-clock -> internal/mlat (engine root): switch on what H-clock built
The clock filter, the ingest and the reference store now provide what the engine switched off or never fed (H-clock section in PROGRESS.md; measured numbers there). Requests, each with its acceptance run:
1. **Gate view.** `eng.ClockView()` may return `clock.GateView{F, Ref}` (frame kind + Ïƒ with receiver jitter, `ingest.ClockKindView`) instead of the never-synced `ingestView`; the ingest gate now has a 50 Âµs floor (`ingest.Options.ClockGateFloor`), so it can no longer remove the copies of a drifting clock. `SyncCandidate` can answer for real: single-copy flushes are re-delivered with every copy (`ingest.Group.Redelivery`; skip `refs.Observe` / counters for those). Acceptance: `MLAT_SEEDS="12 13 14 15 16 17 18 19" MLAT_MIN=30` stays at â‰¤ 1 step per seed and the Â§13.2 gates pass.
2. **Adaptive flush.** Construct `group.NewFlushEstimator()` once and pass it as `ingest.Options.Flush` (the ingest feeds it delays and hearers itself). Acceptance: the result latency P95 drops without losing groups (`Stats.LateMLAT` / clusters per minute unchanged).
3. **Short frames everywhere.** Relative components now carry a Î²_short gauge, so short-frame sync observations can also go to anchor-less components (engine: "anchored components only"). Acceptance: the same seed sweep with short frames everywhere.
4. **Mode S altitude replies.** Pass DF0/4/16/20 messages of every group to `refs.Observe(&m, tau, sig)` too (today only DF17/18 reach it): they feed the reference vertical channel and the TC 20-22 GNSSâˆ’baro pairing.
5. **Weather.** `ref.NewBundleWeather()` as `Options.Weather` (or `refs.Field().SetWeather`), fed from `weather.Poller.OnUpdate` and, for station areas away from the host, `weather.Client.Forecast` per 1Â° cell with stations (rate-limited, only when the operator has weather enabled). Acceptance: `MLAT_RESULTS=row1,mix100` vertical P95 (open WP-GATES request).

## H-clock -> internal/weather: METAR
The task asked for a METAR/QNH source; `internal/weather` has none (Open-Meteo forecasts/analyses and RainViewer only). `ref.BundleWeather` therefore uses Open-Meteo `pressure_msl` as QNH plus the pressure-level profile, which the field's fallback needs anyway. If a METAR client is added later (aviation QNH and temperature per airport, hourly), it should expose a `SurfaceAt(lat, lon, t)`-style lookup so it can implement `ref.Weather` directly.

Optional keys, zero = default: `aggregator.ingest.clockGateFloorUs` (50; âˆ’1 = no floor), `aggregator.ingest.flushSampleEvery` (4); `aggregator.mlat.clock.reoriginS` (86400), `relGaugeBetaSigmaNs` (10; âˆ’1 = off), `externalInflate` (2); `aggregator.mlat.ref.altReplyMinS` (2), `pairBaroS` (1.5), `noAltReplies` (false), `field.profileSigmaM` (25), `field.profileSigmaTK` (1.5). They map onto `ingest.Options`, `clock.Config` and `ref.Options` / `ref.FieldOptions` fields of the same names.

## H-mlatc -> internal/engine, cmd/airview (WP-E): wire the timing quarantine, rate_report and feeder positions
1. **Timing quarantine** (`internal/registry/trust`, WIRING.md there): `gate := trust.New(eng, trust.RegistrySource{R: reg}, trust.ConfigFromModel(cfg.Aggregator.MLAT), trust.RegistryPromoter(reg, log))`; give the ingest `MLAT: gate` instead of the engine (the gate forwards `Observe`/`ObserveLate`, 22 ns per group); `mlatcOpts.Trust = gate`; call `gate.Refresh()` after `reg.SetTiming`. Until this is wired, new stations carry `FlagTimingQuarantine` with no effect. Measured (simnet, `TestQuarantineHostileStations`): three hostile stations next to fifteen honest ones raise hold-out P95 from 37 m to 166 m with open admission; with the gate 26-33 m, the three new honest stations are proven after â‰ˆ 3 min, the hostile ones never.
2. **rate_report**: `Hooks.OnRates` (or `srv.ADSBRate(st, addr)`) into the planner's sync-set frame-rate estimate (see the WP-GR item below).
3. **Feeder positions (PRIV-1)**: replace the `reg.Update` in `applyReceiverLocation` (internal/engine/avflisten.go) by `reg.FeederPosition(ctx, st, registry.FeederPos{Lat, Lon, Alt, AltRef, GNSS: loc.Source == model.LocSrcGNSS, SigmaM, SetEffective: true})` â€” same semantics, plus the rate limit and the hold of large moves pending a survey.
4. `mlatStationInfo` (mlatwire.go): an mlat-client position with `AltRefAmbiguous` currently gets Ïƒ = 30 m + |N| for all three axes (â‰ˆ 77 m at 50Â° N 9Â° E). The ambiguity is vertical only and two-valued: the midpoint `alt + N/2` with Ïƒ_v = |N|/2 is the better estimate (Ïƒ â‰ˆ 39 m), or a vertical-only Ïƒ if `mlat.Position` gains one (WP-M). Not shown to matter in mlatcsim (Ïƒ 30 vs 77 m: no hold-out change), noted for correctness.

## H-mlatc -> internal/server (WP-H): band reference, quarantine and pending-position views
- `bandref.go`: replace the in-memory `bandRefTable` by `reg.FrozenBandRef(ctx, st.SID, registry.TagPeriod(now), lat, lon)` (persisted; a restart no longer refreezes â€” PRIV-1 remainder).
- Owner station view: `gate.Status(idx)` (timing quarantine progress: state, since, windows good/bad, refs, reason; no peer identities); `reg.PendingPosition(ctx, idx)` and a confirm action `reg.ConfirmPendingPosition(ctx, actor, idx)` ("your station announced a new position; it is applied when the survey confirms it, or confirm it here").
- Admin: `reg.Quarantined()`, `gate.Stats()`, `reg.SetTiming(ctx, actor, idx, "trusted"|"excluded"|"quarantine"|"auto")` (then `gate.Refresh()`); station DTO field `timing` (`Station.Timing()`) and `timingOverride`.
- Link views: `mlatc.LinkStats` has `SplitSync`, `SSyncs`, `ADSBAircraft`, `ADSBRate`; `avfsrv.SessionInfo` has `Results` (connection total) and `HostClampMs`; `avfsrv.Stats.HostClamped`, `mlatc.Stats.SSyncs` / `SplitFallbacks`.

## H-mlatc -> docs/aggregator/DESIGN.md Â§5.2 + MLATC.md (integrator / design owner): split_sync default
Change "`split_sync:false`" to: offered by default (`SplitSyncAuto`), with a per-identity fallback to pairs when a client's single syncs are unusable; MLATC.md Â§2.2 table and Â§8 accordingly. Evidence (PROGRESS.md, H-mlatc): 99.98 % vs 86 % complete multi-station sync groups (WP-B3 measurement), half the sync traffic for clients that pair overlapping messages (672 k ssync frames vs 666 k pairs = 1.33 M frames, half of them repeats), hold-out unchanged within noise on 2 worlds Ã— 3 seeds; a client that ignores the flag keeps working (measured). ## H-mlatc -> internal/mlat/plan (WP-GR): use rate_report for sync-set rates
`mlatc.Server.ADSBRate(st, addr) (msgs/s, ok)` / `ADSBRates(st, dst)` and `Hooks.OnRates` carry each mlat-client's ADS-B position rate per aircraft (PROGRESS: "rate_report values are not used yet"). For mlatc stations the group rate is only known once the aircraft is requested; the reported rate is available before, so the sync-set selection can rank candidates for mlatc stations without waiting for groups.

## H-mlatc -> internal/mlat (WP-M) + internal/mlat/track (WP-TR): a wrong 3+alt branch published for an ADS-B aircraft
`MLATCSIM_VERBOSE=1 MLATCSIM_WORLD=sdr MLATCSIM_ONLY=auto go test ./internal/ingest/mlatc/mlatcsim -run TestMixedHoldout -v`: with three RTL-SDR stations on 31090, aircraft 44d6ed (has ADS-B, not hold-out) is published for â‰¥ 15 s as a tracked relative-time 3+alt fix (flags 0b111, grade C, 3 stations) 36 km from the truth; published P99 31-34 km with mlat-clients vs 0.3 km all-AVF in that world (hold-out unaffected). Likely the known wrong-branch issue (WP-TR 3+alt confirmation) on an aircraft whose ADS-B positions reach the engine only through the mlat-clients' sync messages.

## H-mlatc -> internal/engine/simfeed (WP-E): honour split_sync in MLATClient
`MLATClient` ignores the handshake reply's `split_sync` and always pairs (disjoint pairs). Please send `{"ssync":{"t":T,"m":"hex"}}` per DF17 position when the reply has `split_sync:true` (the server now offers it by default), so the engine end-to-end tests exercise the default path; `internal/ingest/mlatc/mlatcsim` shows the emulation (pairs disjoint/overlapping and ssync). Its first "seen" report comes after 15 s, which delays every request of a 2-minute test by 15-16 s.

## H-track -> internal/mlat/geo (WP-G): RangeGrad cost
`geo.Atmosphere.RangeGrad` costs ~0.4 Âµs per station (8-point quadrature Ã— 3 Newton passes = 32 `math.Exp`, 8 `GeocentricHeight`, a geoid lookup with two `Atan2` per call). It was 40 % of a 6-station solve; the solver now linearises it about once per solve (coarse straight-path phase, solve/doc.go "Cost") and meets the 20 Âµs budget (15.9 Âµs), but the tracker's per-station refresh, the clock filter's `PropagationNs` and `quality.VerifyTDOA` still pay it. Suggestions: (1) the exponentials of iteration 3 can be derived from those of iteration 2 by one `math.Exp` of the (A, B) change per node, or the third pass dropped when |Î”A| is below a tolerance; (2) the undulation is constant over a solve/cluster: accept it as a parameter (or cache the last chord midpoint cell); (3) heights along the chord: `GeocentricHeight` per node could be replaced by a quadratic in u fitted from the end points and the midpoint. Acceptance: `BenchmarkRangeGrad` (new) â‰¤ 0.15 Âµs with `raytrace_test.go` unchanged.

## H-track -> internal/server + web (WP-W2/WP-H): honest hold-out figures and new counters
`mlat.PublicQuality` has new fields `crossP50M`, `crossP95M`, `estP50M`, `estP95M`: `est*` is the horizontal error estimated from the cross-track errors alone (`mlat.EstimateFromCross`, free of the ADS-B reference's airframe latency that inflates `p50M`/`p95M` by tens of metres). Please show `estP50M`/`estP95M` as the headline public figure (with a one-line note that `p50M`/`p95M` are against the latency-afflicted ADS-B reference), and `Quality.FixEst`/`TrackEst` next to Fix/Track in the admin console. `mlat.Stats` gained `restarts` and `gateSolves` (tracks restarted on contradiction or gate failures; clusters solved only to check a gated track). Regenerate docs/API.md.

## H-track -> internal/engine (WP-E): owner notification and the phase-3 survey seam
`mlat.Options.Notifier` (`mlat.PositionNotifier`) receives every change of a station's effective MLAT position source as `mlat.PositionChange{Station, Old, New, ECEF, SigmaM, AutoSurvey, Reason}`; please turn `AutoSurvey` changes into an owner notice (DESIGN Â§19.3; the position is owner/admin data only) instead of only logging `OnPosition`. The owner's "do not use the survey" action maps to `eng.OverrideSurvey(idx, true)` (standing until `OverrideSurvey(idx, false)`); `internal/mlat/survey` (phase 3) should depend on the interface `mlat.SurveySink`, which `*mlat.Engine` implements. `eng.SurveyOf(idx)` returns the last survey for the owner view.

The 150 ns target needs fewer quadrature nodes or a closed form: carried to WP-G.

The admin console shows `trackEst` in the accuracy tile, an "Estimated P50 / P95" column and track restarts / gate solves. docs/API.md regenerated.

Found in the live smoke run and fixed: `simnet.BeastEmitter` wrote Radarcape status frames, stamped with a rounded UTC second, at the simulation's integer seconds. When a run did not start on a whole second they were up to 0.5 s behind the surrounding Mode S frames, so a feeder on `airview-netsim -host-listen` saw a false "backwards" clock step every second. Status frames are now written at the instant the clock reads the whole second.

## Integrator -> internal/mlat (WP-M): no hold-out samples when stations feed the engine from its start
Live, the `airview` binary with `airview-netsim -seed 7` (12 AVF + 3 Beast + 4 mlat-clients): when the stations' copies reach the engine from its first second (a restart of an aggregator whose stations are already proven, or `aggregator.mlat.quarantine.disabled=true`), `Stats.HoldoutFixes` and `HoldoutTracks` stayed 0 for 5-10 minutes in four runs while fixes were published (â‰ˆ 210 MLAT tracks for â‰ˆ 240 aircraft) and results were delivered, so the public accuracy card stays empty. A fresh start, where the timing quarantine holds every copy back for its first 3 minutes, scored 270 fix and 759 track samples within 4 minutes. Ruled out: the Mode S altitude replies (`aggregator.mlat.ref.noAltReplies=true`: still 0) and the sync warm-up (20 s and the default 60 s: both 0). Suspects: hold-out aircraft that already have a non-hold-out MLAT track (the track count is â‰ˆ 210 against â‰ˆ 150 in the working run), or reference tracks never reaching `FlagConfirmed` without the quiet start (`worker.refState`). Reproduction: MLAT-RESULTS.md Â§0c; `TestMLATEndToEnd` does not check hold-out counts and should once this is fixed.

In `startFeeder`/the service body call `selfupdate.Attach(...)` before the listeners open (exit non-zero at once on `ErrRolledBack`; any other error is logged, never fatal) and `att.Run(ctx, selfCheck)` in a goroutine (exit non-zero on `ErrRolledBack` / `ErrExitRequested`). Same as the feeder with `Product: "airview"`, `Service: "airview"`, `HealthURL: "http://127.0.0.1:<port>/healthz"` (200; `version`/`uptimeS` are used). `update.enabled` stays false (opt-in). ## WP-UP-selfupdate -> internal/server (WP-H): `/feeder/*` release routes (answers "WP-H-server -> WP-PI-installer / WP-UP")
No engine accessor is needed: mount `http.StripPrefix("/feeder", selfupdate.ReleaseHandler{Dir: releaseDir})` for `GET|HEAD /feeder/manifest.json`, `/feeder/manifest.json.sig`, `/feeder/dl/<target>` and the same below `/feeder/beta/` (AccessPublic, not rate-limited; the handler sets `Cache-Control: no-cache`, `nosniff`, supports Range, serves nothing else, and 404s a manifest without its `.sig`). Suggested setting `aggregator.releaseDir` (default `<data>/releases/feeder`), filled by the operator with `deploy/release publish ... -out` (the server never holds the private key). `PRESET_MANIFEST_KEY` for install.sh = the first of the aggregator's `update.keys` (or a dedicated `aggregator.releaseKey`). `/feeder/install.sh` itself stays with WP-PI/WP-H.

## WP-UP-selfupdate -> deploy/feeder/pi (WP-PI): contract kept; let the timer use the health-checked update
The manifest layout is unchanged from the WP-PI contract (`TestManifestInstallerContract`, `deploy/release TestInstallerCompat` run install.sh's own `ed25519_verify`/`manifest_field` against published releases). File URLs are relative by default (`"url": "dl/linux-arm64"`), which install.sh already resolves to `$URL/feeder/dl/...`. Please write `update.manifestUrl` (`$URL/feeder/manifest.json`) and `update.keys` (`[$MKEY]`) into feeder.json, and once `airview-feeder update` is wired let `update.sh --quiet` (the timer) run `airview-feeder update -service airview-feeder` first (exit 0/10 = done/available, 4 = rolled back, 3 = not configured -> fall back to `install.sh --update`); that adds the 60 s health check and automatic rollback.

## WP-UP-selfupdate -> deploy/feeder/windows (WP-WF): updates via a SYSTEM scheduled task
The service cannot stop and restart itself through the SCM. Install a daily scheduled task (SYSTEM, random delay) running `airview-feeder.exe update -service AirViewFeeder`: rename-aside swap, SCM restart, health check on `/api/health` and rollback. The tray's "Check for updates" can run `update -check -json` (exit 10 = available) and start the task. The installer writes `update.manifestUrl` and `update.keys` like the Pi installer.
## WP-WF-winfeeder -> deploy/build.ps1 (WP-HL / Release): package the Windows feeder
`deploy/feeder/windows/build.ps1 -OutDir <dir> -Version <v>` builds `airview-feeder.exe` + `airview-feeder-tray.exe` (`-H windowsgui`) for amd64/arm64, the icon, `AirView-Feeder-<v>-windows-<a>.zip` and, when Inno Setup 6.3+ is installed, `AirView-Feeder-Setup-<v>-windows-<a>.exe`. Please call it from the release build (it refuses the repository `dist` folder unless the Release stage passes it). Inno Setup is not installed on the build machine yet, so the installer has not been compiled.

## WP-WF-winfeeder -> cmd/airview-feeder (WP-FB / integrator): feeder icon and version resource
The feeder executables carry no icon/version resource (go-winres would be a download). If the release machine generates `cmd/airview-feeder/rsrc_windows_{amd64,arm64}.syso` like cmd/airview, use `go run ./deploy/feeder/windows/mkicon -o <ico>` as the icon source.

## WP-WF-winfeeder -> cmd/airview-feeder update + internal/selfupdate (WP-FB / WP-UP): Windows update contract
The installer creates the SYSTEM task *AirView Feeder update* running `airview-feeder.exe update -service AirViewFeeder` daily; the tray's "Check for updates..." runs `update -service <name> -config <f> -data <d>` elevated and reads only the exit code (0 ok/up to date, 2/3 "not set up", 4 rolled back, else failed). Please accept `-config`/`-data` in the wired `update` command. **The updater must also refresh `airview-feeder-tray.exe`** (same program, PE subsystem GUI): after a successful swap copy the new `airview-feeder.exe` to `airview-feeder-tray.exe` with the optional-header Subsystem field set to 2 (IMAGE_SUBSYSTEM_WINDOWS_GUI; offset e_lfanew+0x5C), rename-aside if it is in use; otherwise the tray stays on the old version. The installer cannot write `update.manifestUrl`/`update.keys` until feeder.json has the `update` block; then it will take `/MANIFESTURL=` `/MANIFESTKEY=` like the Pi installer.

## WP-WF-winfeeder -> internal/feederui (owner): first-run setup in the browser
The tray opens `<status page>#settings` once when no aggregator is configured (Windows browser-based setup). Please make sure `#settings` opens the settings `<details>` (Chrome/Edge expand it on fragment navigation; Firefox does not), and show C1/C2 next to the timestamps and pin controls (H-platform request).

## WP-L2-locate2 -> internal/engine (WP-E / integrator): record guess samples and feed Locate Me v2 (standalone)
At start-up, after the tracker and the store exist: `rec := analytics.NewGuessRecorder(st, analytics.GuessRecorderOptions{})` (defaults 7 days, 2 M rows per station, 10 M total, prune every 10 min), `tr.SetGuessSampleHook(rec.TrackerHook())`, `defer rec.Close()` on shutdown (after the tracker stops). Then `analytics.SetLocateSources(analytics.LocateSources{Terrain: <the terrain.Provider (cached tiles only)>, GNSS: func() (float64, float64, bool) { g := e.ReceiverGPS(); if g == nil { return 0, 0, false }; return g.Lat, g.Lon, true }, Timing: col.Tracks})` with `col := &analytics.L2TimingCollector{}` fed by `col.Observe(&obs)` wherever the tracker hands a `latency.Obs` to the latency analyser (T=1 positions of a GPS-stamping receiver; cheap, bounded). Nothing else changes: `GuessLocation` (`/api/guess`, `/api/guess/run`) now merges the v2 estimate automatically (`Guess.Locate2`, `Fused.R95Km`, an extra `locate2` estimate); `/api/guess/run` already calls `Invalidate`, which also re-runs v2. `guess_samples` migration is registered for main and stations DBs (`internal/store/guess_samples.go`).

## WP-L2-locate2 -> aggregator host (WP-AGG / integrator): Locate Me v2 for stations without a location
On stations.db: `rec := analytics.NewGuessRecorder(stationsDB, analytics.GuessRecorderOptions{Want: func(idx model.StationIdx) bool { return <station idx has no confirmed/surveyed location> }})` and the same tracker hook (samples carry `Station`). For a station's owner/admin view call `stationAnalyzer.LocateMe(ctx, analytics.LocateRequest{Ref: <survey position if any>, RefSource: "survey"})` â€” it reads only that station's samples (prefix `s<idx>`), uses the process terrain, never the host GNSS/timing. Owner/admin-only (Â§8.2), never public. When a station gets located, `stationsDB.DeleteGuessSamples(ctx, idx)` frees its rows. Suggested use before the survey converges: show the v2 estimate and its 95 % circle to the owner as "approximate location", and seed the survey's search centre with it (`LocateRequest.PriorLat/PriorLon` works the other way round as well).

## WP-L2-locate2 -> internal/config (WP-CF): file-configurable Locate Me v2 bounds (Â§20.3)
Please add `locate.enabled` (true), `locate.retentionDays` (7, 1..30), `locate.maxRowsPerStation` (2000000, 10000..20000000), `locate.maxRowsTotal` (10000000, 100000..200000000) with `AIRVIEW_LOCATE_*` env overrides; the engine maps them to `GuessRecorderOptions{Retention, PerStation, Total}` (enabled=false: do not install the hook).

## WP-L2-locate2 -> web/static/js/pages/guess.js (WP-W): accuracy circle and the v2 row
`/api/guess` may now contain an estimate `method: "locate2"` (label "Locate Me v2 (horizon, skyline, signal)") and, when it succeeded with a smaller Ïƒ than the classic fusion (the normal case after an hour of traffic), `fused` is that estimate with `r95Km` (95 % accuracy circle radius) and `guess.locate2` (full result: `terms[]`, `levels[]`, `errorKm` against the receiver GNSS / configured position with `refSource`, `notes[]`, `surface` method "locate2"). The page already lists unknown methods and draws `uncertaintyKm` (1 sigma) circles; please add a METHODS entry for `locate2` (colour, short "v2", how/improve text: horizon and skyline of low traffic, signal level vs distance within each pass, optional T=1 timing) and draw `fused.r95Km` as the solid "accuracy circle" (keep the 1 sigma one dashed), and show `locate2.errorKm` with `refSource` ("vs receiver GPS") in the result card. The classic estimates have `weight: 0` when v2 is fused (shown for comparison).

## WP-L2-locate2 -> internal/analytics/analytics.go + WIRING.md (owner WP-D): mention locate2*.go
Please add `locate2*.go  Locate Me v2 (guess_samples, grid posterior, recorder, T=1 timing collector)` to the package doc list and a pointer to `locate2_doc.go` in WIRING.md (I may only edit locate2*/guess* files).

## WP-SU-survey -> internal/engine (WP-E / integrator): build and feed the reverse-MLAT survey
`internal/mlat/survey` exists (phase 3; details in its WIRING.md). In `newMLAT` (internal/engine/mlatwire.go): `surv, _ := survey.New(survey.Options{Config: survey.ConfigFromModel(cfg.Aggregator.MLAT), Clock: eng.Filter(), Refs: eng.Refs(), Atmosphere: eng.Atmosphere, Sink: eng, Declared: <registry adapter>, Notifier: <owner notices>, Targets: <planner adapter, see the plan request>, Terrain: <cached-tiles terrain.Source or nil>, Now: ntp.Now, Logger: ...})`; put it **in front of** the timing quarantine: `nc.mlatTap = newMLATTap(survey.Tee{Next: nc.trust, S: surv})`; wire `mlat.Options.Survey` to it (a forwarding type: the engine is built first) so the residual-bias flag starts surveys; `go surv.Run(ctx)` with the other MLAT goroutines. In `syncMLATStations` also push `surv.SetStation(idx, survey.Station{Class, Timestamps: <owner allows timing>, GNSS, Confirmed: LocStatus==confirmed, Declared: &survey.Declared{Lat, Lon, Alt, AltRef: DeclaredAltRef}, AGLM, Hint: <Locate Me v2>})` and `surv.RemoveStation`. Adapters: `SetDeclaredSuspect(idx, true, d)` â†’ registry `LocStatus = suspect` + `reg.SetEstimated(survey position)` (mlatStationInfo already maps that to `StationInfo.Suspect/DiscrepancyM`), `false` â†’ back to `declared`; `SurveyFinished(r)` â†’ `reg.AddEstimate(Estimate{Method: "survey", ...})` (field mapping in WIRING.md Â§3), an owner notice (result; "your declared position is N m from the survey"; MSL/HAE verdict `r.AltRef.Chosen`), and `surv.Restore(idx, last)` at start-up from the persisted estimates. Persist the owner's `OverrideSurvey` with the station (still carried from H-track).

## WP-SU-survey -> internal/server (WP-H): survey routes (owner/admin only)
`POST /api/me/stations/{sid}/survey` â†’ `surv.Start(idx, survey.ReasonOwner)` (`ErrNoConsent` â†’ 409 with consent text C1, `ErrRateLimited` â†’ 429, `ErrBusy` â†’ 202 queued); `POST|DELETE /api/admin/stations/{sid}/survey` â†’ `Start(idx, survey.ReasonAdmin)` / `Cancel(idx)`; `GET .../survey` â†’ `surv.Status(idx)` (running job, last results with position, Ïƒ_h/Ïƒ_v, 95 % ellipse, stable/suspect + reasons, autoUse, declared distance/verdict, MSL/HAE verdict, next check); `GET /api/admin/mlat/survey` â†’ `surv.Stats()`. Exact positions: never on public routes, never in logs (DESIGN Â§8.2). The location tab's "Use this location" then confirms the survey estimate (the H-mlatc confirm route).

## WP-SU-survey -> internal/mlat/plan (owner of plan, H-track): request reference aircraft from survey targets
DESIGN Â§10.2 needs the target's frames of ADS-B reference aircraft; selective AVF feeders and mlat-clients send only what the planner asks for. Please add `Planner.SetSurveyTarget(st model.StationIdx, on bool)`: while on, the station's syncSet is every usable reference aircraft (NACp â‰¥ 8, NIC â‰¥ 7) it hears or reports as seen, bounded by the usual station cap, ranked by distance spread (low and high elevations, all azimuths). `survey.Options.Targets` (`SurveyTarget(st, active)`) is the caller; a method with that signature on `*mlat.Engine` forwarding to the planner would let the wiring pass `eng` directly.

## WP-SU-survey -> internal/mlat (root, H-track / WP-M): one wrong station must not suspend the sky
Found by the survey's simnet gates: with one station **declared 300 m off** (synced as declared before its survey flags it), the TDOA verification (`worker.go` `e.verify.Record` â†’ `refs.Suspend`) suspends many reference aircraft near it for 10 minutes: after a 12-minute warm-up, `ref.FlagSuspended` was the main reason why the survey's reference queries failed (`Stats.NoRef` on 85 % of the matched groups in `TestGateDeclared300m` with two stations 300 m off, 52 % in `TestSimnetSmoke` with one), and the same targets collected about 4Ã— fewer observations than when they were surveyed from the start, before the wrong stations had been synced. Please (a) not let a station whose position is only declared (not GNSS/confirmed/surveyed) or flagged `Suspect` take part in ADS-B verification, and (b) attribute contradictions per station: when one station is in most of the contradicting clusters, flag the station (survey request via `SurveyRequester`) instead of suspending the aircraft. The survey copes (it uses the remaining aircraft) but needs longer.

## WP-SU-survey -> internal/model + internal/config (WP-CF / integrator): `aggregator.mlat.survey` block (Â§20.3)
`autoUseSurvey`, `autoUseMaxSigmaM` and `surveyIntervalH` are already read. Please add an optional `aggregator.mlat.survey` block mapped onto `survey.Config` (zero = default; `AIRVIEW_AGGREGATOR_MLAT_SURVEY_*` env; `config check` ranges as in `Config.Normalize`): `maxJobs` (64, 1..4096), `maxCheckFrac` (0.1, 0.001..1), `minDurationSec` (180, 30..21600), `evalEverySec` (60), `maxDurationMin` (30, 1..1440), `noDataAfterMin` (10), `retryAfterMin` (30), `ownerMinIntervalMin` (60, 0 = none), `maxObs` (15000, 500..200000), `minIntervalMs` (1000), `minPeers` (2, 1..16), `maxPeerSigmaNs` (600), `maskDeg` (2.5, 0..15), `heightSigmaM` (20), `defaultAglM` (10), `searchRadiusKm` (50), `stableSigmaGpsM` (30), `stableSigmaM` (200), `minAzSpreadDeg` (180), `minAircraft` (10), `suspectMinM` (100), `suspectK` (3). A `survey.ConfigFromModel` update maps them (WP-SU or the integrator).

Build/vet/test: cross-builds for windows/amd64, windows/arm64, linux/amd64, linux/arm64 and linux/arm (GOARM=7) are clean; `gofmt -l` is empty; `go vet ./...` is clean for windows/amd64, windows/arm64, linux/arm64 and linux/arm. Two 32-bit breaks fixed: `internal/server/sse_delta_test.go` and `sse_view_test.go` (`int64(1_700_000_000_000 + t*1000)` overflowed `int` under `go vet` for GOARCH=arm).

- WP-UP -> internal/model + internal/config (`update` block): **DONE**. `model.UpdateSettings` (enabled, channel, manifestUrl, keys, checkIntervalMin, healthWindowS, maxDownloadMB, healthUrl; defaults false / stable / "" / [] / 1440 / 60 / 256 / "") in `Config.Update` and `FeederConfig.Update` (internal/model/phase3.go), range checks in `Normalize`, `Clone` deep-copies the keys. `selfupdate.SettingsFromModel` and `FeederSettings(autoUpdate, block)` convert (test `TestSettingsFromModel` keeps the two structs in step). Env: the generic layers (`AIRVIEW_UPDATE_*`; feeder `AIRVIEW_FEEDER_UPDATE_*`, keys by index `..._KEYS_0`, or `update.keys=<key>` with `-set` / `config set`). `config check` appends `Settings.Check()` (airview: errors at `update.*`; feeder: warnings, plus "autoUpdate is on but no URL/key").
- WP-UP -> cmd/airview-feeder (wire `update` and Attach): **DONE** (cmd/airview-feeder/update.go). `update` is `selfupdate.RunCommand` and accepts `-config` / `-data`; service `AirViewFeeder` (Windows) or `airview-feeder` (Linux); health `http://<ui.addr>/api/health`, 200 or 503, header `X-AirView-Feeder: 1`. `startFeeder` calls `Attach` before any listener (rolled back: start fails, exit 1); `Run` confirms a trial with a self check (the status page answers) and runs the loop; an exit request ends `run` (the service and tray bodies exit after 30 s so their supervisor restarts the program file). Windows leaves automatic installs to the scheduled task; `AIRVIEW_FEEDER_UPDATER=external` (Pi unit) skips the in-process part.
- WP-UP -> cmd/airview (`airview update` and Attach): **DONE** (cmd/airview/update.go). `airview update [-data DIR] [-config FILE] [update flags]`. A headless `serve` with `update.enabled` (opt-in) attaches before the instance lock (so a new Windows process waits for the old one) with `SelfRestarter` (POSIX: shuts the app down, then execve; Windows: ends the run loop). The desktop window never updates itself.

## Integrator -> internal/mlat/survey (WP-SU): live collection, NoRef and FewPeers
Live (`airview-netsim -seed 7`: 12 AVF, 3 Beast and 4 mlat-client feeders, 250 aircraft, 150 km radius). With the first attribution rule (any declared station whose removal resolved a contradiction was charged) correct stations were charged, five of them were sent to "bias" surveys within a minute, and after about 8 minutes the survey tap had accepted only 3 observations (`Matched` 178k groups, `FewPeers` 99k, `NoRef` 73k, `Masked` 9k). With the unique-culprit rule (restart, same data): 1-2 contradictions instead of ~180, no station charged, and 335 observations accepted in the first 2.5 minutes; but `FewPeers` (47k) and `NoRef` (39k) still reject most matched groups, and two of the four unlocated stations had collected nothing after 5 minutes. Please check (a) whether seven concurrent targets, which are not peers of one another, starve the peer count in a 21-station network (`MaxCheckFrac` only bounds the periodic checks), and (b) why near reference aircraft fail `TransmitInstant` / `Usable` (NoRef) right after a restart. The elevation mask is right (checked: a 10 km aircraft 290 km away is below 1 degree, nearer ones are not).

Please add a link "Software updates" to it on the status page (only shown to loopback clients, or with a note that it works on the feeder's own computer only), and optionally a one-line update state from `GET /api/update` (`available`, `last.phase`). Nothing else is needed from feederui.

## WP-MLATC-udp -> internal/model + internal/config (integrator): UDP transport settings (Â§20.3)
`aggregator.mlat.udp` already reaches `mlatc.Options.UDP` through `mlatc.OptionsFromConfig` (no engine change needed: `Server.Serve` opens UDP on the TCP listener's address and port). For NAT / Docker / CGNAT deployments please add to the `aggregator.mlatc` block (zero = default, env `AIRVIEW_AGGREGATOR_MLATC_*`, config check): `udpHost` (string, "" = the local address of the client's TCP connection; the host told to clients in `udp_transport`), `udpPort` (int 0..65535, 0 = the bound UDP port; the externally reachable port), `udpListen` (string, "" = same address/port as the TCP listener; maps to `Options.UDPAddr`), `udpAnySource` (bool, false; accept datagrams from any address), `udpReaders` (int 0..64, 0 = min(4, GOMAXPROCS)), `modeAcAllClients` (bool, false; send Mode A/C requests also to clients without `"modeac": true`). Then map them in `OptionsFromConfig` (one line each; I can do the mapping once the fields exist). Docs: OPERATOR-GUIDE port table already says "UDP if aggregator.mlat.udp"; mention `udpHost` for port-mapped containers.

## WP-MLATC-udp -> internal/engine (WP-E / integrator): UDP restart and Mode A/C requests
1. `syncMLATC` restarts the listener only when the address changes; toggling `aggregator.mlat.udp` (or the new udp* keys) needs a restart of the mlat-client listener too (compare `opt.UDP`/`UDPHost`/`UDPPort`/`UDPAddr` with the running ones). `NetworkStatus().MLATC` / the `listener.mlatc` health check can show `srv.UDPStatus()` (active, bind error) and `Stats().UDPPackets/UDPLost/UDPBad/UDPUnknownKey`.
2. Mode A/C (DESIGN Â§9.9, phase 5): when `aggregator.mlat.modeAc` is on, after each replan call `srv.SetModeACAll(st, planner.ModeACEligible(st))` for every attached mlatc station (it returns whether the client receives the request). The replies already reach the ingest as `model.FrameModeAC` frames with station ticks; the engine's Mode A/C seam (`ObserveModeAC`) needs them routed there (WP-M).
3. Feed Check / admin link view: `LinkStats.UDP`, `UDPPackets`, `UDPLost`, `UDPDup`, `UDPBad`, `ModeAC`, `ModeACFrames` are new (optional fields for `LiveLink`).

## WP-MLATC-udp -> docs/aggregator/MLATC.md + DESIGN.md Â§5.2 (integrator / design owner): UDP and Mode A/C sections
MLATC.md Â§2.2 row `udp_transport`: "`false`, or `[host, port, key]` when `aggregator.mlat.udp` is on and the client offered `2`". Replace Â§9 "Not implemented" with:
- **Â§9 UDP transport.** After `[host, port, key]` the client sends sync, ssync and mlat data as datagrams (everything else stays on TCP). Datagram: header u32 key, u16 sequence number, u64 base timestamp (big-endian, 14 bytes); messages until the end: 1 sync (i32 even delta, i32 odd delta, 14 + 14 bytes; 37), 2 mlat short (i32 delta, 7 bytes; 12), 3 mlat long (i32 delta, 14 bytes; 19), 4 ssync (i32 delta, 14 bytes; 19), 5 rebase (u64 new base; 9), 6 absolute sync (u64 et, u64 ot, 14 + 14 bytes; 45). Timestamp = base + delta = the JSON value. Flushed at about 1,400 bytes. A Mode A/C reply travels as type 3 padded with 12 zero bytes (to confirm). AirView: random key live after the handshake; source address must equal the TCP client's (option); duplicates within 64 sequence numbers dropped; loss estimated from gaps; never answers a datagram; idle = no data on either path for 150 s. Proven equivalent to the JSON path by `TestUDPInterop`.
- **Â§10 Mode A/C requests (AirView extension).** `{"start_sending_modeac":["0a12",...]}` / `{"stop_sending_modeac":[...]}`, entries = the 2-byte reply value as 4 hex digits (as in `m`), `"*"` = every reply; sent only to clients whose handshake has `"modeac": true` (or with `ModeACAllClients`). Replies are `mlat` messages with a 4-digit `m` (TCP) or the padded long frame (UDP).
DESIGN Â§5.2: "`udp_transport:false` (TCP only until phase 5)" -> "`udp_transport`: `[host, port, key]` when `aggregator.mlat.udp` and the client offers 2 (MLATC.md Â§9), else false". CLEANROOM.md has the provenance entry (WP-MLATC-udp).

## WP-UPLINK-multi -> internal/model + internal/config (integrator / WP-CF): `uplinks[].type` for Beast outputs ("also feed others")
`internal/uplink` now has Beast outputs (`uplink.BeastUplink`, `Manager.ApplyBeast`, `Manager.BeastStatus`; contract in `internal/uplink/WIRING.md` Â§6).

**Fields.** Please extend `model.Uplink`, keeping existing files valid:
- `Type string json:"type,omitempty"`: `"avf"` (default when empty) or `"beast"`;
- `Format string json:"format,omitempty"`: Beast only; `beast_reduce_plus_out` (default), `beast_reduce_out` or `beast_out`;
- `UUID string json:"uuid,omitempty"`: Beast only; "" = generated per uplink;
- `ReduceMs int json:"reduceMs,omitempty"`: Beast only; 250 by default, range 50..5000.

**Normalize, for a Beast entry:**
- the port defaults to 30004 (`uplink.DefaultBeastPort`) instead of 30160;
- `serverPin`, `enrolToken`, `spoolMb`, `shareGnss`, `returnMlat`, `sendLocation`, `pin`, `publicStats`, `showName` and `federate` do not apply. Ignore them, with a config-check note if they are set;
- `timestamps` maps to `!NoTimestamps`;
- you can reuse `uplink.BeastUplink.Normalize` / `Validate` for the notes. They return `path`-prefixed strings.

**Other config work:**
- IDs stay unique across both types (`NormalizeUplinks` already does this).
- Env: `AIRVIEW_UPLINKS_<n>_TYPE` etc., following the existing list scheme.
- `-set 'uplinks[1].type=beast'` and `airview-feeder config set` need no change beyond the new fields.
- `secrets.go`: `uuid` is an identity at a third party. Treat it like other identifiers: shown to the admin/owner, never in public DTOs. It is not a password, so no redaction is needed.

## WP-UPLINK-multi -> internal/engine, cmd/airview, cmd/airview-feeder (WP-E / WP-FB): apply Beast entries
Wherever `uplinkManager.Apply(cfg.Uplinks)` is called (start, config apply, feeder run), split the list once the `type` field exists:

```go
var avfUps []model.Uplink
var beast []uplink.BeastUplink
for _, u := range cfg.Uplinks {
    if u.Type == "beast" {
        beast = append(beast, uplink.BeastUplink{ID: u.ID, Host: u.Host, Port: u.Port, Format: u.Format, UUID: u.UUID,
            Inputs: u.Inputs, ReduceMs: u.ReduceMs, NoTimestamps: !u.Timestamps, Disabled: u.Disabled})
    } else {
        avfUps = append(avfUps, u)
    }
}
m.Apply(avfUps)
notes := m.ApplyBeast(beast) // show the notes in the config check / log
```

- `Manager.Active()` already counts Beast outputs, so the tee includes the manager when only Beast outputs exist.
- `uplinks[].inputs` validation against `Config.Feeds[].ID` applies to both types.
- The `GET /api/uplinks` (and feeder `/api/status/uplinks`) body gains `"beast":[BeastStatus]`. `POST {"action":"retry","id":<beast id>}` works for Beast IDs too.

## WP-UPLINK-multi -> internal/engine (WP-E): consume the selective-mode MINUTE_STATS merge (WP-I2)
`avfsrv.Hooks.OnStationMinute(m avfsrv.StationMinute)` is new (`internal/ingest/avfsrv/WIRING.md` "WP-I2"). Set it in aggregator mode and feed the per-station statistics with it:
- Upsert by `(m.Station, m.Minute)`. A second call for the same minute carries the cumulative sum.
- For counters built from the received frames (lane/rfstat message rate, position rate, DF mix), add `m.Shed()` for that minute. Use `m.Exact.ICAOs` / `CRCFail` / `RSSI` where the station page shows unique aircraft, CRC failures or the signal histogram.
- `m.Selective` can be shown as "selective mode" on the owner's connection page.
- Late records (`m.Late`) belong to history only, the same as late frames.
- Setting the hook enables per-station minute counting in the server, about 3.6 ns per frame.
- `OnMinuteStats` (the raw record) is unchanged.
- PROGRESS.md: WP-I2 "selective-mode MINUTE_STATS merge" is implemented on the ingest side (avfsrv + uplink). Only this engine consumer remains open.

Please add an optional block mapped onto `ssr.Config` (zero = default; `cfg.Normalize()` then `cfg.Validate()` in the config check). Keys and defaults are listed in internal/mlat/ssr/WIRING.md Â§3: `enabled` false, `sites` [{name, lat, lon, heightM, sigmaHM, sigmaUM}], `matchTolNs` 400, `minMatches` 4, `timingSigmaACNs` 120, `latencySigmaS` 0.3, `maxObs` 60000, `maxAircraft` 600, and so on. ## WP-SSR-research -> internal/engine (standalone/feeder receive path): feed the experimental estimator when `ssr.enabled`
Only when enabled: `e := ssr.New(cfg)`. For every decoded airborne position call `e.AddPosition(icao, ssr.Position{T: receiver-timestamp seconds, P: ECEF, SigmaM: from NACp, Squawk: latest Mode A or -1, AltBaroFt: barometric altitude or NaN})`. For every Mode A/C frame call `e.AddReply(ssr.Reply{T, Class: ssr.ClassModeAC, Code})`. For every DF11 reply with a valid address call `e.AddReply(ssr.Reply{T, Class: ssr.ClassModeS, ICAO, IID})`. Call `e.Process(now)` once a second. Use the receiver's own sample-clock time base (12 MHz ticks / 12e6, unwrapped) for both positions and replies. Owner/admin-only status from `e.Stats()`; `e.Solve(hint)` on demand, about 0.3 s, off the hot path. Never auto-apply the result; it may only seed the survey search centre (WIRING.md Â§2).

## WP-SSR-research -> internal/feed (or whoever owns model.FrameModeAC decoding): Mode A/C payload -> identity code
The estimator needs the 12-bit Mode A/C identity as its octal value (A4A2A1 B4B2B1 C4C2C1 D4D2D1, squawk 7700 = 0o7700). Please expose a helper that converts the Beast type '1' 2-byte payload to that value, using the pulse order of the published Beast description. It must also report the SPI/X bits separately, or the frame as invalid. The estimator matches Mode C replies itself with `ssr.ModeCCode(altFt)` (Gillham, Annex 10).

## WP-SSR-research -> internal/server (owner/admin pages): experimental SSR panel (optional, later)
If wired: an owner/admin-only panel "Experimental: SSR self-location" showing `Stats()` (pair matches/min), `Solve()` results (position, sigma_h, learned interrogators with rotation periods), and a "use as survey search centre" button. Never public, never automatic (DESIGN Â§8, Â§19.3).

## WP-PIIMG -> cmd/airview-feeder: keep owner and group of feeder.json in saveConfig
`airview-feeder config set` (and every other saveConfig caller) writes a temporary file and renames it over feeder.json, keeping the mode but not the owner and group. Run as root on a Pi, the installer's root:airview-feeder 0640 becomes root:root 0640 and the service (user airview-feeder) can no longer read its configuration. Please chown the temporary file to the old file's uid/gid (Unix only) before the rename. The Pi image's setup page restores the owner itself (cmd/airview-piimage/pisetup/owner_linux.go), so nothing breaks today; the fix protects admins who run config set with sudo.

## WP-PIIMG -> internal/feederui: accept the Pi's own .local name on the LAN status page
The feeder Pi image advertises http://airview-feeder.local:8099/ (mDNS via avahi). The setup page accepts that name, but after the hand-over the feeder status page refuses every host name except IP literals and localhost (421), so users must switch to the IP address. Please also accept the machine's own host name and host name + ".local" (from os.Hostname at request time; still refuse every other name, which keeps the DNS-rebinding protection). The image's documentation will then drop the "use the IP address" note.

## WP-PIIMG -> deploy/build.ps1 (Release stage): build the Raspberry Pi images
Please run `deploy\feeder\pi-image\build.ps1 -Base <Raspberry Pi OS Lite arm64 .img.xz> -Sha256 <published hash> -OutDir <release dir> -Version $Version` (or call `airview-piimage build` directly with the already built linux/arm64 airview and airview-feeder binaries plus `cmd/airview-piimage/pisetup`). It writes AirView-Station-Pi-<ver>.img(.zip), AirView-Feeder-Pi-<ver>.img(.zip), .sha256 files, <name>.verify.txt and SHA256SUMS. The base image must be downloaded first (about 525 MB, downloads.raspberrypi.com, verify its .sha256); the release stage should decide whether it ships the 850 MB zips or only the .img.zip. Details: deploy/feeder/pi-image/README.md.

Two links are missing outside my packages: (1) every received `model.FrameModeAC` frame with a timestamp (ingest, AVF, mlat-client sessions) must reach `eng.ObserveModeACFrames(frames)` (any goroutine, cheap; it ignores other kinds, untimed frames and frames already marked MLAT) â€” the ingest grouper drops them today because they carry no address; (2) pass `ModeACOut: plan.ModeACFanout{plan.MLATCModeAC{C: mlatcSrv}}` in `mlat.Options` so that the planner asks exactly its Mode A/C stations' mlat-clients for every A/C reply (replaces the per-replan `SetModeACAll` loop of the WP-MLATC-udp hook). If any feeder path delivers the 2-byte payload as 4 octal nibbles instead of the reply pulse order, set `Options.ModeACCodeFormat = mlat.ModeACNibbles` for it (or convert with `track.ACRawFromNibbles`).

## WP-Q2-quality -> internal/mlat (engine root): wire the phase-4 quality features
Contract and validated numbers: `internal/mlat/quality/WIRING.md`. All four are additive; nothing existing changes behaviour until wired.
1. **CRLB map**: `Engine.CRLBMap` may keep calling `quality.CRLBMap` (same signature; station choice per target is now the planner's greedy D-optimal design, which matches the solver's real errors on simnet: median r/Ïƒ_map 0.62-0.93 per band, was 0.39-0.49 outside the network with nearest-12). Please build a `quality.NewCRLBIndex(sts, CRLBParams{Workers: 4, Atmosphere: &atm})` on station changes and cache it: `Map()` for the admin map (1,000 stations: 0.37 s serial / 0.06 s with 8 workers), `SigmaAt(lat, lon, hae)` for point queries, `quality.SummarizeCRLB(cells)` for the per-height summary (could replace the server-side area-per-grade summary).
2. **k_atm**: replace `katm.AddFix` in `worker.go` (every 4th anchored fix, DOF >= 3) by `katm.AddSolved(now, &quality.KAtmFix{Target: f.ECEF, Stations: used site positions, ResidM: f.Resid (m) of used rows, SigmaM: row Ïƒ (m), AltSigmaM: altitude row Ïƒ (0 if none), AltResidM: f.AltResid, ModelK: current atm.K}, atm)` (5.3 Âµs per 8-row fix, 0 allocs). The projected estimator recovers simnet's k_eff within 0.005 where the demeaned one is off by up to 0.08. `ApplyKAtm` should apply only when `katm.Report().Ready`; expose `katm.Report()` (`quality.KAtmReport`) in `Stats.KAtm` / the admin console.
3. **ADS-B verification**: in `worker.go` (two `VerifyTDOA(..., VerifyParams{})` calls) switch to `quality.Verify(..., quality.DefaultVerifyParams())` and `quality.NewVerifierPhase4()`; when the message also has an accepted MLAT fix with `quality.MLATComparable(&f)`, record `quality.CompareMLAT(rep, quality.RefCovECEF(rep, vel, nacp, 0.15, 30), f.ECEF, f.CovECEF, quality.DefaultCompareParams()).Verdict` too. A result with `VerdictNone` and `Culprit >= 0` names a bad station row (feed it to station quality, not the aircraft). Simnet: honest per-message contradictions 1.31 % -> 0.22 %, no honest aircraft suspect, >= 99.5 % of 1 km spoofers and 12/12 ground spoofers caught.
4. **Airframe-latency calibration**: `cal := quality.NewLatencyCal(LatencyCalParams{Salt: holdoutSalt ^ 0x5a})`; for every accepted comparable MLAT fix of an aircraft with a usable reference at the fix instant build a `quality.LatencySample` (along-track MLAT âˆ’ reference, its Ïƒ, ground speed, applied latency, NACp); hold-out aircraft (`hold.In`) -> `cal.Score`, others -> `cal.Learn`; push `cal.ForReference(icao, in)` to `refStore.SetLatency` when it changes; `cal.Decide(now)` once a minute and expose the `ABReport` (admin). This supersedes `LatencyFeedback`. Simnet: enabled after 10 min, network references 37.0 -> 11.4 m RMS along-track vs truth; stays off when there is no latency to learn. Optional config (Â§20): `aggregator.mlat.latencyCal` {enabled (default true: the A/B gate decides), minGain, zEnable} -> `LatencyCalParams`.

## WP-SCALE-clock -> internal/model + internal/config: partition tunables of the clock filter
`clock.Config` has three phase-5 partition fields that are not yet file-configurable (DESIGN Â§20.3): `PartitionStations` (int, default 100 = min(100, maxComponentStations); 4 at least; the largest partition of a partitioned time domain), `ExternalStates` (int, default 64; negative = off; cross-partition members per partition and epoch estimated as shared states) and `PartitionDriftNs` (float, ns/âˆšs, default 1; negative = off; common-mode random walk of the partitions that do not carry the gauge). Please add them to `model.AggMLATClock` as `partitionStations`, `externalStates`, `partitionDriftNs` (zero = default, same names and units as clock.Config, so `clock.ConfigFromModel` maps them by reflection and `TestConfigFromModel` covers them), with range checks in `Aggregator.Normalize` (partitionStations 0 or 4..maxComponentStations; externalStates -1..1024; partitionDriftNs -1 or 0..100), the schema/env (`AIRVIEW_AGGREGATOR_MLAT_CLOCK_*`), `config check` and docs/CONFIG.md. Measured effect: internal/mlat/clock/WIRING.md "Cost and scale".

## Integrator (after Wave-A: W3 + phase 4-5 libs): status of every open request

(2) `katm.AddSolved` replaces `AddFix` (used rows' positions, residuals and sigma in m, altitude row when used); `ApplyKAtm` applies only when `Report().Ready`; `Stats.KAtm.Report`. ## WP-BUILD -> README.md, docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, OPERATOR-GUIDE.md (integrator / WP-DOC): new release artifact names
`deploy/build.ps1` now produces versioned Pi bundles: `airview-pi-<v>.tar.gz` (top directory `airview-pi-<v>/`, adds `update.sh`, `VERSION`, `LICENSE.txt`, `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt`) and `airview-feeder-pi-<v>.tar.gz` (top directory `airview-feeder-pi-<v>/`); the unversioned `airview-pi.tar.gz` / `airview-feeder-pi.tar.gz` are no longer built. New: `airview-pi-install.sh` (one-line Pi install: `curl -fsSL <site>/airview-pi-install.sh | sudo sh -s -- --base-url <site>`, remembered in `/etc/airview/update.env` for `/opt/airview/update.sh`), `airview-feeder-install.sh` (copy of deploy/feeder/pi/install.sh), `airview-docker-<v>.tar.gz` (Docker build context from the prebuilt binaries, deploy/docker), signed update releases in `<OutDir>\release\{airview,feeder}\` with `-SignKey <path>`, Pi images with `-PiImages <base .img.xz>` (old `-PiBase` still accepted). Unchanged: server bundles `airview-server-linux-{amd64,arm64}.tar.gz` + `airview-server-install.sh`, binaries, installers, ZIPs, `SHA256SUMS` (now exactly this build's artifacts). Please update README.md lines ~97-102 and ~283-287 (`airview-pi.tar.gz` -> `airview-pi-<version>.tar.gz`, `cd airview-pi-<version>`), and mark the one-line VPS installer and the Docker image as available in OPERATOR-GUIDE.md section 0. Full list: header of deploy/build.ps1 and deploy/WIRING.md.

deploy/pi/install.sh is now versioned (prints install/upgrade/reinstall, records `/opt/airview/VERSION`, keeps `airview.prev`, rolls back a binary that does not stay active, installs `update.sh`/`uninstall.sh` into /opt/airview when they are next to it). The image payload works unchanged (no VERSION file: the version comes from `airview-linux-arm64 -version`). Contract kept: `install.sh`, `airview-linux-arm64`, `airview.service` next to each other; `sh install.sh` with no arguments.
