## Integrator (after Wave-B: WP-WIRE-45, WP-W45-web, WP-BUILD): status of every open request

Build/vet/test: a stray empty `api_share.go` in the repository root (0 bytes, a mis-targeted write during Wave-B) broke `go build ./...`, `go vet` and every test package; it was moved (not deleted) to the integrator's scratchpad. After that: cross-builds and `go vet ./...` clean for windows/amd64, windows/arm64, linux/amd64, linux/arm64, linux/arm (GOARM=7); `gofmt -l` empty. Flaky test fixed: `uplink.TestPingPongAndLiveness` (the server counts a session on accept, the client after its handshake; the test now waits for both).

- WP-WIRE-45 -> internal/mlat (latencyCal mapping): **DONE in part.** `aggregator.mlat.latencyCal` now builds `quality.NewLatencyCal` (`latencyCalParams`, Salt = HoldoutSalt ^ 0x5a, windowH -> WindowNs), every scored hold-out snapshot fix goes to `Score` (along-track sample from the reference state: speed, NACp, applied latency, MLAT âŠ• reference along-track Ïƒ), `Engine.observeLatency` routes non-hold-out fixes to `Learn`, `Decide` runs in the minute feedback, and `Stats.latencyCal` carries the `ABReport` (nil while off). Tests `TestLatencyCalParams`, `TestLatencySample`. **Carried (design decision):** the planner still solves ADS-B aircraft only for the hold-out, so `Learn` gets no sample and the report stays "insufficient"; `ForReference` is not applied in the reference store. Both stay with internal/mlat (planner budget for learning solves).
- WP-WIRE-45 -> internal/server (SSR routes): **DONE with a different path.** `GET /api/ssr` and `POST /api/ssr/solve` (202 / 409), AccessAdmin in every mode (standalone: local admin; aggregator: admin). Not `/api/admin/ssr`: the route lint reserves `/api/admin/*` for aggregator-only routes, and SSR is a station feature. `TestSSRRoutes`.
- WP-WIRE-45 -> internal/server (keep `ssr` on PUT /api/config): **DONE**, and the same bug fixed for `update` and `locate`, which a PUT without them also reset. New guard `TestPutConfigKeepsEverySection` fails when a new top-level block of model.Config is not kept.
- WP-WIRE-45 -> web/static (SSR panel): **DONE.** Settings > "SSR self-location" (settings/sections-ssr.js): the `ssr.enabled` switch, the advisory warning, replies/positions/aircraft, pair matches, last solve (position, Ïƒh, observations, interrogators with rotation periods), "Solve now". No apply button. Checked live in the browser (switched on, saved, 202 on Solve now).
- WP-W45-web -> cmd/airview (backup sharelinks.json): **DONE** (`backupFiles`; restore is manifest-driven, so it is restored too; `TestBackupRestoreRoundTrip` checks it).
- WP-W45-web -> model + config (`aggregator.shareLinks`): **DONE, one rename.** `disabled`, `maxPerOwner` (1..1000), `maxDays` (1..3650), `defaultDays` (1..3650, above maxDays -> maxDays with a note), and `exactLowDefault` instead of `coarseLowDefault` (a bool that defaults to true cannot be zero = default; `exactLowDefault: false` = new links round low aircraft, as before). Env `AIRVIEW_AGGREGATOR_SHARE_LINKS_*`, config check, CONFIG.md, example. Mapped in the server (`sharecfg.go`): limits and defaults in `GET/POST /api/me/shares` (`coarseLowDefault` in the list answer), `disabled` -> 404 on the owner and public share routes (the admin list and revoke stay). The create dialog takes its validity choices, default and rounding default from the server and says when share links are off. `TestShareLinksBlock`, `TestShareLinksConfig`.
- WP-W45-web -> internal/mlat (optional `Suspects` accessor): **DEFERRED** (optional, not needed for correctness; hold-out aircraft that are also suspect still show as hold-out).
- WP-BUILD -> README.md, docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, OPERATOR-GUIDE.md: **DONE** (versioned Pi bundle and `cd airview-pi-<version>`, `airview-pi-install.sh`, SD-card image, the full artifact list; OPERATOR-GUIDE Â§0 marks the one-line VPS installer, the Docker build context, the feeder listeners, the feeder/Pi/Windows installers and the MLAT engine as available; FEEDER-GUIDE no longer marks the feeder programs as planned).
- WP-BUILD -> cmd/airview-piimage (VERSION in the station payload): **DONE.** The station payload adds `update.sh`, `uninstall.sh` (when present in deploy/pi) and `VERSION` (from `-version`), all in SHA256SUMS; first boot copies every listed file next to install.sh, which then records the version and installs the tools. `TestPayloadCommand` extended.
- Carried unchanged: shed counts in the lane statistics, federation owner overlay / partner polling endpoint, `CompareMLAT`, hold-out samples when stations feed from the start, survey FewPeers/NoRef, per-area weather, mix100 vertical, RangeGrad, harsh20 tail, installer licence text (owner decision).
## Integrator (after Wave-B) -> internal/mlat (WP-M / quality owner): harsh20 regression after Wave-A
harsh20 (no gate) in MLAT-RESULTS Â§0d versus Â§0c: H P50 / P95 13.2 / 66.3 -> 14.9 / 81.6 m, > 1 km 2.99 -> 3.52 %, > 5Ïƒ + 50 m 1.94 -> 2.54 %. Every other scenario is identical, so the cause acts only under heavy outliers and multipath. Candidates from Wave-A: phase-4 verification and its culprit-to-station blame, and `katm.AddSolved`. Please bisect with `MLAT_RESULTS=harsh20` (about 55 s).

## fix:core -> internal/engine (surveywire.go restoreSurvey): MLAT-R2 / MLAT-R3 / MLAT-R5 restore side
The engine side is fixed in internal/mlat (stations.go: a registry update that moves the declared/GNSS/confirmed position by more than max(survey.suspectMinM, 3Ïƒ_h) drops the stored survey; an unstable non-suspect result never replaces a stable survey; a survey for an unknown/removed station is ignored). restoreSurvey still needs three changes in internal/engine:
1. MLAT-R5: restore `SigmaHM` as the ellipse semi-major, not `math.Hypot(SigmaE, SigmaN)` (âˆš(Î»1+Î»2), up to âˆš2 Ã— the live value, so an isotropic 40 m survey that is auto-used live (â‰¤ 50 m) restores as 56.6 m and flips the station to its declared position after every restart). Best: persist the semi-major (or CovENU) with the estimate and take the largest eigenvalue of the 2Ã—2 ENU block (`linalg.SymEigen2(CovENU[0], CovENU[1], CovENU[4])`); with only ÏƒE/ÏƒN stored, `max(SigmaE, SigmaN)` is the closest available (exact for an axis-aligned ellipse).
2. MLAT-R3: pick the newest estimate whose status is `stable` or `suspect` (not the newest of any status); an `unstable` one after a stable one must not hide the stable one.
3. MLAT-R2: skip a restored estimate that is farther than max(suspectMinM, 3Ïƒ_h) from the station's *current* declared position when the declared position changed after the estimate's time (the owner moved the receiver while the server was down). Without a stored "declared at" time: skip it when the station's LocStatus is not `suspect` and the distance exceeds the limit (a suspect declaration is exactly the case where the far survey is right).

## fix:core -> internal/engine + internal/server (admin): expose the new MLAT/trust counters
- `mlat.Stats.ADSBMuted` (SCALE-05): contradictions not charged to aircraft because the network-wide contradicted share was > 30 % over the last 1-2 min (mlat/verifybreaker.go). Please show it next to confirmed/contradicted in `/api/admin/mlat/verification`, and mark healthz/admin "degraded: TDOA verification muted" while it grows.
- `clock.Stats.PanicResets` (SCALE-01): Steps that panicked and were recovered by resetting every clock state (logged at ERROR with the stack by the engine). Please show it on the admin MLAT page and mark healthz degraded when it is > 0 in the last hour.
- `trust.Report.NoTrustedRef` (SCALE-06): the quarantined station has no trusted co-observer and is judged against nearby new stations (local bootstrap). Please show "no trusted reference in range" in the admin network / owner station view (the Reason string already says it while collecting).

## fix:core -> docs/aggregator/MLAT-RESULTS.md: 1,000-station gate (SCALE-05)
SCALE-01 (restructure panic / silent covariance corruption when a split domain's old component was predicted after its first part was rebuilt) is fixed in internal/mlat/clock (TestRestructureSplitOldComponentBehind reproduces the corruption with the old code); it is the prime suspect for the 1,000-feeder quality collapse. A 1,000-station one-world run is now possible (simnet.MaxAircraft = 20,000, SCALE-11: TestTenThousandAircraftOneWorld). Please add a 1,000-feeder / 10k-aircraft row to MLAT-RESULTS after the next integration run (P50/P95/P99, >1 km share, verification contradicted share, ADSBMuted).

## fix:core -> deploy / cmd (registry contention, SCALE-09)
Registry writes now take a two-priority lock (registry/writelock.go): `Admit` overtakes queued connection-log / last-seen / retention writes, and the AVF admission deadline is 30 s (avfsrv/conn.go). The beast/mlatc compat listeners pass their own ctx to `Registry.Admit` (internal/ingest owners: check their deadlines are â‰¥ 30 s too; avfsrv done). Batching conn_log open/close rows is not done (each needs its row ID); revisit if the 1,000-feeder re-run still shows admission timeouts.

## fix:deliverables -> Release stage / integrator: rebuild the release set last (DLV-01)
Every artifact in wp-build\out predates about 40 source changes. Re-run `deploy\build.ps1` (with `-SignKey <real key>`, `-PiImages <verified base>` and, if the release folder is hosted, `-UpdateBaseUrl https://...`) only after every fixer has finished, then repeat the deliverables audit on the new output. The build now refuses itself when any file under cmd/, internal/, web/, deploy/ or go.mod/go.sum changes while it runs ("source files changed while the build ran"), and writes BUILD-INFO.txt (source state time and release public key) next to SHA256SUMS.

## fix:deliverables -> owner (user) + docs/aggregator/OPERATOR-GUIDE.md, FEEDER-GUIDE.md: the real release key (DLV-04)
The manifests in wp-build\out were signed with a throwaway key from the session temp folder. Generating and keeping the real Ed25519 release key is an owner action (key custody): `go run ./deploy/release keygen -out <offline folder> -name airview-release`, back it up offline, and pass it to `deploy\build.ps1 -SignKey`. The build now refuses a key under %TEMP%/%TMP% or with test/throwaway/scratch/dummy in its name unless `-AllowTestKey` (test builds). Docs owners: once the key exists, publish its public half (`go run ./deploy/release pubkey -key <key>`) in OPERATOR-GUIDE (update.keys of stations) and FEEDER-GUIDE (feeder --manifest-key), and in the aggregator's install-script presets (PRESET_MANIFEST_KEY).

## fix:deliverables -> cmd/airview-feeder (update URL from the uplink, DLV-03 follow-up)
Release Windows feeder installers now write `update.keys` (and `update.manifestUrl` when the release build passes `-UpdateBaseUrl`) through the product's new `postInstall` step; Pi images pass `--manifest-key` (and `--url`) to install-feeder.sh via `/opt/airview-image/update.conf`. Remaining gap: a Windows feeder built without `-UpdateBaseUrl` has keys but no manifest URL. Please let the feeder derive `https://<first uplink host>/feeder/manifest.json` (what install.sh does for Pi feeders, and what the aggregator serves from `aggregator.feederReleaseDir`) when `update.keys` is set and `update.manifestUrl` is empty, and adjust the configcmd.go warning text ("the installers write both") accordingly.

## fix:surface -> internal/federation (policy.go / node.go): PRIV-A2 MLAT fixes skip the island guard and NoGround / NoMilitary
Own MLAT fixes go out through PublishMLAT -> flushMLAT, filtered only by `pol.fixAllowed` (mode, MinAltFt, boxes), at 1e-7 deg. STATE records go through Policy.apply (island drop/coarsen, NoGround, NoMilitary). Please (1) evaluate the island guard per fix: `Options.Guard` on the tracker aircraft of `f.ICAO` (the server's guard now also coarsens every low Mode A/C target `#xxxxxx`, PRIV-A6), or carry an island flag in Fix, and drop or coarsen the fix exactly as STATE (policy island=drop/coarse); (2) apply NoGround (fix flags or alt) and NoMilitary in fixAllowed; (3) test: an island-guarded low aircraft with send.mlat=true gives no exact fix on the wire. Note the server's island ground estimate no longer uses station antenna heights (PRIV-A5: terrain model, else privacy.IslandUnknownGroundFt); please use the same rule in the federation guard if it computes its own.

## fix:surface -> internal/registry (public.go): PRIV-A3 the rural guard counts hidden-pin stations
countsForGuard counts every located approved station whatever its pin, so pin precision (owner pin preview, /api/public/stations) is an oracle for hidden stations per 1-degree cell. Please count only stations whose own pin is public (Pin != hidden) toward RuralGuardMin, and never let hidden ones lift a pin to cell01. refreshPublic of neighbours then also stops flipping when a hidden station is approved/located/deleted.

## fix:surface -> internal/registry (public.go wantPublic): PRIV-A4 hysteresis for the public point
The engine no longer rewrites a survey-located effective position for refinements within 3x the larger 1-sigma (internal/engine/surveyloc.go surveyMoved, `surveyRefineSigmas`), which removes most hops. The public point still needs its own hysteresis: keep the stored cell unless the effective position is more than max(200 m, 3 sigma) inside another cell (or the move is above HoldBeyondM / confirmed by owner or admin). Test: a station 20 m from a 0.1 deg line with alternating +-30 m surveys keeps one public cell.

## fix:surface -> internal/registry (claims.go): UX-08 claim audit entries without actor name
Server side done: GET /api/admin/audit resolves an empty actor name from actorId (TestAuditActorName). Please still pass the account name in `Actor{ID: ownerID, Name: ...}` at claims.go:192 and :234 so other readers of the audit table (exports, retention reports) get it too.

## fix:surface -> internal/analytics (traffic.go): UX-02 "today" / "24 h" freeze for up to an hour
Traffic totals for LastHours(24) and Daily(today) leave out the open hour, so the Statistics tiles freeze and contradict the Totals card (and Antenna Lab vs RF Lab CRC share). Please include the open hour (minute table or live counters) in those totals and use one source for 'today', '24 h' and Totals.

## fix:surface -> internal/feederui (static/app.js, style.css, index.html): UX-04, UX-15, UX-16
- UX-04: readField() (app.js:383) turns an empty number input into 0, so clearing Latitude saves lat=0 ("Saved and applied."). Treat an empty required number as a validation error, or as "not declared" for lat/lon; keep 0 only for optional fields (port, beastPort, aglM).
- UX-15: `main { grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr) }` and `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on `.foot` and `#settings-file` (375 px viewport scrolls sideways with long paths).
- UX-16: the Software updates card link text repeats the title: use "Check for and install updates" and show the current version next to it.
- SEC-R1 follow-up: SaveSettings (cmd/airview-feeder/feeder.go) now keeps update.manifestUrl / keys / healthUrl from the file (channel and autoUpdate stay page settings) and returns a note when the request changed them; please show those fields read-only on the settings page ("set by the installer or an administrator").

## fix:surface -> deploy/installer + internal/wininstall: SEC-R1 trust anchors in HKLM, SEC-R5 install-folder DACL, UX-09/UX-10 wizard text
- SEC-R1: `airview-feeder update` on Windows now reads update.manifestUrl / keys from `HKLM\SOFTWARE\AirView Feeder\Update` (values ManifestUrl REG_SZ, Keys REG_MULTI_SZ; cmd/airview-feeder/trust_windows.go). When the key is missing, the first elevated run copies feeder.json's anchors there once (trust on first use); `airview-feeder update -retrust` (admin) re-copies. Please have the machine-scope feeder installer (the postInstall step that writes update.keys) write that HKLM key too, so no TOFU window remains, and remove it on uninstall.
- SEC-R5: machine-scope installs into a custom folder keep inherited ACLs (install.go:252 MkdirAll 0755). Apply a protected DACL to {app} (SYSTEM/Administrators full, Users read+execute, no inheritance) and refuse or re-own a pre-existing destination not owned by Administrators/TrustedInstaller.
- UX-09: firewall rule fixed to port 80 / 8099 and the wizard says "Use the /PORT= switch": make the rule program-based or let the wizard edit the port, with GUI wording.
- UX-10: feeder finish page: add "Next: open the status page (or tray > Settings) and enter your network's aggregator address", or open the status page with #settings on Finish when no aggregator is configured.

## fix:surface -> deploy/docker: SEC-R2 no admin password in the image
A standalone server without http.adminPasswordHash now grants admin only to loopback and private-network peers (RFC 1918, ULA, link-local) that were not proxied, unless `http.allowOpenAdmin` (AIRVIEW_HTTP_ALLOW_OPEN_ADMIN) is set (internal/server/auth.go openAdmin), and `airview serve` logs a warning. Docker port publishing can make every client appear from the bridge gateway (172.17.0.1, private) when the userland proxy is used, so the image must not rely on that: please generate an admin password on first start (write it to a file in the volume and print its path), or document `AIRVIEW_HTTP_ADMIN_PASSWORD_HASH` as required in deploy/docker/README and the compose example.

## fix:surface -> deploy/pi (install.sh / update.sh): SEC-R6 unsigned SHA256SUMS over possibly plain HTTP, run as root
Sign SHA256SUMS (or a manifest) with the release Ed25519 key, pin the public key in /etc/airview/update.env at first install, verify it before extracting (the airview binary can verify: internal/selfupdate has the Ed25519 manifest code), and refuse http:// unless the signature verifies.

## fix:surface -> cmd/airview-piimage/pisetup: SEC-R9 enrol token shown on the first-boot page
After a failed unattended configure, a.initial keeps v.Token and page.go:76 renders it to any LAN client. Clear a.initial.Token (and Pin if secret) before serving, show "a token was provided" with a keep option, and remove feeder-setup.txt once read.

## fix:surface -> cmd/airview-setup (main_windows.go openLog): SEC-R10 /ENROL=<token> in the setup log
openLog writes "Command line: " + os.Args unredacted to %TEMP%\<id>-setup.log. Pass os.Args through the same redaction as wininstall redactArgs (mask values of switches whose names contain TOKEN / ENROL / PASSWORD / SECRET).

## Integrator (after Final fixes: fix:core, fix:surface, fix:deliverables): status of every open request
Build/vet/test: cross-builds clean (windows/amd64, windows/arm64, linux/amd64, linux/arm64, linux/arm GOARM=7); `gofmt -l` empty; `go vet ./...` clean. `go test -count=1 ./...`: every package passes except two timing budgets that fail only while every package runs in parallel (`clock.TestEpochCostGate`, `tracker.TestNetworkBudgets`); both pass when run alone (57 s and 3 s).

- fix:core -> internal/engine restoreSurvey (MLAT-R2/R3/R5): **DONE.** `pickRestoreSurvey` takes the newest `stable` or `suspect` estimate (an `unstable` re-check no longer hides a stable one) and skips one farther than max(suspectMinM, 3Ïƒh) from the current declared position unless the declaration is suspect; `restoredSigmaH` = max(ÏƒE, ÏƒN) (ellipse semi-major for an axis-aligned ellipse, not hypot). Tests `TestRestoredSigmaHSemiMajor`, `TestPickRestoreSurvey`.
- fix:core -> engine + server (ADSBMuted, PanicResets, NoTrustedRef): **DONE.** `/api/admin/mlat/verification` counters gain `muted`, `clockPanicResets`, `advancePanics`; the admin MLAT verification card shows "Muted (network-wide breaker)" and "Recovered panics". The `mlat` health line is `degraded: TDOA verification muted` while ADSBMuted grew within 2 min, and `degraded: recovered panic ...` for an hour after a clock-filter or engine panic; `/healthz` answers 200 `"status":"degraded"` (new `HealthCheck.Degraded`). NoTrustedRef already reaches the owner/admin station view through the trust Reason text ("no trusted reference in range ...").
- fix:core -> MLAT-RESULTS (1,000-station gate, SCALE-05): **DEFERRED.** Not run in this wave (a 1,000-feeder / 10k-aircraft run takes hours); the gated rows were re-run (PROGRESS). Stays open for the next measurement run.
- fix:core -> deploy / cmd (SCALE-09 registry contention): **DONE** for the deadlines: the Beast compat (`beastin.registryTimeout`) and mlat-client admission deadlines are now 30 s like AVF. Batching conn_log rows: not done (as noted by fix:core).
- fix:deliverables -> Release stage (DLV-01 rebuild last): **OPEN for the Release stage** (the integrator does not write dist binaries). The build now also signs SHA256SUMS (SHA256SUMS.sig) when `-SignKey` is given (SEC-R6).
- fix:deliverables -> owner (DLV-04 real release key): **OPEN (owner action).** Key custody cannot be done by an agent.
- fix:deliverables -> cmd/airview-feeder (manifest URL from the uplink): **DONE.** `defaultManifestURL`: with `update.keys` set and no `update.manifestUrl`, the feeder uses `https://<first enabled AVF/1 uplink host>/feeder/manifest.json` (IPv6 bracketed); applied before the Windows HKLM seal, so the sealed copy holds it. configcmd warning updated. `TestDefaultManifestURL`.
- fix:surface -> internal/federation (PRIV-A2): **DONE.** Own MLAT fixes are looked up in the last prepared local picture: island-guard aircraft are dropped (island=drop) or coarsened to the 0.05Â° grid (Ïƒh raised, velocity and tracked flag removed), NoGround / NoMilitary apply, and a fix whose aircraft is not in the picture is held back when a guard or those filters are configured. Synthetic Mode A/C identities (> 24 bits) are never shared (PRIV-A6). `TestOwnFixPrivacy`.
- fix:surface -> internal/registry public.go (PRIV-A3): **DONE.** Only located, approved stations with a public pin count toward RuralGuardMin. TestPublicPointRuralGuard updated (it encoded the old behaviour) and now checks that hidden stations do not lift the guard.
- fix:surface -> registry wantPublic (PRIV-A4): **DONE.** A stored public point keeps its cell until the effective position is more than max(200 m, 3Ïƒ) (capped at 2 km) outside it. `TestPublicPointHysteresis`, `TestOutsideCellM`.
- fix:surface -> registry claims.go (UX-08): **DONE.** `registry.WithActorName(ctx, name)`; claim/redeem/unclaim audit entries record the account name (the server passes it). `TestOwnerActorName`.
- fix:surface -> internal/analytics (UX-02): **DONE.** The hourly rollup also writes the open hour; `hourStats` now re-aggregates the newest hour row from the minute rows whenever they exist. `TestHourStatsOpenHour`.
- fix:surface -> internal/feederui (UX-04, UX-15, UX-16, read-only update fields): **DONE.** Empty latitude/longitude/altitude is a validation error while "Declare position" is on ("Not saved: ..."), else the previous value is kept; `main` gets `minmax(0, 1fr)` and long paths wrap (375 px: scrollWidth 375); the update link reads "Check for and install updates" with the running version; Settings shows automatic updates, the release manifest and the key count read-only. Checked in the browser.
- fix:surface -> deploy/installer + internal/wininstall (SEC-R1, SEC-R5, UX-09, UX-10): **DONE.** SEC-R1: new `airview-feeder update -seal` (stores the anchors in HKLM, never installs) run by a new `machineOnly` postInstall step of the feeder product; `airview-feeder service remove` deletes the HKLM key on uninstall. SEC-R5: per-machine installs apply a protected DACL to the install folder (SYSTEM/Administrators full, Users read+execute) and re-own a folder owned by anyone but Administrators/SYSTEM/TrustedInstaller (`WindowsSystem.SecureInstallDir`, optional `dirSecurer` interface). UX-09: the firewall rule follows the program (no localport) unless `/PORT=` is given; the wizard wording no longer mentions a switch. UX-10: product `finishNote`; the feeder's finish page says it still needs the aggregator and where to enter it. Not tested on a real elevated install.
- fix:surface -> deploy/docker (SEC-R2): **DONE.** `AIRVIEW_GENERATE_ADMIN_PASSWORD=1` (set in both Dockerfiles): a standalone server without an admin password generates one at first start, stores the hash and writes the password to `<data>/admin-password.txt` (0600; removed at a later start once the password was changed). deploy/docker/README.md documents it. Checked live (hash stored, file written).
- fix:surface -> deploy/pi (SEC-R6): **DONE.** `install.sh --base-url` takes `--key <release key>` (or the pinned `RELEASE_KEY` in /etc/airview/update.env), fetches `SHA256SUMS.sig` and verifies it with OpenSSL â‰¥ 3 (Ed25519) before trusting any hash; plain `http://` without a key is refused unless `--allow-unsigned`; the key is pinned for update.sh. `release sign -raw` signs any file; build.ps1 signs SHA256SUMS. Verified by hand (valid 0 / tampered 1). Running as root is unchanged (the installer writes systemd units); the signature closes the trust gap.
- fix:surface -> cmd/airview-piimage/pisetup (SEC-R9): **DONE.** The token from feeder-setup.txt is kept in memory only (never rendered); the page says one is kept and an empty field uses it. `TestSetupPageHidesFileToken`.
- fix:surface -> cmd/airview-setup (SEC-R10): **DONE.** `wininstall.RedactCommandLine` masks `/ENROL=`, `*TOKEN*`, `*PASSWORD*`, `*SECRET*` values (and the value after a bare secret flag) in the setup log. `TestRedactCommandLine`.
- Findings with no fixer (done by the integrator): SCALE-02 (`process.memoryLimitMb`; aggregator default 85 % of a cgroup cap, else 80 % of physical RAM), SCALE-03 (lanes' slow flushes spread over the interval by station), SCALE-04 (measured growth table in OPERATOR-GUIDE Â§2.3), SCALE-07 (`http.sseGzipMax`, aggregator default: every stream), SCALE-08 (the factory `radarcape` feed is ignored in aggregator mode), SCALE-10 (`process.logLevel`, `logMaxMb`, `logFiles`; aggregator default 64 MB Ã— 10). SCALE-12 / UX-13 were already fixed (help lists update and federation).

## Release -> docs/aggregator/OPERATOR-GUIDE.md: test-only variable (DONE by Release)
The 1.0.0 release run of `go test ./...` failed `docs/aggregator TestEnvVarsExist`: OPERATOR-GUIDE Â§2.3 named the test switch `AIRVIEW_GROWTH`, which is not a setting variable. Reworded to "the opt-in store growth test `TestGrowth` in `internal/store`" (no meaning change). Owner of the guide: keep test switches out of the guides or add them to the known list in docs_test.go.

## Release -> deploy/build.ps1: -Resources trips the DLV-01 source check
`build.ps1 -Resources` resets `buildStart` after its own go-winres runs, but then passes `-Resources` on to `deploy\installer\build-installers.ps1`, which regenerates `cmd\airview-setup\res\{admin,user}\rsrc_windows_*.syso` later; the final "source tree unchanged" check then fails on those four files although the artifacts are consistent. Workaround used for 1.0.0: one run with `-Resources` (regenerates every .syso), then the release run without it. Fix: regenerate the setup resources in build.ps1's resources step (before `buildStart`) or exclude `*.syso` written by the installer step. Also: `go run github.com/tc-hib/go-winres@v0.3.3` with `GOPROXY=off` fails (deprecation lookup); offline it works with `GOPROXY=file:///<GOMODCACHE>/cache/download` and `GOSUMDB=off`.

## MANUAL-reference -> docs/aggregator/CLEANROOM.md Â§8: add github.com/google/uuid v1.6.0 (BSD-3-Clause)
`go list -deps ./cmd/airview` for GOOS=linux links github.com/google/uuid v1.6.0 (BSD-3-Clause); it is in the release THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt but missing from the CLEANROOM.md Â§8 module table. The manual chapter docs/manual/04-reference-6-licences.md already lists it.

## MANUAL-network -> web/static/js/pages/admin.js showToken: the enrol-token dialog suggests a command that does not exist
Found on a scratch aggregator (1.0.0, port 18782) while writing docs/manual/03-network-operator.md. After **Create token** the dialog shows "On the feeder, for example: `airview-feeder enrol --server <location.host> --token <secret>`". `airview-feeder` has no `enrol` command (`unknown command "enrol"`), and the host is the browser's `location.host` (127.0.0.1 behind a proxy test) instead of `aggregator.publicHost` and the AVF port; the pin is missing. Suggested line: `airview-feeder setup -server <publicHost>:<avfPort> -pin <avfPin> -enrol <token>` (data from GET /api/public/join plus the pin, see the next entry). The manual crops the line out of its screenshot and tells readers to use `setup`; update docs/manual/03-network-operator.md Â§3.2.4 once fixed.

## MANUAL-network -> internal/server (GET /api/public/join) + web/static/js/net/model.js feederSnippets: Feed this network's manual setup line has no `--pin`
`feederSnippets` builds `airview-feeder setup --server <host>:<avfPort> --enrol <token>`; /api/public/join carries no pin. With the default self-signed AVF/1 certificate the feeder then verifies against the system roots (internal/avf/tls.go: pin == "" -> roots) and cannot connect. The installer line works (install.sh has PRESET_PIN). Suggest: add `avfPin` to /api/public/join when no CA certificate is configured (`aggregator.tlsCert` empty) and append `--pin <avfPin>` to the snippet. Related: web/static/js/settings/sections-uplinks.js hint says "a self-signed aggregator shows its pin on its admin page", but no admin page shows it (only GET /api/admin/network `avfPin` and the start-up log). The manual currently documents the workaround (3.2.2, 3.3.4).

## MANUAL-network -> cmd/airview-feeder cli.go:252 and tray_windows.go:692: claim hint points at https://<host>/claim, which is 404
`airview-feeder claim` prints "Enter it on the aggregator's claim page (for example https://<host>/claim)". The aggregator answers 404 for `/claim`; the page is the SPA route `/#/me/claim` (or `/#/claim`). Either print `https://<host>/#/me/claim` or add a server redirect `/claim` -> `/#/me/claim` (internal/server). The host printed is the uplink host (e.g. 127.0.0.1 in tests), not the web host; fine for real installs. Manual troubleshooting row added in 03-network-feeder.md Â§3.3.13.

## MANUAL-network -> cmd/airview backup.go: `airview backup` ZIP omits federation.key (and mlat-history.gob.gz)
`backupFiles` = config.json, airview.jsonc, sharelinks.json; trees uplink, avf-tls. The federation node identity `<data>/federation.key` (internal/federation IdentityFileName) is not included, so a restore from the ZIP gives the network a new node ID and every peer must be reconfigured. The MLAT console history file is also left out (less important). The server kit's backup.sh tars the whole data directory and is not affected. Manual 3.2.13 tells operators to copy federation.key themselves until this is fixed.

## MANUAL-network -> docs/aggregator/OPERATOR-GUIDE.md: two stale statements
Â§8.10: "(`<data>/sharelinks.json`; until `airview backup` copies it, include it in your own backup ...)" - backup.go already copies sharelinks.json. Â§13.5 Upgrade calls `/usr/local/sbin/airview-backup`, which no installer creates; the kit installs `/usr/local/bin/airview` (`sudo airview backup` = /opt/airview/backup.sh) and `/opt/airview/upgrade.sh` does the backup itself. Also Â§13.1's sample /healthz output lacks the `"mlat":"ok"` check that 1.0.0 returns.

## Soak memory follow-up -> (done here) internal/mlat, internal/avf, internal/ingest/mlatc, internal/engine
Release soak finding (2.58 GB after 30 min, still +4 MB/min; one mlat-client down; healthz degraded during the MLAT start-up sync). DONE: late-copy rings per worker no longer scale with GOMAXPROCS (`mlat.lateSlotsPerWorker`), AVF/1 send buffers in one shared pool, uncompressed mlat-client bytes count 1/4 against the compressed byte limit (and refusals / rate-limit cut-offs are logged), start-up muting is not "degraded" for 10 min after the first verification. Details and the 45-minute curve: PROGRESS.md "Soak memory follow-up". Open: a live 1,000-feeder memory measurement (the OPERATOR-GUIDE formula is extrapolated).

## DBEFF-E-platform-profiles-docs -> internal/store/profile.go (A): fields E fills from storage.tuning.* and the tracker cadence
E resolves the profile in the engine and in `airview db profile` with `store.ResolveProfile(name, kind, dataDir)` and then applies the operator overrides (`storage.tuning.*`, 0 = keep). E needs these exported `store.Profile` members (names as below if still free, else tell E): `Name string` (resolved: standard / low-wear / server), `Reason string` (why auto picked it, e.g. "data directory on mmcblk0 (major 179)"), `FlushInterval time.Duration` (tuning.flushSec), `CheckpointInterval time.Duration` (tuning.checkpointSec), `WALSoftBytes int64` (tuning.walSoftMB), `WriterCacheBytes int64` (tuning.cacheMB), `ReadConns int` (tuning.readConns), `MmapBytes int64` (tuning.mmapMB; E passes 0 for "-1 = off"), and the tracker cadence `TrailFlush, IncFlush, HeatFlush, RangePartial time.Duration` (tuning.incFlushSec, heatFlushSec). Also `Options.BackupBeforeMigrate string` ("auto" | "always" | "never", from storage.backupBeforeMigrate). If ResolveProfile already applies 32-bit / RAM limits, E's overrides are applied after it (operator wins).

## DBEFF-E-platform-profiles-docs -> internal/store/datamig.go + premigrate.go (A): CLI entry points for `airview db migrate`
`cmd/airview/dbcmd.go` (E) implements `airview db migrate --status | --backup | --now`. It needs, besides `(*Store).MigrationStatus()`: (1) `store.ReadMigrationStatus(ctx, dbPath string) ([]MigrationState, error)` that reads `schema_data_migrations` through a read-only connection (safe while the server runs; "pending" for registered migrations without a row); (2) `(*Store).BackupBeforeMigration(ctx) (path string, err error)` writing the same `backup/airview-pre-1.1-<UTC date>.db` the automatic path writes (so the automatic path sees it and does not write a second one), usable while the server runs is not required (E calls it with the instance lock held); (3) `(*Store).MigrateNow(ctx, progress func([]MigrationState)) error` running every pending data migration to completion without the pauses (offline, instance lock held by the CLI), honouring the disk-space check and `BackupBeforeMigrate`. Until they exist E's command reports "not available in this build".

## DBEFF-A-store-core-migration -> internal/store/trail_store.go (B): define maxStoredFlightID now
`tryOpenHandles` (store.go) already calls `maxStoredFlightID(ctx, wdb)` with the writer `*sql.DB`; expected signature `func maxStoredFlightID(ctx context.Context, q querier) (int64, error)` = highest flight_id in legacy `positions`, `trail_tail` and `trail_chunk` (0 when empty; tables may not exist yet on an old file before its DDL ran - DDL migrations run before the call). The full tree does not compile until it exists (A builds in its own scratch copy with a stub meanwhile).

## DBEFF-A-store-core-migration -> internal/store (B, C, D): store-core contracts are in place
All in the tree (internal/store): `Profile` / `ResolveProfile(name, kind, dataDir)` / `Options.Profile` (zero = auto; explicit FlushInterval/ReadConns still win) and `(*Store).Profile()`; `registerDataMigration(DataMigration{Name, Version, Kinds, After, Estimate, Step, Finish})` (datamig.go; `Querier` interface; `(*Store).DataMigrationDone(name)` for readers that must still understand the legacy layout; progress, throttling, disk check, backup, optimize and the vacuum tail are done by the framework; a step must be idempotent per cursor and bounded by `budget` rows); `registerPruner(name, fn(ctx, s, cfg, now, *PruneResult))` - pruners run after RollupHours and BEFORE the legacy 1.0.0 pruning (they can still join on flights that pruneFlights deletes), use `res.AddExtra(name, n)` or the existing fields; `registerHourly(name, fn(ctx, s))` (HourlyMaintenance, name order); `registerPeriodic(name, every func(Profile) time.Duration, beforeTruncate bool, fn)` - run on the checkpointer goroutine (never the writer; use s.Write) every `every(profile)` and, with beforeTruncate, before every TRUNCATE checkpoint: B registers the trail seal here with `func(p Profile) time.Duration { return p.SealInterval }, true`; `RegisterLogicalTable(LogicalTable{Name, Kinds, Columns, TimeCol, Unit, Scan, Count, Active})` (logical.go): Scan(ctx, q Querier, span *Span /*[From,To) in TimeCol units, nil = all*/, limit, fn(row []any)) must yield the 1.0.0 columns, value types (int64/float64/string/[]byte/nil) and `SELECT *` row order; `Active` (optional) lets the physical 1.0.0 table be used while the new layout does not exist. `/api/db/export`, the export list and tools/dbparity use `ScanTable` / `ScanTableDB`. Legacy positions / met_obs pruning in maint.go no-ops when the table is missing or empty.

## DBEFF-A-store-core-migration -> internal/engine (E): profile, hourly job, migration CLI
1. Open both stores with `Options{Profile: p}` where `p := store.ResolveProfile(cfg.Storage.Profile, kind, dataDir)`; for the aggregator's airview.db with profile `auto` pass `"server"` (ResolveProfile returns "server" for KindStations by itself). Apply `storage.tuning.*` by overwriting the Profile fields (0 = keep) and `storage.backupBeforeMigrate` into `p.BackupBeforeMigrate` before Open. Leave `Options.FlushInterval`/`ReadConns` zero so the profile values apply. `Options.MigrationDelay` (default 15 s after Open, < 0 = no background run).
2. sched.go: call `st.HourlyMaintenance(ctx)` instead of `RollupHours` + `Checkpoint` (it does both, plus the hourly jobs of B/C/D, optimize and the before-truncate seal).
3. `airview db migrate --status` = `st.MigrationStatus()` / `st.MigrationInfo()`; `--backup` = `st.BackupNow(ctx)`; `--now` = `st.MigrateNow(ctx)`; `airview db profile` = `st.Profile()` (JSON marshals durations as ms).
4. Tracker cadences: `p.TrailFlush, p.IncFlush, p.HeatFlush, p.RangePartial` (0 on the server profile = tracker default); tile cache default `p.TileCacheMB`.
5. /api/db/stats gains `profile` (store.ProfileView) and `migration` (store.MigrationInfo); `stats` gains checkpoint counters (checkpoints, checkpointRestarts, checkpointTruncates, checkpointsIncomplete, lastCheckpoint, lastCheckpointMs, walPendingBytes, vacuumedPages) - for docs/API.md.

1. `Batch.MergeHeat`: replace `tc.stmt(sqlMergeHeat)` with `tc.stmt(heatMergeSQL(tc.h.kind))` (stats_read.go). Same eight arguments; on airview.db it returns `sqlMergeHeatTiled` (tile computed in SQL from the cell, `ON CONFLICT(ant, tile, cell)`), elsewhere `sqlMergeHeat`. If you sort the rows (3.7), sort heat by `(Ant, HeatTile(Cell), Cell)` on KindMain.
2. `Batch.AddRangeHours`: use `encHist, encSect := rangeHourEncoders(tc.h.kind)` (compact.go). The current "compact on both kinds" rounds `sector_max` to 0.1 km on airview.db, which `/api/stats/range?hist=1` prints (parity break, and Â§0.2 lossless). airview.db: compact histogram (lossless) + exact float32 sector maxima; stations.db unchanged.
Must land together with the kind.go entry below.

In `mainPrepared`, replace `sqlMergeHeat` (from `preparedSQL`) with `sqlMergeHeatTiled` (stats_read.go); `stationsPrepared` keeps `sqlMergeHeat`. The heat 2 migration (stats_migrate.go) switches itself on only when `mainPrepared` contains `sqlMergeHeatTiled` and not `sqlMergeHeat` (`heatTiled`), so land this together with B's MergeHeat change above (B first is safe; A first makes MergeHeat fail on airview.db).

`analytics.New(st, cfg, analytics.WithOptions(analytics.Options{Pending: tracker.PendingIncs}))` for the standalone Analyzer (also `analytics.WithPending(fn)`). ## DBEFF-A-store-core-migration -> internal/model/network.go + docs (E): aggregator.storage.optimizeHours default 24 -> 1
Design Â§4.1 runs PRAGMA optimize hourly on the server profile too. `store.DefaultTierPolicy()` and `TierPolicy.withDefaults` now use 1 h (optimize is bounded by `analysis_limit=400` on the writer connection, so an hourly run costs milliseconds). Please change the config default `OptimizeHours: 24` (model/network.go:678 and its field doc "default 24") to 1 so the configured default matches; values 1..720 stay valid.

## DBEFF-A-store-core-migration -> internal/store/read.go, trail_store.go (B): prepared hot reads (Â§3.16)
`(*Store).readStmt(ctx, query) (*sql.Stmt, error)` (store.go) prepares a query once on the read pool per database generation (closed by Reset/Close; do not close it yourself). Suggested users: the chunk lookup of `Trail`/`Positions`, `AircraftByICAO`, `FlightByID`, `KVGet`: `st, err := s.readStmt(ctx, q); row := st.QueryRowContext(ctx, args...)`.

## DBEFF-B-trails-flights-tracker -> internal/store/store_test.go (A): positions is a logical table now
Trail points are written to `trail_tail` and sealed into `trail_chunk` (trail_store.go); the 1.0.0 `positions` table only holds legacy rows until data migration `trail` 1 has moved them. Tests that read `positions` with SQL must read the logical table instead (helpers in trail_store_test.go): `TestQueueOverflowDropsLowValueRowsFirst` (`SELECT COUNT(*) FROM positions WHERE t = 2600 / t = 1` -> `trailTestCount(t, s, func(r TrailRow) bool { return r.T == 2600 })`), `TestPrune` (every `count(... FROM positions ...)` -> `trailTestCount` with the same filter; the expected `PruneResult.Positions` is unchanged: the registered pruner `trail` deletes the trails of the flights the flight retention removes, the trails past the trail retention and the orphans, and adds them to `res.Positions`), `TestConcurrentReadersWhileWriting` (inside the reader's tx: `np, _, err := countPositions(ctx, tx)` instead of `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM positions`; the tail rows are committed in the same transaction as the flights, so the snapshot stays consistent). `trailTestRows(t, s)` returns every logical row in (flight_id, t) order. `TestCompactBlobs` / `TestRangeHourMerge`: see C's entry (AddRangeHours now uses `rangeHourEncoders(kind)`).

## DBEFF-B-trails-flights-tracker -> internal/store (A): trail hooks are registered
trail_migrate.go registers: data migration `trail` 1 (`Estimate` / `Step` / `Finish`; Finish keeps the empty `positions` table and drops kv `trail.legacy_base`), the logical table `positions` (`Scan` = 1.0.0 columns and `(flight_id, t)` order, `Count` O(1), `Active` = `trail_chunk` exists), `registerPeriodic("trail-seal", p.SealInterval, beforeTruncate=true)` -> `(*Store).SealTrails`, `registerHourly("trail")` -> `CompactTrails` + once a day `VerifyCounts`, `registerPruner("trail")`. migration `flights` 2 is a DDL migration (flights_migrate.go). `maxStoredFlightID` is in trail_store.go. Helpers for maint.go if you want them: `deleteTrails(ctx, tx, ids...) (points, error)` (legacy + chunks + tail, counters exact) and `pruneTrailOrphans(ctx, tx, cutoff)`. Note: legacy rows deleted by maint.go itself (not through these helpers) make the O(1) positions counter drift until the daily `VerifyCounts`; since the `trail` pruner runs first, maint.go's legacy pass finds nothing in practice. The writer itself seals only when no seal ran for 3 x the seal interval (tools / tests without a checkpointer).

## DBEFF-B-trails-flights-tracker -> internal/engine (E): tracker cadence, seal interval, PendingIncs
1. `tracker.Options{TrailFlush, IncFlush, HeatFlush, RangePartial}` from `Profile` (0 = tracker default = profile `standard`: 60 s / 60 s / 300 s / 15 min). The tracker writes the minute rows every minute, checks dirty flight / aircraft rows every 15 s (saved once a minute as before), forces the old day's increments out at a UTC day roll and writes everything in `Flush` (call it on shutdown before `Store.Close`, as today).
2. `store.SetTrailSealInterval(p.SealInterval)` once after resolving the profile (process-wide; it only sets the writer's fallback seal, 3 x the interval; the regular seal is the checkpointer's periodic job).
3. `analytics ... Pending: eng.tracker.PendingIncs` (`func (*tracker.Tracker) PendingIncs() store.PendingIncs`), see C's entry.

## DBEFF-B-trails-flights-tracker -> internal/analytics (C): PendingIncs semantics
`store.PendingIncs` (pending.go): `Coverage/Signal/Elev` carry the UTC day of the accumulated increments (the tracker writes the old day out before it credits a position of a new day, so all pending day rows share one day), `Heat` the all-time grid increments, `Range` the partial range_hour delta rows of the open hour (built like the rows the tracker writes: histogram / positions / far / resid are deltas since the last partial write, MaxKm / SectorMax / Aircraft cumulative for the hour; merge with `mergeRangeHour` semantics). In aggregator mode (sharded tracker) the shards' rows are concatenated: a key can repeat, merge additively. Rows submitted to the store but not yet committed (group commit) are in neither the overlay nor the database for up to one store FlushInterval.

## DBEFF-B-trails-flights-tracker -> internal/store (D): registerTableCounter
`registerTableCounter(name, func(ctx, tx *sql.Tx) (n int64, ok bool, err error))` (counts.go) is what you already use; a name without a physical table is reported as a logical table. TableCounts runs every counter in one read transaction.

## DBEFF-D-other-package-tables -> internal/store/ops.go (B): route the legacy met writer to met_sample
`internal/met` now writes `Batch.AddMetSamples` (met_store.go) into `met_sample`; data migration `met` 2 (met_migrate.go) converts the 1.0.0 `met_obs` rows and its Finish drops `met_obs` and `met_obs_t` and re-creates an empty `met_obs` without index, only because `sqlInsertMetObs` is in `preparedSQL` (a missing table would make Open fail). Please (1) make `Batch.AddMetObs` / `Store.AddMetObs` call `b.AddMetSamples(rows)` (same `MetObs` type), and (2) drop `sqlInsertMetObs` from `preparedSQL`. Readers already union both layouts, so either order is safe. `MetObs` / `MetProfileInc` in types.go are unchanged. `sqlMergeMetProfile` is unchanged (the collector now merges once per closed hour instead of every 15 s).

## DBEFF-D-other-package-tables -> internal/store/read.go (B): weather_fc issue overlay in WeatherForecast
The weather poller writes forecasts with `Batch.SetWeatherForecastDiff` (ops_net.go): only rows whose values changed are rewritten; the issue time of the unchanged rows of the newest forecast lives in kv `store.weather_fc.issue` ("issued,from,to"). Please make `WeatherForecast` apply it, e.g. at the end of `queryWeather` for `table == "weather_fc"`: `if err := s.ApplyForecastIssue(ctx, out); err != nil { return nil, err }` (read_net.go; idempotent, one kv point read). Until then every caller in D's packages (api_weather.go, predict) uses `Store.ForecastRows`, which does exactly that, and the export / dbparity read the registered logical table `weather_fc` (read_net.go). Without the change a third-party caller of `WeatherForecast` would see the issue time of the last change of a row instead of the newest issue.

## DBEFF-D-other-package-tables -> internal/store/doc.go (A): schema reference for D's tables
Please replace the `met_obs(...)` entry: `met_sample(k INTEGER PRIMARY KEY = t << 25 | icao, lat_e5, lon_e5, alt_d = alt - 20000, w = packed wind, o = oat_x100 * 4 + src; VIRTUAL t, icao, alt, wdir_x10, wkt_x10, oat_x100, src)`, kept for Storage.TrailRetentionDays (pruner `met_sample`); `met_obs` is the logical 1.0.0 table (export). kv keys: `count.met_sample` (row counter), `count.guess_samples.<stn>` (per-station counters, seeded by DDL migration `guess_samples` 2), `store.weather_fc.issue` (see read.go request above). The full description is in the header comment of met_store.go.

## DBEFF-D-other-package-tables -> internal/store (A, B): counters registered by D
`registerTableCounter` (all O(1), kv reads): `met_obs` (logical: met_sample counter + legacy rows while met/2 runs), `met_sample`, `guess_samples` (sum of the per-station counters; both kinds). Info only, nothing to do. `(*Store).VerifyGuessCounts(ctx, fix)` recounts guess_samples (full scan) if B's daily `VerifyCounts` wants to include it.

## DBEFF-D-other-package-tables -> internal/engine/engine.go (E): registry WAL options
`registry.Options` gained `WALHardCapPages` (default 131072 = server hard cap), `JournalSizeLimit` (default 128 MiB), `WriterCacheKiB` / `ReaderCacheKiB` (8 / 4 MiB). The defaults are the server profile's; if `storage.tuning.*` overrides the stations.db profile, pass `p.WALHardCapPages` / `p.JournalSizeLimit` from the resolved stations profile so the registry's connection and the store's writer agree. No other wiring: the registry has no checkpoint loop, the store's checkpointer serves stations.db.

## DBEFF-D-other-package-tables -> internal/engine/engine.go (E): registry WAL options (amends the entry above)
Nothing to wire after all: the registry applies `WALHardCapPages` by itself before each write while `store.CheckpointServes(path)` reports an open Store on the same file (the store's checkpointer serves stations.db), and falls back to SQLite's 1,000-page checkpoint when it runs alone (tools, tests). Only pass `WALHardCapPages` / `JournalSizeLimit` if `storage.tuning.*` changes the stations.db profile's values.

## DBEFF-C-stats-heat-rollups-analytics -> docs/perf/DB-RESULTS.md (E): group C measurements
Measured on copies of the baseline databases (i9/NVMe, in-process, `go test ./internal/store -run TestStatsLayoutMeasure` with `AIRVIEW_DBEFF_C`, `./internal/analytics -run TestOverviewMeasure` with `AIRVIEW_DBEFF_C_OV`, `TestFlightsTwoYearsMeasure` with `AIRVIEW_DBEFF_C_2Y`):
- heat key: the design's `(ant, tile16, cell)` touched MORE leaves per flush (gen7: 60 s +64 %, 300 s +8 %) because the three antennas' copies of a cell land on three leaves. Implemented `(tile4, cell, ant)` (4x4-cell tiles): distinct heat leaves per flush gen7 60 s 90 -> 65 (-28 %), 300 s 290 -> 173 (-40 %); gen31 78 -> 61 (-23 %), 248 -> 158 (-36 %). With the heat flush intervals per hour (gen7): 1.0.0 15 s x 62 leaves = ~14,900 leaf writes/h -> standard 300 s x 173 = ~2,100/h (-86 %), low-wear 900 s x ~440 = ~1,800/h (-88 %). The -60 % per-flush target is not reachable by key order alone (the grid is only ~1,100 leaves; a 300 s flush touches 15 % of them). heat bytes 4.44 -> 4.82 MB (gen7, +tile column). Migration 0.17 s (gen7) / 0.34 s (gen31).
- /api/antenna/heat DB read: 18-28 ms in 1.0.0 (warm), 27-32 ms now (warm), ~60-80 ms first call after open in both; the cost is the per-row scan of ~36 k rows through database/sql, not page I/O (the ant-first key measured 21-24 ms). <= 15 ms not reached.
- range_hour: 1,406 -> 479 B/row (gen7), 1,393 -> 472 B/row (gen31) with lossless compact histogram + exact float32 sector maxima, WITHOUT ROWID. Migration 0.9 s / 5.0 s.
- coverage + signal_hist + elev_hist (sealed days, lossless): gen7 1.82 -> 0.97 MB (53 %, 5 of 7 days sealable), gen31 8.06 -> 3.05 MB (38 %). The <= 30 % target is not reachable losslessly: coverage max_km (an arbitrary float64, 8 bytes, incompressible; XOR prediction saves 3 %) is half of the sealed payload.
- daily distinct aircraft (Daily / Overview): gen7 33-35 -> 3-7 ms; gen31 105-126 -> 12-27 ms; synthetic 2-year table (3.36 M flights) 120-170 -> 5-16 ms; answers equal for offsets 0, +1 h, +5.5 h, -8 h. flights_hour ~4.7 MB/year.
- /api/stats/overview r1 (gen7): 124-148 -> 107-131 ms. Not <= 80 ms: `Top` (30 days, 5 rows) costs 70-80 ms on gen7 because every generated flight is a new airframe (29,111 airframes in 31,187 flights); its per-airframe attribute lookup (TestTopEqualsLegacy) helps real stations, where airframes repeat. COUNT(*) of flights is not a cost (2-6 ms at 3.4 M rows).

## DBEFF-A-store-core-migration -> docs/CONFIG.md, docs/perf/DB-RESULTS.md (E): measured profile changes
1. Checkpoint soft size is now 40 MiB (standard) / 48 MiB (low-wear) / 64 MiB (server) and journal_size_limit 64 MiB on station profiles (= the 64 MiB hard cap), not the design's 16 / 32 MiB and 32 MiB. Measured on rt-7d with only the checkpointer (group commit kept at 2 s): 16 MiB -> 30 checkpoints/h, ~150 MB/h database-file writes; 40 MiB -> 16.4 checkpoints/h at a WAL volume of 663 MB/h (= 11.6/h at the baseline's 470 MB/h), ~90 MB/h database-file writes (target <= 94), largest WAL file 44 MB (cap 64 MiB). With the 1.1 write volume the interval (5 / 10 min) triggers first.
2. mmap_size is 0 on Windows for every profile: Windows cannot shrink a file that another connection of the process maps, so a reader's mapping kept the incremental vacuum (and Prune's free-page release) from ever shrinking airview.db (found by TestPruneReleasesFreePages). `storage.tuning.mmapMB` still overrides it.

## DBEFF-B-trails-flights-tracker -> all groups: trail_tail holds one row per flight and trail flush
Since 03:00 UTC (15 Nov) `trail_tail` is `(flight_id, t0, t1, n, data BLOB)`: the points of one flight from one tracker trail flush, varint-coded like a chunk (one row per flight per minute instead of one per point: gen1trace store statement rows 13.2 M -> 4.9 M/day standard). Migration `trail` 1 was changed in place (not shipped). A scratch database opened by a build between ~02:20 and ~03:00 has the per-point tail and must be recreated from a 1.0.0 copy. `TableCounts["trail_tail"]` is the physical row count; the logical `positions` count is unchanged (points).

Done as requested: kind.go `mainPrepared` carries `sqlMergeHeatTiled` instead of `sqlMergeHeat` (stations.db unchanged), so `heatTiled` is true and migration `heat` 2 is live. With it, `TestHeatMigration` and `TestHeatMigrationMergesNewWrites` fail with "there is already another table or index with this name: heat_v1" (stats_migrate_test.go:52 / :120) - the tests' own 1.0.0 set-up probably collides with the DDL that now runs at open. A's tests (store_test.go / stations_test.go) accept compact or raw range_hour BLOBs on airview.db.

## DBEFF-E-platform-profiles-docs -> internal/store/stats_migrate.go (C): heat row count while migration `heat` 2 is pending
End-to-end check (1.0.0 demo database, 12,469 heat rows, new build started on it with `low-wear`): `/api/db/stats` `tables.heat` was **0** from the open until the `heat` 2 step ran (the DDL at open moves the rows to `heat_v1`; `TableCounts` counts the physical, empty `heat`), then 12,469 again. On a Pi the step can wait behind `trail` 1 for tens of minutes. Please register a counter (`registerTableCounter("heat", ...)`: `heat` + `heat_v1` while the latter exists) or give the logical table a `Count`, so the table count is the logical one throughout (design Â§0.1, tolerance `db-stats-shape` only allows the new objects). ## DBEFF-E-platform-profiles-docs -> internal/store/datamig.go (A): vacuum hint names a command that now exists

## DBEFF-D-other-package-tables -> docs/perf/DB-RESULTS.md (E): group D measurements
Measured on the i9 (scratch dbeff\D-other-package-tables). met: 2.4-hour real-time demo run (demo.aircraft=1300, 37,215 rows) met_sample 30.6 B/row incl. key (1.0.0 met_obs + met_obs_t 79.8-82.4 B/row); `TestMetWALPerHour` (250 aircraft, 14,040 obs/h) 8.34 -> 2.68 MB/h WAL; met_profile 7,500 UPSERTs/h -> one per (hour, level) per closed hour (120 rows in 2.4 h). Layout note: met_sample is a rowid table keyed k = t<<25|icao with the design's columns as VIRTUAL generated columns; the design's WITHOUT ROWID PK(t, icao) measured ~40 B/row (31 B record, leaves ~83 % full on appends) and would miss the <= 32 B target. Migration met/2 of a 1.0.0-written file: 2,977 rows in 21 ms, all rows equal at met precision (lat/lon 1e-5), met_obs_t dropped. guess_samples: per-station kv counters exact after 2.4 h (215,543 = GROUP BY); the 2.4-hour SQL trace has no `GROUP BY stn` except the one-time counter seed of DDL migration guess_samples/2; the pruner does one PK seek per station per 10 min. weather_fc: an unchanged forecast writes 0-4 of 168 rows (1.0.0: 168; `TestWeatherForecastDiffEqualsFullWrite`); with live Open-Meteo data (the model really changes) ~105 of ~167 rows per hourly poll. Parity (1.0.0 exe vs new exe on copies of the same 1.0.0 database, live feed off): /api/met/profile, /api/met/winds, /api/met/history, /api/guess (Locate Me), /api/weather/*, /api/forecast/weather and the weather_fc / weather_obs / met_profile / guess_samples / latency_minute exports byte-equal (timestamps normalised); met_obs export equal at met precision (row order within a second by ICAO). Registry (stations.db DSN): internal/registry scale test 5,000 stations, 4 alternating rounds vs the 1.0.0 registry.go, medians: admit +1.7 %, locate+pin +0.9 %, purge 1000 +0.7 %, BenchmarkAdmitReconnect +1.8 %, single reconnect +5 % (35.0 -> 36.8 us, noise level). Online navdata route/photo cache rows now ride the group commit (up to ~40 single-row commits/h before when online lookups are on).

## DBEFF-B-trails-flights-tracker -> all groups: flight / aircraft rows refreshed on one tick
The tracker refreshes the rows of active flights and airframes together once per 60 s (one tracker-wide tick, `Tracker.lastRefreshSec`) instead of each on its own 60-s phase; a row never written (new flight / airframe) is still written at the next 15-s row check, and `Flush` writes everything. Same row contents, same maximum lag; an aircraft page (~40 airframes, random ICAO order) is rewritten once per minute instead of up to four times.

## DBEFF-A-store-core-migration -> docs/perf/DB-RESULTS.md (E): integrated measurement (Â§1 targets), full tree of 15 Nov 06:24 UTC
Method: rt-7d (copy of gen7, `-demo`, demo.aircraft=1300, GOMAXPROCS=4, GOMEMLIMIT=600MiB) for 28 min, steady state minutes 14-27 (the data migration and its vacuum tail end by minute ~11), sampler (process I/O counters), walwatch (-shm), `/api/db/stats` every 15 s, CPU profile minutes 11-21; i9/NVMe with other scratch instances running in parallel (bytes and commits do not depend on the CPU; CPU numbers are slightly pessimistic). Database-file writes = process writes - WAL bytes (the walwatch back-fill is only an upper bound: it counts every WAL frame, not distinct pages). Scratch: dbeff\A\f6-std, dbeff\A\f6-low (runrt2.ps1, analyze.py).

| Metric | 1.0.0 | standard | target | low-wear (forced) | target |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| all bytes written by the process | 747 MB/h | 321 MB/h (-57 %) | <= 160 MISSED | 247 MB/h (-67 %) | <= 80 MISSED |
| WAL bytes | 469 MB/h | 250 MB/h | (~107 est.) | 195 MB/h | (~62 est.) |
| database-file writes | ~278 MB/h | ~70 MB/h | <= 40 MISSED | ~52 MB/h | <= 20 MISSED |
| commits (walwatch tx / store flushes) | 1,925 /h | 388 / 372 /h | <= 450 met | 125 / 124 /h | <= 150 met |
| checkpoints (fsync pairs) | 93 /h | 9.2 /h | <= 13 met | 4.6 /h | <= 7 met |
| checkpoint inside a commit (pprof traces) | always | 0 | 0 met | 0 | 0 met |
| largest WAL file | - | 46.6 MB | <= 64 MiB met | 57.3 MB | <= 64 MiB met |
| store writer CPU (pprof share of `Store.run`, incl. checkpoints) | 1.2 % | 0.69 % | <= 0.6 MISSED (slightly) | 0.46 % | <= 0.5 met |
| peak private bytes (no read sweep) | - | 184 MiB | - | 190 MiB | - |
| Stats.Dropped | - | 0 | 0 met | 0 | 0 met |

WAL by table (walpages over 26 WAL snapshots, minutes 14-26): standard aircraft 48.5 %, heat 19.0 %, guess_samples 7.2 %, flights 4.5 %, coverage 4.2 %, flights_icao 2.0 %, signal_hist 1.7 %, trail_tail 1.5 %; low-wear aircraft 62 %, flights 5.3 %, heat 5.0 %, trail_tail 4.9 %, guess_samples 3.5 %, trail_chunk 2.9 %. The `aircraft` table alone writes ~120 MB/h of WAL in both profiles (design estimate 20 MB/h): it is the reason the bytes targets are missed (sent to B below).

Sizes after migration + vacuum tail (standard, live demo traffic, same parallel host): gen7 553 MB -> 107.7 MB (<= 150 met); migration 85 s from Open (15 s start delay, 3.6 s backup, `trail` 1 61 s; <= 3 min met), peak file 556.1 MB (+0.5 %, <= +15 % met), WAL max 46 MB, vacuum tail done ~10 min after start. gen7busy 941.9 MB -> 179.7 MB (<= 240 met), migration 2.1 min, peak +0.2 %. gen31 2.41 GB -> 435 MB (<= 0.65 GB / <= 0.85 GB plateau met), migration 5.5 min from Open (`trail` 1: 59.4 M points in 4.1 min; <= 12 min met), peak +0 %, vacuum tail done 43 min after start; that run (build of 05:15) hit a 67.1 MB WAL during Prune's free-page release, fixed since (next entry). Stats.Dropped 0 in every run. The pre-migration backup (`auto`) writes a copy of the old file once (455 MB / 774 MB / 1,979 MB in 3.6 s / 5.9 s / 14.4 s).

Not measured here: rows per day as store statements (needs the scratch SQL-trace build; `flushedRows` is ~1.0 M/h standard, ~0.8 M/h low-wear in queue row units, not comparable), the 75-URL read sweep and the read latencies (B / C measured theirs).

## DBEFF-A-store-core-migration -> docs/CONFIG.md, docs/perf/DB-RESULTS.md (E): store changes after the first integrated measurement
1. Early flush: `Options.FlushRows` defaults to 2,000 rows per second of the group-commit interval (1.0.0's rate; clamped to 4,000..60,000: standard 20,000, low-wear 60,000, server 10,000; stations.db 20,000 unchanged). A busy low-wear station queued ~6,000 rows per 30 s and every flush was an early one at 4,000: commits 195 /h -> 124 /h.
2. Bulk work checkpoints between its transactions once the WAL holds the soft size (data-migration steps; Prune / Maintain incremental-vacuum steps of up to 16 MB each): the WAL stays under the 64 MiB hard cap (before: 64.4 MB during `trail` 1, 67.3 MB during Prune's free-page release after the migration).
3. Vacuum tail after the data migrations: 2,000 pages per checkpointer tick (10 s on stations) instead of one step per half checkpoint interval (that would have taken ~2 h for gen7 and added ~24 checkpoints/h meanwhile): gen7 is back to its size ~10 min after start, gen31 after ~40 min.
4. Exports: `/api/db/export` (list) and `/api/db/export/<table>.csv` first write what the station holds in memory (`Tracker.Flush`; the met collector's open hour once D adds `FlushOpenHour`), so an export contains everything collected so far, as in 1.0.0 (design Â§0.1; heat increments would otherwise be up to 5 / 15 min old).

## DBEFF-A-store-core-migration -> internal/tracker, internal/store/ops.go (B): aircraft rows write half of the WAL; srcMask lost; record announcements
1. Bytes targets missed because of `aircraft`: ~120 MB/h of WAL in both profiles (48.5 % standard, 62 % low-wear; design 20 MB/h), measured on the build with your single 60-s refresh tick. With gen7's 29 k airframes in random ICAO order every refresh rewrites ~250 leaf pages of `aircraft` (+ `aircraft_last_seen`), each a full 4 KB frame. Ideas: write only rows whose persisted columns changed beyond last_seen (if the readers merge the live tracker's last_seen), a longer refresh on low-wear (needs a design change: Â§4.3 promises <= 60 s), or keep the volatile columns (last_seen, counters) in a small table keyed by a dense id in first-seen order so that the active airframes share pages. Scratch: dbeff\A\f6-std\walsnaps, dbeff\A\f6-low\walsnaps (`measure\walsum.ps1`).
2. Parity (live phase, full tree 06:27): `/api/history/aircraft?limit=500` `srcMask` old 17 / 18, new 1 / 2 for 16 of 226 airframes, also in `aircraft.csv` (`...,41000,17,0` vs `...,41000,1,0`), even after the export's `Tracker.Flush`: bit 16 of the source mask (which 1.0.0 stores) is not persisted by the new aircraft write path.
3. Parity (live phase, two of five runs): the "New all-time record: longest tracked flight" event / alert / notice carries 197 s in the new build vs 164 / 170 s in 1.0.0, and `/api/alerts/recent` lists the record alerts in another order: records are announced up to ~30 s later than with 1.0.0's 15-s cadence. Please announce records on the 15-s row check again (a handful of rows).
4. Done for you: store_test.go reads `met_obs` through the logical table (`metObsRows` in `TestWeatherNTPMetReceiverStatus` and `TestPrune`), so switching `AddMetObs` to `AddMetSamples` no longer breaks A's tests.
5. Info: tools/dbparity tolerance `trail-retention` (README): the row check skips the logical positions rows of flights that ended before 1.0.0's own prune watermark; 1.0.0 leaks a point that arrived after the flight row's last update (flights.positions = 0) past the retention, your pruner deletes it.

`/api/db/export/met_profile.csv` and the export list miss the open hour (live phase: 32 of 124 lines, every run): the collector merges an hour when it closes. api_data.go (A) calls, before every export, `FlushOpenHour(ctx context.Context) error` on `Engine.Met()` when the collector implements it (interface `openHourFlusher`, type assertion). Please add it to `*met.Collector`: write the open hour's profile increments now (additive UPSERT) without changing what the met pages show afterwards (they must not lose the open hour from memory, or must read it back from the table). `/api/db/stats` `tables.met_profile` is not compared in the live phase (tolerance, README); the CSV must be equal.

## DBEFF-integrator -> all groups: every DBEFF request processed (15 Nov, full tree)
Status of each open DBEFF request after the integration pass (build, gofmt, vet, full `go test ./...` and the four cross-builds clean; `clock.TestEpochCostGate` / `tracker.TestNetworkBudgets` fail only under the parallel full run and pass alone):
- **C -> stats_migrate_test.go (A's report):** DONE. `TestHeatMigration` / `TestHeatMigrationMergesNewWrites` now build a real 1.0.0 heat table (`legacyHeat`: drop the tiled table, create the (cell, ant) layout, merge with the 1.0.0 UPSERT) instead of re-running the DDL that Open already applied.
- **E -> stats_migrate.go (heat count while heat 2 is pending):** DONE. `registerTableCounter("heat", countHeatLogical)`: while `heat_v1` exists the count is heat + legacy cells not yet merged; otherwise the plain COUNT(*). Both heat-migration tests assert the logical count at every step.
- **D -> ops.go (AddMetObs -> met_sample):** DONE. `Batch.AddMetObs` calls `AddMetSamples`; `sqlInsertMetObs` is no longer prepared. D's tests use a test-only 1.0.0 writer (`addLegacyMetObs`, met_store_test.go).
- **D -> read.go (forecast issue overlay):** DONE. `WeatherForecast` applies `ApplyForecastIssue`; `ForecastRows` is now an alias. A full `SetWeatherForecast` after diff writes first writes the window's issue into its rows and clears the kv window (`finaliseForecastIssue`), so it is never overridden by an older window. The diff test compares stored rows through `queryWeather`.
- **D -> doc.go:** DONE (met_sample entry was in; heat corrected to `(tile, cell, ant)` with 4 x 4 tiles, weather_fc issue window and the aircraft refresh documented).
- **A -> internal/met (FlushOpenHour):** DONE. `(*met.Collector).FlushOpenHour(ctx)` hands the open hour's profile increments and pending observations to the store now (additive UPSERTs; History / HourProfile unchanged, asserted in `TestProfileWrittenOncePerClosedHour`). dbparity live: met_profile.csv and the export list equal.
- **A / E -> tracker + ops.go (aircraft rows ~half of the WAL):** DONE, as an on-demand refresh. The rows of active airframes are refreshed every `Profile.AircraftRefresh` (standard 300 s, low-wear 900 s, server = tracker default 300 s; `storage.tuning.aircraftFlushSec` 60..3600) instead of every 60 s; a new airframe's first row still goes out at the next 15-s row check (new-airframe hook, per-day counts). Readers of aircraft rows call `Tracker.FlushAircraftRows(ctx)` first (server `freshAircraftRows`: /api/history/aircraft, /api/aircraft/{icao}, /api/history/flights/{id}, /api/export/aircraft.csv, /api/stats/overview lifetime totals; the table exports already flush everything), which refreshes rows older than 60 s and waits for the commit, so every answer has rows at most a minute old as in 1.0.0; at most one extra commit a minute. Removal saves wait for the next refresh too (`heldSeen`). A crash loses at most AircraftRefresh of aircraft counters (CONFIG.md power-cut table). Tests: `TestAircraftRowsOnDemand`, `TestRowRefreshAligned` (aircraft tick). `/api/db/stats` profile gains `aircraftRefreshMs` (docs/API.md regenerated).
- **A -> srcMask bit 16 / record announcements (B):** not reproduced: dbparity (tree of 07:41, fixture e91a36db1119) passes all three phases, live with 0 differing calls (history/aircraft and aircraft.csv equal, records and alerts equal, met_profile equal). The earlier srcMask differences were intermittent (0 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 12 rows in A's five runs); if they recur, compare the first row-check time of the affected airframes.
- **A -> model/network.go optimizeHours:** already 1 in the tree.
- **D -> engine (registry WAL options):** nothing to wire (D's amendment).
- **E -> datamig.go (vacuum hint):** info only.
- **Start-up migration on every database:** verified. Station: dbparity migrate/crash phases (data migrations start 15 s after Open, resume after kills). Aggregator: a 1.0.0 aggregator data directory (airview.db + stations.db) started with the new build: DDL migrations at Open on both files, airview.db data migrations done 15 s later; stations.db has only DDL migrations. `airview db migrate --status` now reports such a file as `none` ("no data conversion needed") instead of a permanent `pending` (`store.HasDataMigrations`). The feeder (`airview-feeder`) has no database; a station in feeder mode uses the station path.
- Also: docs/API.md generation no longer publishes the generating machine's temp paths and host name (docgen_test.go scrubs them).
- **Measured (rt-7d, minutes 14-27, docs/perf/DB-RESULTS.md §8):** process writes standard 321 -> 229 MB/h (1.0.0 747), low-wear 247 -> 120 MB/h; WAL 250 -> 159 / 195 -> 83 MB/h; commits 402 / 125 per hour, checkpoints 9.2 / 4.6 per hour, largest WAL 46.7 / 57.5 MB, Dropped 0. The 160 / 80 MB/h byte targets are still missed (heat / flights / statistics pages make up the rest).

Interim, client-only rule that is already correct today: `x.reg` set -> "aircraft database" when `/api/db/acdb/sources` reports one installed, otherwise "received"; only `info.reg` set (or `!info.acdb`) -> "derived from the ICAO address" (same wording as pages/aircraft.js:921). Type: `x.type` -> "aircraft database"; `info.typeInfo` -> name from "ICAO Doc 8643". Footnote with the installed database's `attribution` (`GET /api/db/acdb/sources` -> installed info; required by the tar1090-db ODC-By licence).
2. Exact version (server): `aircraftInfo` gains `regSource` / `typeSource` = "acdb" | "callsign" | "derived" | "history" | "feed" set in `buildInfo` where each value wins (row -> "history", db -> "acdb", live -> the tracker's source; `refdata.Lookup` -> "derived"), plus `acdbAttribution` when "acdb" won. detail.js prefers these when present.
3. Better silhouettes for aircraft whose type is not in the client regex table: in render(), call `shapeFor({ ...a, typeInfo: info?.typeInfo })` (detail.js:386); pages/map.js:400 and reports/replay.js:339 may pass `typeInfo` / `wtc` too if the live JSON ever carries them. `shapeFor` now uses the type designator first, then Doc 8643 class / wake category, then emitter category (map/aircraft-icons.js).
4. New exported helper `shapeForType(designator)` and new shape id `turboprop` (map legend, if any lists shapes, should add "Turboprop").

## r101-defaults-radarcape -> web/static/js/pages/timing.js: Radarcape status text / 1PPS tick
feed.BeastStatus.String changed (internal/feed/beast.go): keywords kept ("GPS locked", "antenna fault", "not valid", "acquiring", "no GPS satellites", "not reported"); NEW verdict "not synchronised" (bit 4 sync clear while antenna/sats/timestamp fine; the clock class is gps_emul, earlier the text wrongly said "GPS locked") -> please map to amber "DPLL not locked". The text now ends with " - check: <advice>" (BeastStatus.Advice) — show it as the tip when present. 1PPS error is now "1PPS error %+d ticks (%+.0f ns)" with 15 ns per tick (Jetvision: "1 tick is 15ns"); decodeStatus `ppsNs: pps * 15.625` -> `pps * 15`. Also the GPS status byte is printed ("status byte 0xBF").

## r101-defaults-radarcape -> docs/manual (timing chapter, 02-station-08-timing.md; 04-reference-1-configuration.md is generated from docs/CONFIG.md)
"time not valid" = the Radarcape sets bit 5 of the GPS status byte of its type-'4' status frame (mlat-client: timestamp_ok false, decoder RADARCAPE_EMULATED): its timestamps are emulated, not GPS-disciplined. AirView decodes this correctly; it is the receiver's own verdict. What to check: Radarcape web interface GPS page / systemstatus.json — satellites in use, "Sync" (DPLL locked) and "TimeUTC"; clears by itself minutes after power-up / a GPS outage; if it persists with good satellites, restart the Radarcape. Factory defaults: one omni antenna + Beast localhost:30005.

## r101-db-powercut -> docs/CONFIG.md ("What a power cut can lose", trail row) or internal/engine (trail cadence)
Power-cut check (TerminateProcess at random points, 1.1 build, demo traffic): a killed process lost up to ~64 s of trail points on `standard` and ~75 s on `low-wear` (runs killed 63-75 s after start had no trail row committed yet): the tracker hands the trail to the store once a minute and it is then committed with the next group commit (10 s / 30 s). The table says "<= 60 s + write-back" for both profiles. Either the row should read "<= 70 s" (standard) / "<= 90 s" (low-wear) "+ write-back", or the engine should request an immediate flush after the minute's trail append. Every other measured row (flights, aircraft, coverage/stats minute, small rows) stayed within its documented window; integrity_check was "ok" after every kill.

## r101-release -> internal/config/reference.go (+ regenerated docs/CONFIG.md): "Storage layout migration" says "AirView 1.0 ... converted to the 1.1 layout"; the release shipping it is 1.0.1. Suggest "A database written by AirView 1.0.0 is converted to the new storage layout of 1.0.1 (internally the 1.1 layout, hence the backup file name)". The manual copy (docs/manual/04-reference-1-configuration.md, "Storage profiles") already uses that wording.
## r101-release -> internal/store/met_store_test.go TestMetSamplePruner: flaky at a second boundary (full run: "pruned 3601, want 3600"; passes 4/4 in isolation). Row i=3600 has T == now-30d exactly (i*240 s = 10 days), and Prune takes its own time.Now() later, so if the clock ticks over a second that row is pruned too. Fix: start the rows at now-40*86400+120 so no row lies on the cutoff.
## r101-release -> internal/mlat/clock TestEpochCostGate: failed again in the full go test ./... run (200-station epoch 121.8 ms > 80 ms) while a release build ran in parallel; passes in isolation (57.8 s). A timing gate under load, not a regression signal; consider skipping it under -short or when GOMAXPROCS is shared.
